# Automation Roadmap

This roadmap covers improving and extending the automation capabilities of Windows App Manager MCP. It comes from an automated feature review on 2026-10-10: two independent multi-agent review runs. Each run had seven subsystem reviewers, an adversarial verifier per subsystem, an external landscape scan, a roadmap synthesizer and a critic. The two runs were then merged into this document. The review was static: no tests or binaries were run.

**Findings:** 120 distinct verified defects (2 critical, 19 high, 62 medium, 37 low). Both runs independently found 90 of them. M0 fixes or partly fixes 36 (34 when it shipped, plus D-PAL-15 and D-PAL-23, whose M0 fixes were recorded later), M1 fixes 25 and partly fixes 12, M2 fixes 29 and partly fixes 9 (25 of them were still open after M1), and M3 fixes 2 and partly fixes 1 (2 of them were still open after M2); 23 are still open. The full register, with a status column, is in the [appendix](#appendix-defect-register). File and line references are as of commit `309c821`.

Task status is tracked in [BACKLOG.md](../BACKLOG.md) (Phase 13). This document holds the design notes.

## Assessment

Both reviews reach the same diagnosis of WindowsAppManagerMcp. The architecture is sound: services behind interfaces, DI, mockable native wrappers, per-monitor DPI, structured Success/ErrorCode results, and UIA and CDP read paths. It is not yet safe or reliable for an agent to drive a Windows task from start to finish. Five problems stand out. (1) The model cannot see screenshots, because they arrive as base64 inside a JSON text block. (2) Keyboard and mouse input has no target and no verified activation, so it can land in the agent's own terminal; the test suite already did this with Alt+F4. (3) Many tools report success after doing the wrong thing or nothing at all: maximize on the wrong monitor, unknown states treated as restore, save_layout always true, CDP reads from the wrong tab, and a controlTypes filter that drops nested matches. (4) Headline features are broken: a stale plugin binary, an unserializable debug-launch result, and no published release. (5) Everything is addressed by raw HWND plus hand-computed physical pixels, with no waits and no element actions, so a simple form fill costs 4-10 round trips that cannot be verified. The plan works in layers. M0, shipped in 1.12.0, stops the bleeding. M1 makes seeing and typing safe by default. It starts with a default host-window input guard, ships image content together with the true capture origin, and adds verified activation, guarded input with receiving-window reporting, and a desktop-safe fixture e2e suite to validate the Windows-specific behaviour. M2 makes results honest and settles the MCP surface (contract tests, annotations, stable SDK, curated toolsets) before M3-M5 add about 30 tools. It also pulls scroll and clipboard forward. M3 is the biggest lever. It moves from blind coordinates to selector-addressed, pattern-first UIA actions with stable refs, popup-aware search, Chromium accessibility activation, text and table reading, virtualized items and server-side waits, each returning verified post-state. That cuts typical tasks to 1-2 calls that can each be checked. M4-M7 complete the input vocabulary, build a thin browser bridge with a Playwright handoff, add breadth, and hold the deferred items. Design assumptions were checked against the repo. ModelContextProtocol 0.5.0-preview.1 already exposes McpServerOptions.Filters.CallToolFilters and McpServer.ElicitAsync, so the M0 exception filter and M2 elicitation can be built on the current SDK. ProcessStartInfo.ArgumentList is honored with UseShellExecute=true on .NET 10; re-check this on the net8 target. The main residual risks are Windows-behaviour details the critics flagged: the foreground-lock unlock, popup hit-testing, InvokePattern blocking while its modal is open, Edge title formats, and stale element refs. That is why the fixture e2e suite sits in M1, ahead of the large feature work.

## Milestones

| Milestone | Goal | Items |
|-----------|------|-------|
| [M0: Stop the bleeding](#m0-stop-the-bleeding) | Every later fix reaches users and can be tested without touching the developer's desktop. The most dangerous confirmed defects are gone: unsafe tests, the stale binary, the IntPtr crash and opaque errors, the '(x86)' launch block, silent kill escalation, races and UIA crashes, lost layouts, CDP hangs and wrong-tab reads, and docs that teach Alt+F4. | 8 |
| [M1: See and type safely](#m1-see-and-type-safely) | The model sees real pixels it can click from accurately. Keyboard and mouse input cannot reach the agent's own host, a shell window, an elevated or deny-listed window, or an unintended target. Every input result reports where it actually went. The user can pause everything with a hotkey. A desktop-safe suite against a self-owned fixture app validates the real foreground, UIPI and capture behaviour. | 12 |
| [M2: Honest results and a stable MCP surface](#m2-honest-results-and-a-stable-mcp-surface) | Every tool either does what it reports or says precisely why not: typed parameters, real post-state, verified close, handoff-aware launch and faithful layouts. The MCP surface (contract tests, generated docs, annotations, stable SDK, curated toolsets, server instructions) is settled before M3-M5 add about 30 tools. Scroll and clipboard, the two primitives agents miss first, are pulled forward. | 17 |
| [M3: Semantic find-act-wait](#m3-semantic-find-act-wait) | The biggest lever. Move from blind coordinates to selector-addressed, pattern-first UI Automation, so 'click Save in app X' becomes 1-2 calls that can each be verified. Elements are found by name, automationId and type, including in menus, popups, dialogs, Chromium/Electron apps and virtualized lists. Agents act through UIA patterns with verified post-state, read text and tables directly, and wait server-side instead of sleeping. | 14 |
| [M4: Input vocabulary](#m4-input-vocabulary) | Keyboard and mouse produce exactly what was asked, on any keyboard layout and button configuration. The tools cover the chords, drags, multi-clicks, modifier-clicks and long text that real tasks need, and nothing can stay held down after a call returns. | 4 |
| [M5: Browser bridge](#m5-browser-bridge) | Reliable CDP for debug-enabled Chromium, bound to the right window and tab, with a few high-value tools: readiness, tabs, navigation, waits and paged content. The UIA fallback is locale-independent, and anything richer is handed to Playwright MCP explicitly. After M2 this milestone can run in parallel with M3/M4. | 8 |
| [M6: Platform, efficiency and breadth](#m6-platform-efficiency-and-breadth) | Fewer, leaner tools and fewer round trips through consolidation and run_steps. Coverage for surfaces UIA misses (OCR, visual change detection), packaged apps, richer process, monitor and z-order control, layout schema v2 and MCP resources and prompts. Most of these sit behind opt-in toolsets. | 9 |
| [M7: Later](#m7-later) | Valuable but deferred: a modern capture backend and visual aids, event-driven window tracking, virtual-desktop detection, opt-in takeover detection, best-effort workspace restore, and a scenario harness that measures round trips. | 6 |

## M0 Stop the bleeding

Every later fix reaches users and can be tested without touching the developer's desktop. The most dangerous confirmed defects are gone: unsafe tests, the stale binary, the IntPtr crash and opaque errors, the '(x86)' launch block, silent kill escalation, races and UIA crashes, lost layouts, CDP hangs and wrong-tab reads, and docs that teach Alt+F4.

M0 shipped in release 1.12.0 on 2026-10-10 (PR #1, release commit ef7d56f). It covers the 8 planned items plus integration fixes, a six-lens final review, and a hardening round driven by an external review. CI on main is green: 1509 tests, a check that desktop tests stay skipped on the runner, and a strict check of the committed plugin exe on the release tag. The repository's first GitHub release was created from that tag.

- M0.3 shipped differently from its first design: failures come back as structured isError results with an errorCode instead of a rethrown McpException, and every tool call is logged with its duration. The M0.2 and M0.3 designs below describe what shipped.
- The DesktopNoInput test tier planned in M0.1 is deferred to M1.11, because no capture-only desktop tests exist yet.
- marketplace.json metadata.version stays at 1.0.0 on purpose: it versions the marketplace listing, not the plugin. Set-Version.ps1 stamps plugin.json and the plugin entry.
- Beyond the plan, M0 also routes every process start and kill through NativeProcessWrapper, checked by IL guard tests that now work as an allow-list. It stops tracking .claude/settings.local.json, replaces the third-party release action with the GitHub CLI, pins the remaining third-party actions to commit SHAs, and adds a CI step that proves desktop tests stay skipped.
- The plugin exe in bin/ was rebuilt for 1.12.0 with scripts/Publish-Plugin.ps1 and reports 1.12.0+fc8d350.

### M0.1 Deny-by-default desktop test gating

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m0-test-isolation` (Shipped in 1.12.0)

Builds on the merged input-isolation fix (6c4648d). Any test that touches the real desktop is skipped unless the developer explicitly opts in, so no runner, filter or worktree can send input, launch apps or kill processes by accident. CI runs only non-desktop tests.

**Design.** Add [DesktopFact]/[DesktopTheory] (plus a DesktopNoInput variant for self-owned-window capture tests). They set Skip unless an opt-in env var such as WAM_DESKTOP_TESTS=1 is present. The deny is in code because runsettings and --filter are bypassed by IDE runners, explicit filters and agents running plain dotnet test in .worktrees. Keep [Trait('Category','Desktop')] only for CI selection. Inject IProcessLauncher (Start(ProcessStartInfo) -> IProcessHandle) into ProcessService so ProcessServiceTests:458/479/500 stop launching Chrome and Notepad. Replace the KillProcess-on-csrss/lsass test (:431) with a pure test of the ProtectedProcesses set. Optional: widen InputEvent now to (Kind, Vk, Scan, Flags, Dx, Dy, MouseData), so later scan-code, absolute-move and wheel work does not force a test rewrite. test.yml runs non-desktop tests with --blame-hang-timeout and a re-baselined coverage gate. Remove the unfiltered 'dotnet test' pre-approval from .claude/settings.local.json and the README. Deferred: the DesktopNoInput variant moves to M1.11, where the first self-owned-window capture tests need it.

*Sources: r1:R1, r2:R1, r1:crit:R1, r2:crit:R1, r1:INP-12, r1:MCP-5, r2:INP-1, r2:MSP-6, r2:PAL-G10, r2:MSP-G5*

### M0.2 Single-source version and working release pipeline

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m0-release-pipeline` (Shipped in 1.12.0)

Make the exe the plugin runs the one built from main. Make the version visible over MCP, make tag pushes produce GitHub releases, and remove stale duplicate binaries and wrong publish paths.

**Design.** As shipped: Directory.Build.props holds the single <Version>, and Program.cs reports it, with the commit SHA, as ServerInfo.Version. scripts/Set-Version.ps1 stamps Directory.Build.props, .claude-plugin/plugin.json and the plugin entry in marketplace.json. scripts/Publish-Plugin.ps1 publishes into bin/, the path plugin.json runs, and refuses uncommitted build inputs (and -AllowDirty into bin/). scripts/Test-PluginBinary.ps1 checks the exe's version and that no build input (src/, config/, root build files) changed since the commit it was built from. /commit and /update-plugin call these scripts. The stale dist/, publish-new/ and .claude-plugin/bin binaries are gone. build.yml checks the exe strictly on v* tags and whenever a push or pull request changes it. Its release job (contents: write) creates the GitHub release with the gh CLI, and every third-party action is pinned to a commit SHA. README states the real runtime requirement (.NET 8 Windows Desktop Runtime). The CI guard that starts the exe and diffs its tools/list against a schema snapshot came with M2.12.

*Sources: r1:R2, r2:R2, r1:crit:R2, r1:MCP-3, r1:MCP-G8, r1:MCP-12, r2:MSP-4, r2:MSP-5, r2:MSP-G6, r2:MSP-14, r2:MSP-15*

### M0.3 Tool contract: serializable DTOs, serialization test, exception-surfacing filter

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m0-tool-contract` (Shipped in 1.12.0)

Fix the launch_browser_with_debug crash, which comes from System.Text.Json refusing to serialize IntPtr. Add a test over every tool that would have caught it, and replace the SDK's opaque 'An error occurred invoking' with actionable messages.

**Design.** As shipped: DebugBrowserLaunchResultDto uses long? for the window handle, and a contract test serializes every tool's result type with all members populated using the SDK's McpJsonUtilities.DefaultOptions. A call-tool filter (Infrastructure/ToolErrorReporting.cs, registered through the shared ServiceRegistration used by Program.cs and the DI tests) returns a structured isError result instead of rethrowing: errorCode ToolError for deliberate McpException (or the specific code of a ToolErrorException, e.g. PresetNotFound, StorageUnavailable), InvalidArguments for argument-binding failures, UnhandledException for anything else; cancellation and protocol errors pass through. It logs every call's start and completion (tool, duration, outcome; never arguments or results) in the WindowsAppManagerMcp.ToolCalls category at Information, with a Warning for slow calls.

*Sources: r1:R6, r2:R3, r1:R8, r1:crit-prio:R8, r1:R3, r1:MCP-2, r2:BRW-1, r2:MSP-2, r2:BRW-G7*

### M0.4 Launch validation, argument quoting and truthful kill_process

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m0-launch-and-kill` (Shipped in 1.12.0)

Allow normal paths such as 'Program Files (x86)' while guarding against argument injection through .bat/.cmd files. Quote arguments correctly. kill_process must never silently escalate to a hard kill and must report what really happened.

**Design.** InputValidationService: drop the shell-metacharacter check on FileName and WorkingDirectory, because UseShellExecute=true never goes through cmd.exe. Keep the control-character and embedded-quote checks. Use Path.GetFullPath plus Exists for rooted paths, and set an ErrorCode on every rejection branch. Build arguments with ArgumentList, verified to apply under ShellExecute on .NET 10 (confirm on net8), or with a CommandLineBuilder.Join that follows CommandLineToArgvW rules for backslashes before quotes, embedded quotes and empty arguments. Pass --user-data-dir without manual quotes. If the resolved target is .bat/.cmd, including PATH shims such as code.cmd, reject arguments containing & | < > ^ % ! or a double quote unless allowShellMetacharacters=true (the BatBadBut class). Debug-launch url: Uri.TryCreate with an http/https/file allowlist; trusted browser paths bypass the user-input validator. kill_process(processId, confirm, mode=graceful|force (default graceful), gracefulTimeoutMs=5000, includeChildren=false, expectedProcessName/StartTime to guard PID reuse). Graceful mode posts WM_CLOSE only to the Alt-Tab set (visible, unowned, non-WS_EX_TOOLWINDOW), never to IME, tray or hidden helper windows. It never escalates: it returns exited=false with status stillRunning or blockedByPrompt and the GW_ENABLEDPOPUP dialog. Force mode calls Kill(entireProcessTree: includeChildren). Check the WaitForExit result. Results add method, exited and killedPids. Map Win32Exception 5 to AccessDenied and server ancestors to RefusedHostAncestor. Extend ProtectedProcesses with sihost, ctfmon, StartMenuExperienceHost, ShellExperienceHost, SearchHost, TextInputHost and fontdrvhost.

*Sources: r1:R49, r2:R36, r2:R9, r1:R55, r2:crit:R9, r1:PAL-2, r1:PAL-5, r1:PAL-7, r1:PAL-8, r1:PAL-9, r1:BRW-5, r1:BRW-14, r1:PAL-missed-3, r1:PAL-missed-5, r1:MCP-missed-3, r2:PAL-2, r2:PAL-3, r2:PAL-7, r2:PAL-13, r2:PAL-14, r2:BRW-2, r2:BRW-9, r2:BRW-13*

### M0.5 Concurrency safety and a UIA traversal that survives bad nodes

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m0-concurrency-and-uia-crash` (Shipped in 1.12.0)

The SDK runs tool calls concurrently. Make the monitor state an atomic snapshot and serialize all injected input. Make UIA traversal survive null ControlType (WinUI AppBar and SemanticZoom) and per-element failures instead of aborting the whole call.

**Design.** MonitorService.RefreshMonitors builds a local ImmutableArray and publishes it atomically with Volatile.Write. Each public method reads exactly one snapshot, and GetAllWindows/FindWindows take one snapshot per enumeration. Optionally use a ~250 ms TTL plus SystemEvents.DisplaySettingsChanged invalidation behind an INativeMonitorWrapper. InputSimulationService gets a process-wide SemaphoreSlim(1,1) InputGate held for each whole Click, TypeText or SendKeys (InputBusy on timeout), so streams never interleave and Ctrl is never held across another call. UIA: when ControlType is null (ids 50039/50040 under the managed client), fall back to LocalizedControlType and then 'Unknown' instead of TryGetValue(null) aborting the call. Raw ids cannot be read through System.Windows.Automation. Wrap each node in try/catch that skips the node, adds a Warning and counts SkippedElements. Enumerate children so that one vanished sibling cannot end the walk. The LayoutService cache is handled in M0.6.

*Sources: r1:R7, r2:R26, r1:R42, r2:R20, r2:R11, r1:WC-11, r1:INP-7, r1:SCR-11, r1:MCP-13, r1:UIA-2, r1:UIA-missed-2, r2:WC-10, r2:INP-missed-monitor-race, r2:SCR-missed-monitor-race, r2:UIA-3*

### M0.6 Layout storage outside the versioned plugin folder, with honest results

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m0-layout-storage` (Shipped in 1.12.0)

Saved layouts live inside the versioned plugin folder today, so every update wipes them, and the sample presets never ship. Move storage, seed the samples, migrate old files, report save and delete outcomes honestly, reload from disk and make the cache thread-safe.

**Design.** Default to %LOCALAPPDATA%/WindowsAppManagerMcp/layouts, overridable with one env var (pick WAM_LAYOUTS_DIR or WAM_LAYOUTS_PATH) that plugin.json can set. Embed config/layouts/*.json as resources and seed them on first run. Migrate any *.json found in AppContext.BaseDirectory/layouts once. Create the directory lazily and return StorageUnavailable instead of failing the singleton factory. save_layout passes the write result through (SaveFailed), returns InvalidName for bad names, and takes overwrite with a replaced flag. delete_layout returns PresetNotFound. get_layout and apply_layout reload from disk using an mtime check. _presetCache becomes a ConcurrentDictionary with a reload lock. The path is reported later in get_server_info.

*Sources: r1:R57, r2:R39, r2:crit-prio:R39, r1:PAL-3, r1:PAL-16, r1:MCP-8, r1:MCP-missed-1, r2:PAL-5, r2:MSP-11, r2:PAL-missed-layout-cache-stale*

### M0.7 CDP timeouts, safe tab selection and explicit data source

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m0-cdp-timeouts` (Shipped in 1.12.0)

No CDP call may hang; today one hangs forever when a JS alert is open. Never return another tab's or another browser's data with Success=true. Every result says whether it came from CDP or UIA.

**Design.** Browser tools become async with timeoutMs (default 5000-10000, clamped 1000-30000) linked to the request CancellationToken. That token goes into ConnectAsync, SendAsync and ReceiveAsync, replacing CancellationToken.None at CdpService.cs:94/129/187/195 and the .GetAwaiter().GetResult() calls. Pass Runtime.evaluate its timeout. Timeouts return CdpTimeout. An open JS dialog is detected through Page.javascriptDialogOpening or a short probe and returns DialogOpen. Target selection: never match an empty title or host, and no reverse-substring matching. Require the listening-port owner PID (GetExtendedTcpTable) to equal the window's PID. When several targets remain, bind through Browser.getWindowForTarget windowId plus bounds. Use the title only as a tie-breaker, after a browser-aware normalizer strips Unicode Cf characters (U+200B), ' and N more pages' and profile/browser suffixes. Exact title equality would regress Edge. Otherwise return AmbiguousTarget with the candidates. Results add Source ('cdp'|'uia'), TargetId and CdpError. A new mode parameter takes auto|cdp|uia, and cdp mode never silently falls back. A textOnly=false request that UIA cannot honor is reported, not dropped.

*Sources: r1:R61, r2:R44, r2:crit-prio:R44, r1:crit:R61, r1:crit-prio:R65, r1:BRW-1, r1:BRW-2, r1:BRW-6, r1:BRW-13, r2:BRW-3, r2:BRW-4*

### M0.8 Docs safety hotfix

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m0-docs-safety` (Shipped in 1.12.0)

Stop the skill and docs from teaching the failure that already closed the user's terminal, and from pointing agents at paths that are known to be broken. Document the four browser tools and fix the tool counts and parameter names.

**Design.** Delete the '%{F4}' and 'Press Alt+F4' guidance and blind destructive-chord examples: SKILL.md:65/231, README.md:130, TOOLS.md:1529-1536. Add a rule: confirm get_foreground_window before any destructive chord, and prefer close_window. Remove 'analyze the base64' (SKILL.md:200/241) and describe the real screenshot result shape and maxWidth/saveToFile until M1 image content lands. Remove the controlTypes filtering advice (SKILL.md:206/245) until M1.8 ships. Document get_browser_url, get_active_browser_url, get_browser_content and launch_browser_with_debug, with the CDP security note: unauthenticated local endpoint, empty profile. Set the tool count to 42 and fix parameter names (executable, not path; processId, not pid; positioning, not maximize). Replace 'wait briefly' with explicit poll-and-verify guidance until wait_for_window exists.

## M1 See and type safely

The model sees real pixels it can click from accurately. Keyboard and mouse input cannot reach the agent's own host, a shell window, an elevated or deny-listed window, or an unintended target. Every input result reports where it actually went. The user can pause everything with a hotkey. A desktop-safe suite against a self-owned fixture app validates the real foreground, UIPI and capture behaviour.

M1 is complete on the integration branch `m1-see-and-type-safely`: all 12 items, their review follow-ups (`m1-wave1-followups`, `m1-wave2-followups`, `m1-wave3-followups`), the fixes from a final review of the integrated branch (`m1-fix-concurrency`, `m1-fix-guards`), a docs pass and the fixes for the code gaps it found (`m1-last-fixes`). The server serves 47 tools (42 in 1.12.0). The safe suite (desktop tests skipped) has 2839 unit, contract and integration tests and 167 E2E harness tests; the gated fixture scenarios run only with the opt-in, locally or through e2e.yml on demand.

- M1 shipped in release 1.13.0 on 2026-10-10 (PR #3, release commit 7d57de9), which rebuilt bin/WindowsAppManagerMcp.exe (scripts/Set-Version.ps1, then scripts/Publish-Plugin.ps1). Until then the committed exe was the 1.12.0 build, which ignores the windowHandle argument the M1 skill tells agents to pass, so the merge to main and the release went together.
- The final review found that a hung application could hold the input gate, and with it every input and window tool, indefinitely (D-WIN-12, made worse by the gate). `m1-fix-concurrency` bounds every wait inside and for the gate (M1.10 follow-ups), and `m1-fix-guards` closes gaps in the guards: the target policy for every window change, hidden windows, the menu Esc and the pause chord's modifiers, chord spellings, the agent's console server, unreadable process trees, mouse capture and held buttons, minimized captures and the partial UI Automation tree (see the items below).
- Deliberate trade-offs, recorded under M1.5 and M1.10: with the default pause chord the agent can release only a stuck Windows key, and a mouse button that the server did not press blocks clicks and moves until the user presses and releases it.
- Moved on: get_server_info and server instructions (M2.11), window selectors (M2.4), tool annotations (M2.10), asynchronous and verified geometry calls (M2.3), capture health for hidden, cloaked and hung windows (M2.9), cached UI Automation walks (M3.1), the capture registry and image-space clicks (M3.11), the WebView2 fixture pane (M3.3) and the in-process MCP contract tests (M2.12). The gaps the M1 docs pass found in the code are fixed on `m1-last-fixes`; the notes under M1.3, M1.5, M1.7 and M1.10 say what they were.

### M1.1 Default host-window input guard

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m1-security-probes` (detection, Done), `m1-input-guard` (wiring, Done)

Input that names no target still goes to the focused window, which is usually the agent's own terminal. A cheap HostGuard on the default input path makes that impossible unless the agent explicitly targets the host.

**Design.** At startup a HostGuard singleton records three things. (a) The server's ancestor PIDs, from a Toolhelp parent walk with creation-time checks. (b) The root owner of GetConsoleWindow(). (c) GetForegroundWindow() at server start, which covers Windows Terminal default-terminal delegation, where WindowsTerminal.exe is not an ancestor. Before every SendInput batch, inside the input gate, resolve the receiving root window: GetAncestor(GetForegroundWindow(), GA_ROOTOWNER) for keys, and WindowFromPoint (a new P/Invoke) plus GA_ROOT for mouse input. If that window is in the host set, return TargetIsAgentHost with the observed foreground window, unless the call explicitly targets it with allowHostTarget=true. Add WindowInfo.IsAgentHost and report the host set in get_server_info. Unit-test with mocked wrappers.

**As shipped (detection, `m1-security-probes`).** IHostGuard (HostGuard) is created at server start by a hosted service, so the foreground window it records is the one the agent was started from. It records the ancestors with their start times, the root owner of GetConsoleWindow() and the root owner of the startup foreground window, and answers IsHostProcess(pid) and IsHostWindow(hwnd), root-owner aware. Refinements: an ancestor PID that now belongs to a newer process stops counting, while one that cannot be verified still counts. The process that owns the console host window is a host process. The startup foreground window protects only itself and the popups it owns as host windows, but its process is a host process for the process-level guards (kill and close-process): killing a delegated Windows Terminal closes the agent's terminal even though it is neither an ancestor nor the console host. The recorded host is logged once at Information. The desktop shell (the owner of GetShellWindow()) is left out of the window rule, because explorer.exe is often an ancestor and also owns the taskbar, the desktop and File Explorer; a startup foreground window that belongs to the shell is not recorded. Follow-up (`m1-fix-guards`): the record also holds the console server behind the server's console: the process that owns the console window, and every conhost.exe or OpenConsole.exe that the process showing the console (the terminal, unless it is the desktop shell) started. Under ConPTY (Windows Terminal) the console window is a pseudo-window the terminal owns, so OpenConsole.exe is neither an ancestor nor the console host and was not recorded before; ending it ends the agent's console. These are host processes, so kill_process refuses them (M1.7).

**As shipped (wiring, `m1-input-guard`).** The input guard of M1.5 asks IsHostWindow before every SendInput batch, with or without a target: for keys about the foreground window, for the mouse about GA_ROOT of WindowFromPoint. Both are passed to IsHostWindow as they are, because it now walks the owner chain itself (see below), so a GA_ROOTOWNER pre-step is not needed. A host window is refused with TargetIsAgentHost, and the result's receivingWindow names it. allowHostTarget lifts the rule only when the call names the window (or one it owns) as windowHandle; without windowHandle it has no effect, and the message says so. Refinement: GA_ROOTOWNER walks GetParent, which follows owners only through WS_POPUP windows, so a WPF or WinForms window owned without WS_POPUP was its own root owner and slipped past IsHostWindow, the close guard and the startup recording. WindowOwnerChain (the window, its GA_ROOT, every GW_OWNER above it and whatever GA_ROOTOWNER names, at most 32 owners) is now the one rule for IsHostWindow, the close guard's shell check, the recorded startup foreground window (the top of the chain) and the input guard's mouse rule, so close, input and IsAgentHost agree. WindowInfo.IsAgentHost comes from IsHostWindow wherever WindowService builds a WindowInfo; find_windows, get_all_windows and get_foreground_window return isAgentHost: true and leave it out otherwise. IInputSafetyInfo reports the host record, the target policy and the destructive chords for get_server_info (M2.11), which is still open. Follow-up (`m1-wave3-followups`): kill_process asks IsHostProcess too, and checks a forced kill's whole process tree with it (see M1.7).

*Sources: r1:crit-missing:host-input-guard, r2:crit-missing:host-input-guard, r1:crit:R55, r1:R55, r2:crit:R8, r1:INP-1, r1:MCP-4, r2:MSP-3*

### M1.2 Captures as MCP image content with the true origin and an image-to-screen transform

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m1-captures` (Done)

Return every capture as an image content block the model can see, with vision-sized defaults. Ship the corrected window-capture origin and transform in the same change: downscaled images on top of a (0,0) origin would increase mis-clicks.

**Design.** A shared ToolImages helper returns CallToolResult{Content=[ImageContentBlock{Data, MimeType}, TextContentBlock(metadata JSON without ImageData)], IsError=!Success} and isolates the SDK's byte-API change. maxDimension sets the long edge (default 1568), with maxWidth kept as an alias. Default to jpeg q80 for monitor and window captures, and png for small regions or on request. An unknown format returns InvalidFormat. Encode from Format32bppRgb so the undefined alpha byte cannot make regions transparent. output=image|file|both|metadata, with saveToFile kept as an alias. outputPath is resolved with GetFullPath, needs an extension that matches the format, gets no implicit directory creation, and needs overwrite=true to replace an existing file. Origin: IScreenCaptureWrapper returns CaptureResult(Bgra, Width, Height, ScreenOriginX/Y, Method). Frame captures use the GetWindowRect origin; client captures map the client rectangle to the screen with MapWindowPoints (new P/Invoke), which is also right for mirrored right-to-left windows. This replaces CapturedRegion(0,0) at ScreenshotService.cs:185, and capture_with_elements overlays use the same origin. Metadata: imageToScreen{offsetX, offsetY, scaleX, scaleY}, originalWidth/Height, mimeType, byteSize and coordinateSpace='physical-virtual-screen'. ScaleFactor is documented as informational only. Delete the dead SaveImageToFile and EncodeImage.

**As shipped (`m1-captures`, with the wave 1 follow-ups).** Infrastructure/ToolImages builds every capture result: an ImageContentBlock (base64, image/jpeg or image/png) followed by a TextContentBlock with the metadata JSON (no image data), and isError = !success; a failure returns only the metadata. [ToolResultPayload] names the metadata type of each capture tool for the contract tests. maxDimension (default 1568, 0 = full resolution) limits the long edge; maxWidth given alone replaces that default and limits only the width, as before. format auto is JPEG at quality 80, or PNG when the long edge is at most 512 pixels; png, jpeg and jpg are accepted and anything else is InvalidFormat. Pixels are encoded from Format32bppRgb, so images are opaque (D-SCR-10). output is image, file, both or metadata, with saveToFile as an alias. outputPath must be an absolute path on a drive letter whose extension matches the format (auto follows the extension), in an existing folder; no folder is created, an existing file needs overwrite=true (FileExists), and the default is a new file in %TEMP%\WindowsAppManagerMcp\captures. Beyond the design, UNC and device paths (\\server\share, \\?\, \\.\) are refused with InvalidOutputPath, so a prompt-injected path cannot send a capture, or an NTLM handshake, to another computer. IScreenCaptureWrapper returns CaptureResult with the screen origin and the method (PrintWindow, or BitBlt when the window refused to draw itself): GetWindowRect for frame captures, and for client captures the client rectangle mapped with MapWindowPoints (a wave 1 review fix: the client point (0,0) of a mirrored right-to-left window is its top-right corner, so mapping that point alone gave the wrong origin). capture_with_elements draws its overlays and reports imageBounds from the same origin. The metadata adds imageToScreen {offsetX, offsetY, scaleX, scaleY} with a scale per axis, originalWidth/Height when scaled, mimeType, byteSize, captureMethod and coordinateSpace physical-virtual-screen. Refinements: an edge that would scale below one pixel keeps one, and the per-axis scale absorbs it; overlay pens are widened by the downscale factor, so lines stay visible in a scaled image (D-SCR-08), and overlay rectangles are clipped to the image (D-SCR-15); the dead encode and save paths are gone and EncoderParameters is disposed (D-SCR-18). capture_with_elements passes on the UI Automation walk's errorCode (for example OperationTimedOut) and draws the partial tree a timeout leaves (M1.8). Follow-up (`m1-fix-guards`): capture_window and capture_with_elements refuse a minimized window (IsIconic of the window or of its top-level window) with CaptureFailed before any pixel or UI Automation read, instead of returning the 160x28 icon image at (-32000,-32000). The fixture suite checks the frame and client origins and swatch pixels at full resolution, and the client origin of a mirrored window (M1.11). Still open: hidden and cloaked windows are captured as they are, frame captures keep the invisible DWM borders, and a hung window's PrintWindow has no deadline (M2.9); the capture registry, captureId and image-space clicks (M3.11); a write root for outputPath and cleanup of old capture files (not scheduled).

*Sources: r1:R15, r2:R12, r1:R17, r1:crit-prio:R17, r2:R13, r2:crit-prio:R13, r1:R5, r1:R55, r1:SCR-1, r1:MCP-1, r1:SCR-5, r1:SCR-12, r1:SCR-G3, r1:MCP-missed-2, r2:SCR-1, r2:MSP-1, r2:SCR-G1, r2:SCR-2, r2:SCR-9, r2:MSP-missed-overlay-offset*

### M1.3 Verified window activation (EnsureForeground)

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m1-foreground` (Done)

One routine that gets a window in front despite the foreground lock and reports what is actually in front afterwards. focus_window, the input guard, element-action fallbacks and layout focus all use it.

**Design.** IWindowService.EnsureForeground(handle, timeoutMs=1000) returns FocusOutcome{IsForeground, ForegroundHandle/Title/Process, Method: already|setForeground|attachThreadInput|inputUnlock, BlockingPopup?, ErrorCode FocusFailed|NotResponding|Cloaked}. It runs on one dedicated long-lived thread, which gets a message queue on its first User32 call, so attach and detach happen on the same thread. Steps: (1) Succeed if the GA_ROOTOWNER of the foreground window is the target's root. Owned dialogs count as success; report them through GW_ENABLEDPOPUP. (2) Refuse a hung or cloaked target. (3) SW_RESTORE if the target is iconic. (4) SetForegroundWindow. (5) AttachThreadInput(GetCurrentThreadId(), fgTid, TRUE), then BringWindowToTop and SetForegroundWindow, detaching in a finally block. Skip this step when the foreground thread is hung or the foreground process has higher integrity. (6) Last resort: make the server the last-input process with a zero-delta MOUSEEVENTF_MOVE or an unassigned VK, never a lone Alt tap into the agent's terminal, then SetForegroundWindow. After activation, if GetGUIThreadInfo(targetTid).flags has GUI_INMENUMODE, send Esc to the target. Poll for verification after each step. focus_window returns FocusOutcome.

**As shipped.** EnsureForeground lives on its own IForegroundService (ForegroundService), next to IsInFront(handle), the same in-front rule as a cheap read for the input guard's re-checks; the unverified IWindowService.FocusWindow is gone. The thread calls are behind a new INativeThreadInputWrapper (GetCurrentThreadId, GetWindowThreadId, AttachThreadInput, GetGuiThreadInfo), and NativeCallGuardTests pins AttachThreadInput to it. ForegroundThread runs every activation on one long-lived background thread, one call at a time, started on first use. A call to another thread's window (ShowWindow, BringWindowToTop) can block until that thread answers, so a call that misses timeoutMs plus a 2 s grace returns NotResponding: its token is cancelled, so it takes no further step, its thread finishes, detaches on the same thread and exits, and the next call starts a new thread; while 2 such threads are still blocked, calls fail at once. The blocked call itself still completes, and SW_RESTORE and BringWindowToTop activate, so the window can still come to the front late; the input guard re-checks IsInFront. The wait for an earlier call and the work have separate limits (each the call's timeout), so a call that waited in line keeps its full time; one that waited too long fails with FocusFailed ("another window activation was still running"), not NotResponding. A detach that Windows refuses retires the activation thread, so no later activation runs on a thread that may still share another thread's input state. Refinements: the in-front rule is the foreground window's GW_OWNER chain: the target counts as in front when it is the foreground window or in that chain, so a dialog target's owner or sibling dialog does not count as the dialog. GA_ROOTOWNER equal to the target is only a fast positive: it walks GetParent, which follows owners only through WS_POPUP windows, so a WPF or WinForms owned window (overlapped, owned through GWLP_HWNDPARENT) is its own root owner, and a required GA_ROOTOWNER match would report the target's own WPF dialog in front as FocusFailed. The dialog in front of the target is BlockingPopup; so is the GW_ENABLEDPOPUP of a target that a modal dialog disables (WS_DISABLED), and that dialog is what gets activated. The unlock is a zero-distance MOUSEEVENTF_MOVE only, not an unassigned VK: a key event goes to the keyboard focus, which is the agent's terminal exactly when activation is refused, and terminals can forward unknown keys to the console app; the unlock is skipped when the input desktop is not available. Esc is sent only after an activation step (never on "already"), once, right after re-verifying that the target is in front, and reported as MenuClosed when the menu closed. There is no second Esc: nothing shows whether the window has processed the first, and an Esc that arrives after the menu has closed reaches the window (IDCANCEL in a dialog). A menu still active when the call returns, including one the user opened in a window already in front, is reported as InMenuMode, so the input guard can refuse to type into it. focus_window returns FocusResult (success kept, equal to isForeground; handle, method, foregroundHandle/Title/Process, blockingPopup, menuClosed, inMenuMode, error, errorCode). apply_layout focuses its focus placements with EnsureForeground, and a placement that does not come to the front is an ArrangeFailed error that names the errorCode and the reason (D-PAL-25). LayoutService collects one FocusResult per focused placement that was arranged, and apply_layout returns them as focus, in placement order (`m1-last-fixes`: the docs pass found that the tool's ApplyLayoutResultDto had no field for them and dropped them). The E2E guard classifies AttachThreadInput as a new ThreadInputAttach kind: refused in the no-input tier, and in the input tier allowed only for the calling thread's own queue; a zero-distance mouse move may be sent wherever the cursor is. Gated scenarios bring the fixture to the front, and bring it to the front with its WPF modal open (the dialog is activated and reported as BlockingPopup). Follow-up (`m1-wave2-followups`): bringToFront in move/snap/set_bounds/move_to_monitor no longer calls BringWindowToTop (r2:WC-missed-BringWindowToTop-activates, D-WIN-16). SetWindowPos(HWND_TOP, SWP_NOACTIVATE) alone raises the window without activating it, and the docs say that focus_window activates. Two paths still activate the window whatever bringToFront says: restoring a minimized window with SW_RESTORE (set_window_bounds, snap_window, move_window_to_monitor and the batches), and move_window_to_monitor's 'maximize', which uses SW_MAXIMIZE and ignores bringToFront. The code change is left to M2.3; since `m1-wave3-followups` the bringToFront descriptions and the TOOLS.md Window Control note name both cases. Follow-up (`m1-wave3-followups`): IsInFront decides with WindowOwnerChain (M1.1), the rule of the close guard, IsHostWindow and the input guard's mouse rule, instead of its own GA_ROOTOWNER-then-GW_OWNER walk. It counts the same windows (GA_ROOTOWNER of a top-level window is on its GW_OWNER chain), and a test checks every foreground and target pair of the dialog fixtures against WindowOwnerChain.

**Follow-ups (`m1-fix-guards`).** EnsureForeground refuses, before any activation step: a window that the target policy (M1.12) denies, with TargetDeniedByPolicy, before the activation thread runs; and a hidden top-level window (not WS_VISIBLE, such as an app's window hidden in the notification area) with the new WindowErrorCode.WindowHidden (27), because SetForegroundWindow can activate it and keys would go to a window the user cannot see (the input guard reports it as TargetNotForeground). When a modal dialog disables the target, the policy is checked for the dialog that would be activated too (a credential prompt of another process). The menu-closing Esc is skipped while a modifier key is down, and the menu reported as InMenuMode, so a held Ctrl, Alt or Shift cannot turn it into Ctrl+Esc, Alt+Esc or Ctrl+Shift+Esc; the input tools refuse with ModifiersHeld in the same case. The docs pass found that a minimized target was restored before the denied-dialog check, so a refused focus could already have restored the owner, and with it the dialog, while the error said the window was not restored. `m1-last-fixes` decides before the restore: the policy is checked for the GW_ENABLEDPOPUP of a disabled target and, for a minimized one, for every top-level window it owns (found by enumeration, because Windows hides the windows of a minimized owner and GW_ENABLEDPOPUP finds only visible ones), and a refusal restores nothing. A denied dialog that shows up only after the restore is still refused, with an error that says the window was restored. The window calls of the other window tools got a deadline of their own (M1.10 follow-ups, D-WIN-12).

*Sources: r1:R25, r2:R6, r1:crit:R25, r2:crit:R6, r1:WC-1, r1:WC-G3, r2:WC-1, r2:WC-G4, r2:MSP-3, r2:WC-missed-BringWindowToTop-activates, r2:PAL-missed-apply_layout-ignores-focus-result*

### M1.4 Integrity, elevation and desktop-state probes

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m1-security-probes` (Done)

A small mockable service that answers three questions: is the target more privileged than the server, is the input desktop available, and is the session locked. Without it, UIPI and secure-desktop failures are invisible to the agent.

**Design.** IProcessSecurityProbe uses a new NativeMethods.Advapi32 (OpenProcessToken; GetTokenInformation with TokenElevation and TokenIntegrityLevel; GetSidSubAuthority), User32 OpenInputDesktop/CloseDesktop and WTSQuerySessionInformation. Results are cached per PID plus creation time. GetIntegrity(pid) returns Low/Medium/High/System/Unknown; ACCESS_DENIED on OpenProcessToken from a medium-integrity server maps to LikelyElevated. It also exposes ServerIntegrity, IsInputDesktopAvailable() and IsSessionLocked(). Consumers: the input guard (TargetElevated, DesktopUnavailable, SessionLocked before SendInput); UIA warnings for elevated targets; WindowService Win32 error mapping (5 to AccessDenied); get_server_info; list_processes (M6.6).

**As shipped.** ProcessSecurityProbe sits on a read-only INativeSecurityWrapper, whose Advapi32, desktop and Wtsapi32 calls are on the NativeCallGuardTests allow-list. The levels add Untrusted and MediumPlus, and ProcessIntegrity.IsMorePrivilegedThan compares raw RIDs, as UIPI does. Only ACCESS_DENIED from OpenProcessToken, after OpenProcess succeeded, maps to LikelyElevated, and only while the server runs below high integrity. ACCESS_DENIED from OpenProcess itself maps to Restricted, which does not count as more privileged: it comes from the process DACL, so it covers SYSTEM and service accounts but also another user's medium-integrity process in the same session ("Run as different user") and apps with hardened DACLs, which UIPI does not block. Protected processes allow PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION, so protection alone does not cause it. Measured on Windows 11 from a medium process: same-user high-integrity processes started by the Task Scheduler expose their token (reported as High), and every process of another account denied OpenProcess. A token denial from an elevated server, or with the server's own level unknown, is Restricted too. The cache key is the creation time that the token query itself read, so a PID reused in between is not mixed up; Unknown results are not cached, and the cache is bounded. IsInputDesktopAvailable also requires the input desktop to be the server's own desktop and the session not to be disconnected, because OpenInputDesktop still succeeds in a disconnected session. The input guard (M1.5) consumes them: SessionLocked, DesktopUnavailable and TargetElevated (never for Restricted).

*Sources: r2:R7, r1:R26, r1:R52, r1:INP-5, r1:PAL-G4, r1:MCP-G5, r2:INP-3, r2:UIA-8, r2:WC-9, r2:PAL-G4, r2:PAL-14, r2:MSP-G7*

### M1.5 Target-guarded input with receiving-window reporting

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m1-input-guard` (Done)

Every mouse and keyboard tool can name its intended window. The server refuses to send input that would land elsewhere, including clicks into a modal-disabled window. Every result reports where the input actually went.

**Design.** Add an optional window target (a handle now; a selector after M2.4) to click, right_click, double_click, mouse_move, type_text, send_keys and every new input tool. IInputTargetGuard runs inside the M0 input gate. Probe checks (M1.4) come first. Keys: EnsureForeground, then require the foreground root to equal the target root. Mouse: accept the WindowFromPoint+GA_ROOT window if it equals the target root, is in the target's GW_OWNER chain, or belongs to the target process or a descendant (Toolhelp walk; covers WebView2/msedgewebview2 and IME popups). Otherwise return TargetOccluded. If IsWindowEnabled(target) is false, return BlockedByModal with the GW_ENABLEDPOPUP handle and title. type_text re-checks the foreground root every 32 characters and stops with TargetLostFocus and CharactersTyped. A configurable destructive-chord list (%{F4}, ^w, ^q, ^+{ESC}, #l) requires an explicit target, otherwise DestructiveChordNeedsTarget. Every input result, guarded or not, gains ReceivingWindow{Handle, Title, ProcessName, ProcessId}, ForegroundAfter and ActualX/Y. The host guard (M1.1) and the policy (M1.12) run on the same path.

**As shipped (`m1-input-guard`).** click, right_click, double_click, mouse_move, type_text and send_keys take an optional windowHandle (long) and allowHostTarget (bool). InputTargetGuard runs inside the input gate before the first SendInput batch, and a cheaper re-check before every later one. Order: an untargeted destructive chord (a property of the request, so it comes first); SessionLocked, DesktopUnavailable; for an explicit target: InvalidHandle, then TargetElevated, TargetDeniedByPolicy, TargetIsAgentHost (unless allowHostTarget), then BlockedByModal; then the receiving window. With a target the receiving window must belong to the target first (TargetNotForeground, TargetOccluded), so an occluding window is reported as occlusion rather than by its own probe; then its own probes, policy and host checks, which matters for a dialog of another process that the target owns (a CredentialUIBroker prompt owned by the target is refused by policy). Keys: EnsureForeground, then IForegroundService.IsInFront (the owner chain, WindowOwnerChain since `m1-wave3-followups`), and the foreground window is the receiving window. Mouse: the GA_ROOT of WindowFromPoint is accepted when it is the target root or a window the target owns (owner chain); the process rule (target process or a descendant, through the new read INativeProcessWrapper.GetAncestorsOf) is limited to popups: windows without a title bar or tool windows, and not WS_EX_APPWINDOW. Accepting any window of the target process would let another main window of the same process count as the target: Notepad, Explorer, browsers and Office keep several main windows in one process, so a click aimed at one could land in another. BlockedByModal uses WS_DISABLED (what IsWindowEnabled reads, so no new P/Invoke) and names GW_ENABLEDPOPUP, or else the first visible, enabled window in Z order that the target owns, which also finds WPF dialogs. Keys go to a dialog the target owns only while the target is enabled (a modeless dialog); a modal one is BlockedByModal, so a stray Enter cannot answer a save prompt. type_text sends batches of 32 characters (one per batch with delayMs; surrogate pairs are never split), re-checks the foreground before every batch and stops with TargetLostFocus and CharactersTyped. Without a target the anchor is the window the first batch went to; a dialog it owns that comes to the front passes the window checks and typing goes on. type_text is refused while the receiving window is in menu mode (TargetInMenuMode: FocusOutcome.InMenuMode, or GetGUIThreadInfo without a target); send_keys is not, so {ESC} and arrows still work in menus. Clicks are checked before the cursor moves and again before each button press. Destructive chords are matched on the parsed key events, so %{f4}, +%{F4} and ^+w count too; the list comes from WAM_DESTRUCTIVE_CHORDS (space-separated, "default" and "none" understood). send_keys has no Windows-key modifier, so #l is kept in the list only for when it gets one; '#' stays a character. Every result gains receivingWindow, foregroundAfter, blockingWindow and, for the mouse, actualX/actualY. New InputErrorCode values from 20: TargetIsAgentHost, TargetNotForeground, TargetOccluded, BlockedByModal, TargetLostFocus, DestructiveChordNeedsTarget, TargetElevated, DesktopUnavailable, SessionLocked, TargetDeniedByPolicy, InvalidHandle, TargetInMenuMode. EnsureForeground's own input (the zero-distance move, one verified Esc) keeps its own rules and is not re-checked by the guard. Gated E2E scenarios (not run in the default suite): BlockedByModal with the fixture's WPF modal, %{F4} without a target, TargetIsAgentHost while the test run's host is in front, targeted typing that brings the fixture back from behind its document window, and TargetOccluded under the document window. Still open: a selector target (M2.4) and get_cursor_position.

**Review fixes (`m1-input-guard`).** The mouse rule's process part accepts a popup of another process only when that process is a direct child of the target (the WebView2 browser process an app starts itself), and never when the target is the desktop shell or another explorer.exe: they start almost every program, so with a File Explorer target a fullscreen browser or a borderless game at the point counted as part of it. The desktop and the taskbars never count as popups. allowHostTarget lifts the host rule only for the target's top-level window and the windows it owns, not for a host popup accepted by the process rule. A modal dialog that opens while type_text types (over the target, or over the dialog that gets the keys) is BlockedByModal, and so is a modal dialog of a dialog the target owns at the first check. Mouse input with no window at the point fails closed (TargetOccluded). Each button press is sent in one SendInput call together with an absolute move to the checked point (MOUSEEVENTF_ABSOLUTE with MOUSEEVENTF_VIRTUALDESK, aimed at the middle of the pixel, so the floor mapping Windows uses and a rounding one both give the pixel back), so a user moving the mouse cannot make the press land elsewhere; a pointer that does not stay at the point after being placed again (ClipCursor) is CursorNotAtPoint (32), and the E2E fixture guard checks where a batch's absolute move ends. The input gate is a semaphore (InputGate) shared with a call-tool filter (InputGateToolFilter) that runs every window-changing tool (focus, move, restore, close, launch, kill, apply_layout) inside it, so a parallel focus_window cannot change the foreground between the guard's check and the input; a contract test classifies every tool, and an unclassified one is serialized. type_text and send_keys re-check right before every batch, the first one too. send_keys refuses keys after a chord that hands the keyboard to another window (Alt+Tab, Alt+Esc, Ctrl+Esc, the Windows key, Alt+Space, a destructive chord) with KeysAfterFocusChange (33), and Ctrl+F4 joins the default destructive chords. Residuals, documented in TOOLS.md: the user or another program can still change the foreground in the milliseconds between the last check and delivery; application keys (Enter, menu mnemonics) can open or switch windows; and a borderless window of a program the target started itself counts as the target.

**Follow-ups (`m1-fix-guards`).** Windows sends mouse input to the window that captured the mouse, not to the window at the point, so the mouse rule now reads the capture (GetGUIThreadInfo) of the foreground thread and of the thread at the point before every press and refuses with the new InputErrorCode.MouseInUse (40), unless the window at the point holds the capture or a window of its thread that the input may reach does (an open menu captures the mouse for its owner). The click and move tools also refuse with MouseInUse while a mouse button is down (GetAsyncKeyState of the five buttons), right before the pointer is placed and before every press: the press would go to the window the button went down on. Chords are matched in every spelling of their key: send_keys sends a literal character as a Unicode packet, so '% c' slipped past the Alt+Space rule; a key press of a character that has a key of its own (space, Tab, Enter, Backspace, Esc, letters and digits) now counts as that key for the focus-change, destructive and pause chords. Trade-off: a mouse button that Windows reports down although the server did not press it (stuck, or left over from a server that crashed) blocks every click and move with MouseInUse until the user presses and releases it; release_all_input releases only the buttons the server tracks. The docs pass found that the capture check treated a GUITHREADINFO that cannot be read (the thread just exited) as no capture, while the guard fails closed on its other unknowns; since `m1-last-fixes` a thread of the window at the point or of the foreground window that cannot be read, or whose GUI state cannot be read, is refused with MouseInUse as well.

*Sources: r1:R26, r2:R8, r2:crit:R8, r2:R11, r1:INP-1, r1:INP-5, r1:MCP-4, r1:UIA-G6, r1:INP-G6, r2:INP-2, r2:INP-3, r2:MSP-3, r2:INP-G6, r2:MSP-G3, r2:WC-G4*

### M1.6 Unfiltered window reads and user-facing listings

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m1-window-reads` (Done)

Post-action reads, get_foreground_window and the guard must be able to see untitled popups, menus and splash screens. Listings must stop showing phantom cloaked or shell windows and must attribute UWP windows to the real app.

**Design.** INativeWindowWrapper gains GetClassName, GetWindowLongPtr(GWL_EXSTYLE), GetWindow(GW_OWNER), DwmGetWindowAttribute (CLOAKED=14, EXTENDED_FRAME_BOUNDS=9), IsHungAppWindow and GetDpiForWindow. ReadWindowInfo(hwnd) does no filtering and backs GetWindowInfo, get_foreground_window, post-action reads, captures and the guard's ReceivingWindow. The IsUserFacingAppWindow predicate applies to listings only: the window is visible, not cloaked, not WS_EX_TOOLWINDOW unless it is also WS_EX_APPWINDOW, unowned, and its class is not Progman, WorkerW, Shell_TrayWnd or Shell_SecondaryTrayWnd. Cheap filters run before Process lookups, and PID-to-name is cached per call. An explicit handle, className or includeShell=true bypasses the predicate, so the taskbar and tray stay reachable. For ApplicationFrameWindow, report the CoreWindow child's PID and process with HostProcessName=ApplicationFrameHost. find_windows gains includeCloaked, includeToolWindows and className. WindowInfo gains ClassName, IsCloaked and IsAgentHost.

**As shipped (`m1-window-reads`, refined in the wave 1 and wave 2 follow-ups).** INativeWindowWrapper gained GetClassName, GetWindowLongPtr, GetWindow, IsCloaked (DWMWA_CLOAKED), GetExtendedFrameBounds (DWMWA_EXTENDED_FRAME_BOUNDS), IsHungAppWindow, GetDpiForWindow and EnumChildWindows (which lists nothing for a zero parent), with a new NativeMethods.Dwmapi. NativeCallGuardTests keeps these window-query APIs on their own list, which only NativeWindowWrapper's query members may reach, followed through private helpers. GetWindowInfo and get_foreground_window read any window unfiltered, so untitled popups, menus, splash screens, the taskbar and cloaked windows are described with their class name instead of coming back null, and post-action reads and capture_with_elements work on untitled windows. Listings apply IsUserFacingAppWindow: visible, not cloaked, not a tool window unless also WS_EX_APPWINDOW, not Progman, WorkerW or a taskbar, titled, top-level (its GA_ROOT is itself) and not the server's own. Unlike the design, owned windows still count: dialogs such as Save As are common targets, and some apps own their main window. Cheap reads run before process lookups, and each process name is read once per call. FindWindows takes a WindowQuery: a handle, a className or includeShell=true skips the predicate, and includeCloaked and includeToolWindows widen the listing but keep the shell filter. A UWP ApplicationFrameWindow reports its hosted CoreWindow's process, with ApplicationFrameHost as HostProcessName and HostProcessId; a minimized app is matched by its top-level CoreWindow's title when that is unambiguous. UwpFrames gives the UI Automation tools the same attribution without sending messages, and find_windows, apply_layout and save_layout also match the host name, so presets that stored ApplicationFrameHost still apply. WindowInfo gained ClassName, IsCloaked, OwnerHandle, HostProcessName and HostProcessId, and IsAgentHost (IHostGuard.IsHostWindow) with `m1-input-guard`; window results always carry className and isCloaked, and ownerHandle, hostProcessName, hostProcessId and isAgentHost only when they apply. list_processes counts every visible, titled top-level window again from an unfiltered query, so a process whose only window is on another virtual desktop is not dropped. Still open: the listing filter matches the shell classes by name alone (WindowClassification.IsShellWindow, which also checks the shell's process, is used by the close guard), and apply_layout does not match cloaked or tool windows (M2 layout work).

*Sources: r1:R32, r2:R29, r1:crit:R32, r1:WC-4, r1:WC-5, r1:WC-13, r1:PAL-14, r1:UIA-6, r1:SCR-6, r1:WC-G2, r2:WC-3, r2:WC-8, r2:WC-12, r2:MSP-missed-untitled-windows, r2:PAL-missed-layout-captures-shell-windows*

### M1.7 Close guards for shell and agent-host windows

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m1-close-guards` (Done)

close_window today posts WM_CLOSE to Program Manager (which opens the Shut Down dialog) or to the agent's own terminal after nothing more than an IsWindow check.

**Design.** close_window and close_windows_batch refuse Progman, WorkerW, Shell_TrayWnd, Shell_SecondaryTrayWnd and HostGuard windows with ProtectedWindow, unless force=true. A batch of more than 3 items requires confirm=true. A PostMessage failure reads GetLastPInvokeError and maps 5 to AccessDenied. Verified close with prompt detection follows in M2.5.

**As shipped.** WindowService takes IHostGuard and checks before it posts WM_CLOSE. The shell rule looks at the window, its GA_ROOT top-level window and its GA_ROOTOWNER root owner, and also matches the GetShellWindow() window whatever its class: DefWindowProc destroys any window that gets WM_CLOSE and does not handle it, so a child or an owned helper popup of the taskbar (its task list, a tooltip) is as unsafe to close as the taskbar itself. The host rule is IHostGuard.IsHostWindow (root-owner aware). File Explorer folder windows stay closable. force=true skips both rules; in close_windows_batch it applies to every window, and refusals are reported per window (WindowResultItem.ErrorCode) while the rest of the batch goes on. More than 3 windows without confirm=true is a ConfirmationRequired tool error and nothing is closed. INativeWindowWrapper.PostMessage now returns Win32CallResult(Succeeded, Win32Error), with the error read inside the wrapper right after the call; a NativeCallGuardTests rule keeps last-error reads in the Native wrappers. 5 maps to AccessDenied, 1400 (the window closed in between) to InvalidHandle, and anything else to CloseFailed with the Win32 error in the message. Still open: the McpServerTool Destructive annotations (M2.10); WindowInfo.IsAgentHost came with `m1-input-guard` (M1.1).

**Follow-ups (`m1-wave2-followups`).** The shell rule now needs the shell's process: a Progman, WorkerW, Shell_TrayWnd or Shell_SecondaryTrayWnd window counts only when it belongs to the process of GetShellWindow() (WindowClassification.IsShellWindow, pure, for the input guard to reuse), because WorkerW is also the class of SHCreateWorkerWindow helpers in any process. The shell window itself counts whatever its class, and while there is no shell window (explorer.exe restarting) the class alone counts. A visible top-level dialog (#32770) is checked without its owners: WM_CLOSE on a dialog works like its Cancel button (DefDlgProc turns it into IDCANCEL) and closing an owned window leaves its owner alone, so a dialog the shell owns, such as Shut Down Windows, can be dismissed without force; hidden dialogs, child dialogs inside a shell window and other owned popups of the shell stay protected, and the host rule still applies. The last-error rule also flags framework members that read the last error themselves (new Win32Exception() and Win32Exception(message), Marshal.GetHRForLastWin32Error, GetLastPInvokeErrorMessage), checked against their IL. A description test ties the written-out 'more than 3 windows' to MaxCloseBatchWithoutConfirm. The GW_OWNER chain for owned overlapped (WPF/WinForms) windows of shell and host windows followed in `m1-input-guard`: the shell rule walks WindowOwnerChain for every window except a visible top-level dialog, and the host rule is IsHostWindow, which walks it too (M1.1).

**Follow-ups (`m1-wave3-followups`).** kill_process asks IHostGuard.IsHostProcess, so the process that shows the server's console and the app that was in front at server start (Windows Terminal as the default terminal) are refused with CannotTerminateHost like the ancestors. The live ancestor walk stays as a fallback for a host record whose ancestor read failed at start. A forced kill reads the process tree first and is refused, naming the child, when a process in it belongs to the host or is this server; the policy check of the same tree (M1.12) comes first. A graceful close asks only the main window to close and does not read the tree.

**Follow-ups (`m1-fix-guards`).** kill_process also refuses the console server of the server's console (OpenConsole.exe or conhost.exe, see M1.1) with CannotTerminateHost. A forced kill whose process tree cannot be read is refused with the new ProcessErrorCode.ProcessTreeUnreadable (15) instead of ending an unchecked tree: INativeProcessWrapper.GetDescendantsOf returns null when the Toolhelp snapshot fails, where it returned the same empty list as "no children". The docs pass found two paths that still failed open: a snapshot whose Process32FirstW fails read as an empty process list, and a snapshot that lacks the process just found running gave an empty list, both read as "no children". `m1-last-fixes` makes both return null (ProcessTreeUnreadable), through a seam that tests drive: NativeProcessWrapper.ReadProcessList reads the entries and is null when not even the first can be read, and the wrapper takes the snapshot reader as an optional constructor argument.

*Sources: r1:R55, r2:R10, r1:WC-12, r1:PAL-5, r2:WC-11*

### M1.8 Fix the UIA traversal filters behind a testable tree seam

**Priority:** P0 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m1-uia-tree` (Done)

controlTypes:'Button,Edit' returns nothing with Success=true, size and enabled filters prune whole subtrees, and maxDepth is off by one. None of this can be unit-tested today.

**Design.** Add IUiaTree (FromHandle, GetChildren(node, ct), Read(node) returning a properties record) with a System.Windows.Automation adapter in DI and a FakeUiaTree for tests. UIElementService replaces its static calls at :65/:112/:153. A node that fails the type, size or enabled filter is not emitted, but its matching descendants are spliced into its parent; only IsOffscreen prunes a subtree. Each emitted node is counted once, which removes the double count at :296. maxDepth counts returned levels exactly. Unknown controlTypes names return InvalidControlType listing the valid names. Rect.Empty or non-finite bounds become Bounds=null. A sibling-advance failure sets Truncated instead of stopping silently. A timeout returns the partial tree. Re-enable the controlTypes guidance in SKILL.md once this ships. Builds on the M0.5 null-ControlType and per-node handling.

**As shipped (`m1-uia-tree`, with the wave 1 and wave 2 follow-ups).** IUiaTree (IsAvailable, FromHandle, FromPoint, GetFocusedElement, GetParent, GetChildren, Read, ReadInteraction, IsInteractable, ReadIdentity, ReadValue) has a System.Windows.Automation adapter, UiaTree, in DI and a FakeUiaTree for tests; UIElementService and ElementInspectionService (M1.9) reach UI Automation only through it, which NativeCallGuardTests checks. controlTypes, interactableOnly, minWidth and minHeight decide only about the element itself: a node that fails one is not emitted, and its matching descendants are spliced into its parent in tree order; only visibleOnly (IsOffscreen) prunes a subtree. interactableOnly is enabled and (keyboard-focusable or an action pattern: Invoke, Toggle, Value, SelectionItem, ExpandCollapse, RangeValue), read by IsInteractable, which stops at the first true property instead of reading all eight; a node whose interaction properties cannot be read is left out and counted as skipped, and its children are still walked. maxDepth counts returned levels exactly (1 = direct children, clamped to 1-10) and depthReached reports the deepest level read; each emitted node is counted once. Unknown controlTypes names fail with InvalidControlType listing the valid names, which include Unknown and the localized AppBar and SemanticZoom names. Empty or non-finite bounds are left out (bounds null) instead of int.MinValue. A child list or node that cannot be read is skipped and counted (skippedElementCount) and the walk goes on with the siblings; truncated with truncationReason (Timeout, ElementsSkipped) reports an incomplete tree. A timeout returns the elements read so far with truncated=true; only a walk that has not matched an element yet fails with OperationTimedOut. Every read runs on a UiaWorker thread with the deadline plus a 2 s grace; a read still blocked then is abandoned and counted against its application until it returns (a UWP app counts as itself, not as ApplicationFrameHost): while 2 reads of one application are stuck, further reads of it fail at once, and all reads fail at once while 16 are stuck. Test classes that call the real client run one at a time in RealUiAutomationCollection (its first call fails with E_FAIL when two threads make it at once), which a test enforces, and the server's first call is serialized by UiaClientStartup. SKILL.md and TOOLS.md describe the filters again. Follow-up (`m1-fix-guards`): a walk abandoned inside a blocked UI Automation call also returns its partial tree. The walk keeps its tree under a lock that is never held during a UI Automation call, so the caller copies what was read and returns it with truncated=true and truncationReason Timeout (D-UIA-03). Still open: one CacheRequest per walk and a hung-window precheck (M3.1); get_ui_elements does not take the MCP request's cancellation, so it ends at the timeout plus the grace (D-UIA-04); a minimized or elevated target still returns an empty tree without a diagnostic (D-UIA-07, M2.9); capture_with_elements does not report truncation (M2.9); the UIA3 engine and raw control-type ids (M3.2); per-node HasMoreChildren at the depth limit (M3.1).

*Sources: r1:R42, r2:R19, r1:crit:R42, r1:UIA-1, r1:UIA-8, r1:UIA-10, r1:UIA-11, r1:UIA-G9, r1:SCR-missed-1, r2:UIA-1, r2:SCR-4, r2:UIA-6, r2:UIA-12, r2:UIA-13, r2:UIA-missed-unknown-controlTypes, r2:UIA-missed-sibling-advance-drop*

### M1.9 get_focused_element and get_element_at_point

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m1-uia-tree` (Done)

The cheapest checks before typing ('is the caret in the Email field?') and the cheapest way to map a screenshot point to a semantic element. Pulled forward because the observe-act-verify guidance relies on them.

**Design.** get_focused_element() reads AutomationElement.FocusedElement on the UIA worker under a timeout. It returns name, controlType, automationId, className, isPassword, value (at most 200 characters, masked for passwords), the owning top-level hwnd (GA_ROOT) and the process. get_element_at_point(x, y, ancestors=3) uses AutomationElement.FromPoint, whose physical coordinates match the click space, and resolves the top-level window with WindowFromPoint plus GA_ROOT. Both run under a timeout, because FromPoint can hang on unresponsive providers. They return an element ref once the M3.4 registry exists.

**As shipped (`m1-uia-tree`, with the wave 1 follow-ups).** ElementInspectionService reads through IUiaTree (M1.8) on the UiaWorker, with timeoutMs (default 5000, clamped to 1000-30000) linked to the MCP request's cancellation, and returns OperationTimedOut when UI Automation does not answer; the per-application limit on stuck reads applies. get_focused_element reads FocusedElement; get_element_at_point(x, y, ancestors = 3, 0-10) reads FromPoint at physical virtual-screen pixels and returns CoordinatesOutOfBounds for a point on no monitor. The element has name, controlType, automationId, className, frameworkId, bounds, isEnabled, isOffscreen, isKeyboardFocusable, hasKeyboardFocus, isPassword, patterns and value: the Value pattern's text, at most 200 characters with valueTruncated. Deviation: a password field's value is never read, rather than read and masked. The window is the root (GA_ROOT) of WindowFromPoint for a point, through INativeHitTestWrapper, and for the focused element the root of the nearest element with a native handle; processId and processName are the element's own process, which for a UWP app differs from the ApplicationFrameHost window. ancestors stop at the top-level window. The descriptions mark names and values as untrusted and point to type_text and send_keys. Still open: element refs (M3.4).

*Sources: r2:R24, r2:crit-prio:R24, r1:R46, r1:UIA-G6, r2:UIA-G6*

### M1.10 Input session safety: pause hotkey, cancellation and stuck-input recovery

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** improve · **Branch:** `m1-session-safety` (Done)

Give the user a guaranteed way to stop a runaway action. Make long input cancellable, and make sure an interrupted batch can never leave modifiers or buttons held down system-wide.

**Design.** A dedicated message-loop thread calls RegisterHotKey with a configurable chord (default Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Pause); a registration failure is reported in get_server_info. Pressing the hotkey sets Paused, cancels in-flight input through a linked CancellationTokenSource and sends compensating key-ups. While paused, every input tool returns PausedByUser. get_control_status reports the state. Resuming requires the hotkey again or a resume_input call confirmed by the user through elicitation, so the agent cannot resume itself, and send_keys refuses to inject the pause chord. type_text and send_keys become async and take the request CancellationToken. The server tracks the keys and buttons it has injected down. On a short SendInput count, an exception or a cancellation, it sends KEYUPs for every modifier that GetAsyncKeyState reports down. A release_all_input tool does the same on demand, and it also runs on IHostApplicationLifetime.ApplicationStopping and on stdin EOF. If the user is physically holding a modifier, input returns ModifiersHeld unless releaseHeldModifiers=true.

**As shipped (`m1-session-safety`).** IInputSession (InputSession) is the one way input reaches SendInput: the input tools and EnsureForeground's zero-distance unlock and Esc send through it. One lock covers the pause state, every SendInput and SetCursorPos call and the held-input bookkeeping, so once Pause has returned no batch starts until input is resumed; a pause lands between two batches, never inside one (SendInput inserts a batch without interleaving other input anyway). HeldInputTracker works out what is down from the events SendInput inserted: it inserts a prefix of the batch and returns its length, so the held set is exact, not a GetAsyncKeyState guess. On a short count the session sends the up events of what the inserted prefix left down; when SendInput throws (count unknown) it releases the keys and buttons of that batch that GetAsyncKeyState (the new read INativeInputWrapper.IsKeyDown) reports down. Every input call also releases at its end (a failed release is retried there) and before it starts, and refuses with ModifiersHeld when keys the server pressed still cannot be released, so no hold spans two calls. Refinement: releasing Alt or Windows on its own opens the menu bar or the Start menu, so a release that includes them first sends VK 0xE8 (unassigned) down and up, as AutoHotkey's menu mask key does. The pause hotkey runs on PauseHotkeyListener's own background thread: RegisterHotKey without a window (WM_HOTKEY goes to that thread's queue), MOD_NOREPEAT (holding the chord must not toggle back and forth), then a GetMessage loop stopped by a posted WM_APP message; the native side is the new INativeHotkeyWrapper, pinned by NativeCallGuardTests and classified as a new GlobalHotkey kind that no E2E tier allows. Refinement: a keyboard sends Ctrl+Pause as Ctrl+Break (VK_CANCEL), so a chord with Ctrl and Pause registers both VK_CANCEL and VK_PAUSE (remote sessions and some keyboards send VK_PAUSE); either one registering counts, and a partial failure is reported. WAM_PAUSE_HOTKEY sets the chord ("none" turns it off; an invalid value keeps the default and says why). A refused registration (ERROR_HOTKEY_ALREADY_REGISTERED) is reported by get_control_status and logged at start; get_server_info (M2.11) is not built yet. The hotkey always toggles; pausing cancels the session's pause token (CancelAsync, so tool continuations do not run on the hotkey thread), which every input call links with its MCP request token, and releases what the server holds, never the user's keys. While paused every input tool returns PausedByUser, a running call stops between batches or in the delay between keystrokes with what it did so far, and EnsureForeground activates nothing (WindowErrorCode.PausedByUser 26), because activating takes the user's keyboard focus and can send input. resume_input resumes only when the user confirms through MCP elicitation (McpServer.ElicitAsync, a required boolean defaulting to false, a 5-minute answer limit): ResumeNeedsUser when the client declares no form elicitation, ResumeDeclined otherwise; send_keys refuses the chord (PauseChordNotAllowed: one of its keys going down with at least its modifiers held). type_text and send_keys are async and take the request token; every input call waits at most 30 s for the gate (InputBusy) and stops waiting on cancellation or a pause. ModifiersHeld refuses input while Shift, Ctrl, Alt or Windows (left or right) is down; releaseHeldModifiers=true sends them up first and reports them; type_text also stops when a modifier goes down mid-text, which catches the user starting the pause chord. release_all_input releases what the server holds, and with releaseHeldModifiers the modifiers that are down (not while paused). get_control_status reports the pause, the hotkey and held input. The control tools run outside the input gate (a new ControlTools list), so a release or the wait for the user's answer never queues behind a long call. InputSessionStartup starts the hotkey when the server starts and releases held input on ApplicationStopping, which the SDK's SingleSessionMcpServerHostedService also triggers when stdin closes (it stops the application when its session ends), and in StopAsync. New InputErrorCode values 34-39: PausedByUser, ModifiersHeld, InputBusy, PauseChordNotAllowed, ResumeNeedsUser, ResumeDeclined. Tests use fakes for SendInput, the key state and the hotkey thread; MCP-level tests cover elicitation (accept, decline, no answer, no support), notifications/cancelled and stdin EOF. Deferred, as the critics asked: takeover detection stays out (opt-in and hook-based, M7.4); the hotkey is the primary control. One gated E2E scenario (FixtureSessionTests, input tier, not run in the default suite) pauses real typing into the fixture through the session, as the hotkey's handler does, and checks that the fixture got exactly charactersTyped characters, that no modifier is left down and that typing works after the resume; pressing the real chord would need real input on the user's desktop, so the hotkey itself is covered by unit tests with a fake message loop.

**Review follow-ups (`m1-session-safety`).** Every MCP client session starts its own server, and only one process can register a chord, so with two sessions the second server could not be paused. The pause is now shared: a named manual-reset event in the user's Local\ namespace (INativeHotkeyWrapper.OpenDesktopPause, opened by InputSessionStartup before the hotkey starts). The hotkey sets and resets it; every session reads it under its lock before each batch (a pause another server made is in force before the next batch) and a watcher thread waits on it, so running calls stop at once and held input is released; resume_input resets it, so a confirmed resume resumes every server, and the question says so. A session takes over only the flag's changes, so its own pause holds even when the flag cannot be written.
