A server that finds the chord taken (ERROR_HOTKEY_ALREADY_REGISTERED) retries every 2 s, so the chord survives the server that held it, and reports OtherServer when a server holds it (the holder keeps a named mark) or Failed when another program does; get_control_status reports sharedPause. While paused, the call-tool filter that runs the window-changing tools inside the input gate returns PausedByUser without running them (launch_application could otherwise start a program that types); read-only tools keep working. EnsureForeground also stops at every step boundary on a pause, and reports a window that came to the front meanwhile as PausedByUser. send_keys and the clicks read the modifiers again right before each batch (a Shift pressed while the guard activated the target would have turned Delete into Shift+Delete). releaseHeldModifiers never releases the pause chord's modifiers since the user may be pressing it; since `m1-fix-guards` nothing at all is released while any of them is down, because a chord's modifiers go down one after another. resume_input resumes only the pause it asked about (a pause number) and allows one open question; a hotkey press within 2 s of the pause is ignored, so a second press to be sure does not resume. Declined: on a SendInput exception, modifiers of the batch that GetAsyncKeyState reports down are still released, as this item's design asks; leaving a modifier the server may have pressed stuck is worse than releasing one the user pressed mid-call.

**Limits (`m1-session-safety`).** A hotkey cannot tell an injected press from the user's: send_keys refuses the chord, but a program the agent starts (launch_application, refused while paused) could press it, or reset the shared pause, which any program of the user can open. An elevated server and one that is not may not share the pause (the default security of an event an elevated server created keeps a server that is not elevated from opening it); each then keeps its own, and its log says so. resume_input relies on the client showing the question to the user; a client that answered elicitations by itself would defeat it. GetAsyncKeyState reports the session's logical key state, so a key the user holds and a stuck key look the same (hence the explicit releaseHeldModifiers), and it reads 0 while a UAC prompt has the input. A server process that is killed outright releases nothing. The C# SDK client (0.5.0-preview.1) did not send notifications/cancelled when its own token was cancelled in the tests; the server handles the notification, so the MCP-level test sends it explicitly.

**Follow-ups (`m1-fix-concurrency`).** No wait inside or for the input gate is unbounded any more. The window calls that can block on another thread's window (SetWindowPos, ShowWindow) go through IWindowCallRunner (WindowCallRunner): a window that IsHungAppWindow reports hung is refused at once, every other call runs on a background thread of its own with a 5 s deadline (the time after which Windows calls a window hung), and a call that misses it is abandoned and counted until it returns. While one call to an application is still blocked, further calls to it fail at once, and at most 8 can be blocked in all, so a hung application holds the gate for at most one deadline. The single-window tools report this as NotResponding; the batches and apply_layout fail only that window's placement, with the reason in its error. This partly fixes D-WIN-12: SWP_ASYNCWINDOWPOS and ShowWindowAsync are not used, and an abandoned call can still complete once the application answers (M2.3 keeps the asynchronous, verified geometry). The call-tool filter that runs the window-changing tools inside the gate waits at most 30 s (InputBusy, isError, without running the tool), and a pause during that wait returns PausedByUser at once. launch_application and launch_applications_batch clamp waitTimeoutMs to 0-30000, and the window batches clamp delayBetweenMs to 0-5000; each single wait is bounded, but a batch holds the gate for its whole length, which the agent chooses. The tool error filter is now the outermost call-tool filter (the SDK runs the first filter added as the outermost, although the XML documentation of McpServerOptions.Filters says the last), so a call refused while paused and the wait for the gate appear in the ToolCalls log lines and durations; ServerFilterPipelineTests pins the order. A tool name the server does not serve bypasses the gate, so it gets the SDK's protocol error at once, also while paused. The hotkey's 2 s resume delay is measured on the monotonic clock (TimeProvider.GetTimestamp), so a wall-clock change while paused cannot make resume presses be ignored. The server marks itself as the chord's holder before its status says Registered and before Start returns, so a server started right after it reports OtherServer, not Failed; a mark that cannot be made is logged and is not fatal (the hotkey stays Registered, and only a server started later reports Failed instead of OtherServer). When the first registration fails after a key was registered, the keys and the mark are freed, so the hotkey never listens while its status says Failed. resume_input's one-open-question guard lives on the input session (IInputSession.TryOpenResumeQuestion), shared by the whole server: the SDK builds a new InputControlTools for every call, so the instance field it was before never fired in the real server. NativeCallGuardTests pins the shared pause's named events to NativeHotkeyWrapper: every named WaitHandle constructor, OpenExisting and TryOpenExisting, the System.Threading.AccessControl factories, and the names themselves.

**Follow-ups (`m1-fix-guards`).** releaseHeldModifiers and release_all_input release no modifier the server did not press while any modifier of the pause chord is down (the keys and buttons the server pressed are still released), the menu-closing Esc of EnsureForeground is skipped while a modifier is down (M1.3), and chords are matched in every spelling of their key (M1.5). Trade-off: with the default chord (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Pause) the agent can release only a stuck Windows key; a Ctrl, Alt or Shift stuck after a remote-session glitch returns ModifiersHeld until the user presses and releases it. Likewise a mouse button the server did not press blocks the mouse tools with MouseInUse (M1.5). The docs pass found that with two or more keys down the ModifiersHeld message for the pause chord read "so them are not released", and that release_all_input's error called every key down "the modifiers of the pause hotkey", although one modifier of the chord is enough and the other keys down are left alone too; both messages are fixed on `m1-last-fixes`, and they, the release_all_input description and TOOLS.md now state the rule that way (ReleaseInputResult's documentation already did).

*Sources: r2:crit-missing:pause-hotkey, r1:R56, r2:R11, r1:R28, r1:crit:R28, r1:crit:R29, r1:INP-8, r1:INP-G8, r2:INP-G7, r2:INP-12, r2:INP-missed-partial-batch-stuck-modifiers*

### M1.11 Desktop-safe end-to-end tests against a self-owned fixture app

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** improve · **Branch:** `m1-fixture-e2e` (Done; scenarios added as M1.1-M1.10 landed)

After M0.1, nothing tests the real foreground, UIPI, SendInput, capture and UIA paths, where Windows behaviour differs from mocks. A fixture app that the suite owns makes these tests safe to run.

**Design.** tests/FixtureApp is a small WinForms or WPF exe with known AutomationIds: an edit box, a checkbox, a combo box, a button that opens a modal, a context menu, a 10k-item virtualized list, a WebView2 pane and a document that shows a save prompt on close. The E2E project is gated by the M0.1 opt-in. It drives the fixture through the in-process MCP client (M2.12; direct services until then), uses only targeted, guarded input against the fixture, and kills the fixture in Dispose. It covers EnsureForeground, the host guard, BlockedByModal, the capture origin and transform, DPI moves, and later find/act/wait. It runs in a separate CI job on windows-latest only after confirming that the runner has an interactive desktop; otherwise it runs on a self-hosted runner or in Windows Sandbox (.wsb) through workflow_dispatch. It never runs locally without the opt-in. Add the DesktopNoInput gate tier (deferred from M0.1) for capture-only tests against the fixture window.

**Scaffold (as built).** tests/FixtureApp is WamFixtureApp, a code-only WPF app (WPF rather than WinForms for real UIA virtualization in the 10k-item list and popup-window context menus). It has the controls above except the WebView2 pane, which would add a package and a runtime dependency to every build; add it with the Chromium work in M3. It writes a JSON-lines event log (a ready event with its pid, hwnd, DPI, window and client rects and every element's screen rect, then every change it receives) as ground truth independent of the server, takes an instance token for its titles, can start without activation or with the modal or the document open, sets its own DPI awareness, and ends itself when the test host exits or after a maximum lifetime. The gate moved into a shared project, WindowsAppManagerMcp.Tests.Infrastructure, and gained [DesktopNoInputFact]/[DesktopNoInputTheory]: their own opt-in WAM_ALLOW_DESKTOP_NOINPUT_TESTS=1 (the full opt-in includes it), traits Category=Desktop plus Category=DesktopNoInput. tests/WindowsAppManagerMcp.E2ETests builds the real services through ServiceRegistration and puts every native wrapper behind a fail-closed DispatchProxy guard. That includes the process and monitor wrappers that ProcessService and MonitorService would otherwise create themselves: the harness registers them, and an ordinary test walks every resolved service for unguarded wrappers. WrapperCatalog classifies every wrapper member explicitly, and a member it does not list is refused. No-input tests can only read. Input tests can act only on the fixture process, with the foreground (keys) or the window under the cursor (mouse) checked before every SendInput batch; since M1.1 and M1.5 shipped, this is a second, independent layer under the server's own input guard, so a guard bug cannot reach a window outside the fixture. No tier may start a process, and CDP calls are refused. First scenarios against current behaviour: find_windows, get_window_info, get_ui_elements (AutomationIds, control types, bounds against the fixture's own rects, realized items only), capture_window of the client area and the frame with swatch pixels at the expected offsets (no-input), and focus_window plus type_text into the edit box (input). .github/workflows/e2e.yml runs them only through workflow_dispatch and skips with a notice when the runner has no interactive desktop; test.yml's coverage check now merges every project's coverage file.

**As shipped (scenarios).** WrapperCatalog classifies every wrapper member the M1 branches added (thread-input attach and global hotkey are kinds of their own: the input tier allows only the calling thread's own attach and zero-distance moves wherever the cursor is, and no tier registers a hotkey), and the input tier also checks where a click's absolute move ends. The gated scenarios now cover: find_windows and the fixture's owned modal (FixtureWindowTests), the UI element tree, bounds and the virtualized list (FixtureUiElementsTests), the frame and client capture origins with swatch pixels (FixtureCaptureTests) and the client origin of a mirrored right-to-left window the test creates itself (MirroredWindowCaptureTests), EnsureForeground with and without the WPF modal (FixtureForegroundTests), the input guard's BlockedByModal, %{F4} without a target, TargetIsAgentHost, targeted typing that brings the fixture back and TargetOccluded (FixtureInputGuardTests), focus_window plus type_text (FixtureTypingTests), the close guard against the fixture document and the desktop shell (FixtureCloseTests), and a pause in the middle of real typing (FixtureSessionTests). They run only with the opt-in, locally or through e2e.yml on demand; nothing runs them on a schedule. Still open: DPI moves (with M2.3), the in-process MCP client (M2.12), an alpha==255 check of real captures, the WebView2 pane (M3.3), and a desktop runner that runs the suite regularly.

*Sources: r1:crit-missing:fixture-e2e, r2:crit-prio:R5, r2:R5, r1:R5, r1:SCR-G10, r2:MSP-G5*

### M1.12 Server-enforced target allow/deny policy

**Priority:** P2 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m1-input-guard` (Done)

The agent acts on untrusted screen and web content, and the model sets its own confirm flags. A server-side policy stops a prompt-injected agent from typing into password managers, credential prompts or other sensitive apps.

**Design.** WAM_DENY_PROCESSES defaults to common password managers, CredentialUIBroker.exe and consent.exe; an optional WAM_ALLOW_PROCESSES restricts targets further. The policy is enforced through a PID-to-image-name lookup in IInputTargetGuard, element_action, close/kill and capture (deny-listed windows are refused or blacked out). Violations return TargetDeniedByPolicy, and get_server_info reports the active policy. The model cannot override it with tool arguments.

**As shipped (`m1-input-guard`).** ITargetPolicy (TargetPolicy over TargetPolicySettings) is read from the environment at start. The default deny list is 1Password, Bitwarden, KeePass, KeePassXC, LastPass, Dashlane, Keeper, KeeperPasswordManager, Enpass, CredentialUIBroker and consent; a WAM_DENY_PROCESSES value replaces it ("default" puts it back, "none" alone turns it off), and WAM_ALLOW_PROCESSES, when set, is the only list of allowed names. The deny list wins over the allow list. Names compare case-insensitively without a folder or ".exe", and a window is matched by its process and, for a UWP frame, by the app it hosts too. Enforced, with TargetDeniedByPolicy, by the input guard (target and receiving window, before the host rule, so allowHostTarget cannot lift it), close_window and close_windows_batch per window (force does not lift it; WindowErrorCode 25), kill_process (forceKill does not lift it; ProcessErrorCode 14) and capture_window and capture_with_elements (before any pixel or UIA read; ScreenshotErrorCode 11). Not done, by choice: take_screenshot and capture_region do not black out a denied window that is visible in the captured area, which would need the window's visible region from the Z order and the DWM frame; the docs say so. element_action did not exist yet (M3), and get_ui_elements and the browser reading tools were not covered (M3 covers the UI Automation reads: see the M3 follow-ups below). IInputSafetyInfo exposes the active policy for get_server_info (M2.11).

**Review fixes (`m1-input-guard`).** forceKill ends the whole process tree, so a forced kill now reads the tree first (INativeProcessWrapper.GetDescendantsOf, a Toolhelp walk that skips reused PIDs, classified Read in the E2E WrapperCatalog) and refuses with TargetDeniedByPolicy, naming the child, when the policy refuses any process in it (`m1-wave3-followups` checks the same tree for host processes, see M1.7). Settings fail closed: a WAM_DESTRUCTIVE_CHORDS entry that is not one chord (a comma-separated list, say) keeps the default chords in force as well, and whitespace right after ".exe" separates names in WAM_DENY_PROCESSES and WAM_ALLOW_PROCESSES, so "KeePass.exe Bitwarden.exe" does not become one name that replaces the default list. InputSafetyStartup logs the deny list, the allow list and the chords in force at start. The default deny list adds NordPass, ProtonPass and "Proton Pass", RoboForm and pwsafe (Password Safe).

**Follow-ups (`m1-fix-guards`).** The policy covers more than the design and the first as-shipped note list: every window change refuses a denied window with TargetDeniedByPolicy, through IWindowService.EvaluateTargetPolicy, the rule close_window already used. That is focus_window (the target, before any step, and a modal dialog of another app that activation would activate for it), move_window, resize_window, set_window_bounds, set_window_state (minimize included), snap_window, move_window_to_monitor, their batches (per item, with errorCode) and apply_layout (a TargetDeniedByPolicy error; the window is neither arranged nor focused). Without this, an agent could restore a minimized password manager and then read it with take_screenshot. Limits, which TOOLS.md now states instead of the earlier claim that the agent cannot bring a denied window on screen: input can still restore or activate it (a click on its taskbar button, whose window is the shell's; Alt+Tab as the last key of send_keys), the app can bring itself forward (when launch_application starts a single-instance app again), restoring an allowed window with set_window_state 'restore' (or the other restoring window tools) shows a denied app's dialog that it owns (focus_window checks the windows a minimized target owns before it restores it since `m1-last-fixes`, see M1.3), and take_screenshot and capture_region still do not black anything out. Users who rely on the policy to hide an app should close it or keep it on another virtual desktop while the agent works.

**Follow-ups (M3: `m3-element-action`, `m3-dialogs`, and the M3 review: `m3-fix-policy`, `m3-fix-action`, `m3-fix-surface`, `m3-fix-failclosed`).** element_action, respond_to_dialog and file_dialog check their target through IInputTargetGuard.CheckTarget, and element_action also checks the own window of an element of another process (CheckElementWindow), so the policy covers the M3 acting tools. The M3 review widened the policy, with the repo owner's decisions. (1) The default deny list adds CredDialogHost (a Windows credential prompt), CHXSmartScreen and smartscreen (the SmartScreen prompt "Windows protected your PC" is drawn by the CHXSmartScreen UWP app, which smartscreen.exe, the reputation broker, starts) and SecHealthUI (the Windows Security app); the UWP ones are matched through the app an ApplicationFrameHost frame hosts. A WAM_DENY_PROCESSES value without `default` drops them. (2) The policy covers the UI Automation reads of a window, named or not: get_ui_elements, find_ui_elements, get_focused_element, get_element_at_point, read_element_text, read_table and wait_for_element refuse a window of a denied app with TargetDeniedByPolicy (the new UIElementErrorCode 24) before any warm-up or read (UiaTargets.ReadVerdict: the window's process and, for a UWP frame, its app), as capture_window and capture_with_elements already did, and get_active_popups refuses a named one; get_focused_element and get_element_at_point also check the element's own process. M3.5 had decided that a named window may be read while the reads returned names; M3 then added text and table reads, values in find results and a warm-up that turns on the web tree of an Electron password manager. A popup counts as a window nobody named, so the reads leave out a popup of a denied app or of the agent's host and count it (M3.5). (3) It fails closed on every path: a window whose process cannot be named (one that runs elevated, for example) is refused while the policy denies anything, by the reads, the input guard (the input tools, element_action, respond_to_dialog, file_dialog), the captures (also for a window that restoreIfMinimized would show), get_desktop_state (its window, its focus and its screenshot), every window change, close and activation (WindowService.EvaluateTargetPolicy now uses EvaluateFailClosed, so focus_window, the window tools and their batches, close_window, close_windows_batch, close_application and apply_layout refuse it, and save_layout leaves out its title) and the launch tools' placement and reuseExisting; with WAM_DENY_PROCESSES=none and no allow list it is allowed. (4) allowHostTarget never reaches a window of the server's own process, or a window one owns (TargetIsAgentHost): the server shows no window of its own, so such a window is a prompt Windows showed for it, such as the security warning of a downloaded file, which ShellExecuteEx shows in the calling process when launch_application opens the file. Not covered: the browser reading tools (get_browser_url, get_active_browser_url, get_browser_content), take_screenshot and capture_region, which black nothing out, and a security prompt that runs inside a shared process, such as Explorer's "Open File - Security Warning" when File Explorer opens a downloaded file: an image-name deny list cannot name explorer.exe without denying File Explorer. BACKLOG.md lists the shared-process prompts under the M3 follow-ups, with two optional deny entries that need a real-desktop check first (Microsoft.AAD.BrokerPlugin, SecurityHealthHost).

*Sources: r1:crit-missing:target-policy*

## M2 Honest results and a stable MCP surface

Every tool either does what it reports or says precisely why not: typed parameters, real post-state, verified close, handoff-aware launch and faithful layouts. The MCP surface (contract tests, generated docs, annotations, stable SDK, curated toolsets, server instructions) is settled before M3-M5 add about 30 tools. Scroll and clipboard, the two primitives agents miss first, are pulled forward.

M2 is complete on the integration branch `m2-honest-results`: all 17 items (`m2-foundation`, `m2-tool-surface`, `m2-server-info-toolsets`, `m2-geometry`, `m2-layouts`, `m2-close-verify`, `m2-launch`, `m2-capture-health`, `m2-scroll-clipboard`, `m2-audit-log` and `m2-selectors`), the wave-B review follow-ups (`m2-wave-b-followups`), the fixes from a final review of the integrated branch (`m2-fix-integration`, `m2-fix-privacy`), a docs pass (`m2-docs-pass`) and the fixes it found (`m2-last-fixes`). The server serves 54 tools (47 in 1.13.0) in eleven toolsets, all on by default. The safe suite (desktop tests skipped) has 4581 unit, contract and integration tests and 179 E2E harness tests; the gated fixture scenarios run only with the opt-in.

- M2 shipped in release 1.14.0 on 2026-10-11 (PR #4, release commit 9d5280d), which rebuilt bin/WindowsAppManagerMcp.exe (scripts/Set-Version.ps1, then scripts/Publish-Plugin.ps1). Until then the committed exe was the 1.13.0 build (47 tools: no window selectors, verified close, toolsets, scroll, clipboard, audit log or get_server_info), so the merge to main and the release went together. docs/TOOLS.md lists what a client sees change under "Changes for existing clients".
- The final review looked at the integrated branch through privacy, MCP-surface, integration and docs lenses. `m2-fix-privacy` closed its privacy and MCP-surface findings: input that pastes follows get_clipboard's rule (PasteNotAllowed), the agent's copy windows can no longer be kept open, one clipboard question at a time, get_clipboard audited, confirmation questions that drop format characters, `force` on a protected window confirmed by the user, launch_browser_with_debug destructive and refusing switch-like URLs and real profiles, WAM_EXCLUDE_TOOLS failing closed, scroll's bare foreground selector and the instructions' act rule. `m2-fix-integration` closed the integration findings: hidden and other-desktop windows, reused handles in close_application, the input tools' selector recheck inside the gate, kill_process held to the process the user confirmed and releasing the gate before its wait, apply_layout's activating restore and lost launches, the batch-launch handoff, the audit log's items and its background writer, and get_server_info's clipboard and URI scheme settings. The items below say what changed.
- Known limits the final review accepted, recorded under the items: a right click in a terminal outside the known classes is not recognised as a paste (M2.16); a batch launch's handoff rule can credit a failed launch with a later window of another item's instance (M2.6); close_application takes every shell-cloaked window as one on another virtual desktop (M2.5).
- Follow-ups after the release (listed under M2 in BACKLOG.md), done on M3's integration branch and shipping with M3 in 1.15.0. Done on `m3-force-confirm` (M2.10's follow-up note): on a client without elicitation, `force` on a protected window needs `confirm=true` too, in close_window and close_windows_batch (the user's decision of 2026-10-11). Done on `m3-m2-followups` (see each item's follow-up note): right-click paste detection for the VS Code and other Electron terminals, Alacritty and WezTerm, by process where the class is shared (M2.16); a real-handoff check (exit code 0) before a batch launch credits another item's window (M2.6); IVirtualDesktopManager to tell other-desktop windows from suspended or prelaunched frames (M2.5); the audit log's wait without blocking a thread-pool thread (M2.17); and the payload budgets re-measured with `visibleBounds` (M2.12).
- Moved on: wait_for_window and wait_for_element (M3.7, M3.8), launch-and-arrange (M3.13), CacheRequest and a hung-window precheck for UI Automation reads (M3.1), the browser tools' UI Automation deadline (M5.7), per-window integrity and elevation fields (M6.6), layout schema v2 with monitor identity and launch info (M6.8), run_steps honouring the toolsets (M6.2), and paste-mode typing (M4.4).

### M2.1 Honest error contract: isError, no null results, per-item batch errors

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** improve · **Branch:** `m2-tool-surface` (Done)

Results with Success=false go out with isError=false, null results arrive as empty content, and one bad batch item aborts the whole batch with an NRE.

**Design.** Add IToolOutcome{Success, Error, ErrorCode} and a helper ToolResults.Json(dto) that returns CallToolResult{TextContentBlock(JSON), IsError=!Success}; use it in every tool. get_layout and get_foreground_window return {found:false, errorCode:'NotFound'} instead of null. Batch items are validated individually, with InvalidArgument per item and no NRE at WindowManagerTools.cs:386. Argument errors throw McpException listing the allowed values. The M0.3 call filter gains timing and errorCode logging.

**As shipped (`m2-tool-surface`).** Refinement: instead of changing every tool to return a CallToolResult through ToolResults.Json, the outermost call filter (ToolErrorReporting) applies Infrastructure/ToolOutcome.WithIsError to every result: a JSON body whose top-level `success` is false goes out with isError true, unchanged. The tools keep their typed results (and their unit tests), and a new tool cannot forget the rule; a list or a batch summary has no top-level success and keeps isError unset, while each batch item carries its own success, error and errorCode. The call log now records such a result as `isError <errorCode>`. Already done before M2.1: the exception filter with timing and errorCode logging (M0.3), delete_layout's PresetNotFound (M0.6), and get_layout, which already returned a PresetNotFound tool error instead of null. get_foreground_window now returns a tool error with the new WindowErrorCode NoForegroundWindow (28) instead of null; a found window keeps its shape. Batch items are validated one by one: a null item, a missing or non-positive size, and an unknown or missing snap position or state fail that item with InvalidArguments, InvalidSnapPosition or InvalidState (the existing tool-error code InvalidArguments is reused rather than adding a near-identical InvalidArgument), and an explicitly null batch array fails the call with InvalidArguments; launch_applications_batch does the same for a null item or a null applications array (review fix). A value of the wrong JSON type in an item (a string handle) still fails the whole call with InvalidArguments, because the array binds as a whole; items as JsonElement would be needed to change that. Argument errors for fixed values are bound and reported as described under M2.2. The ratchet lists IsErrorPendingM21 and NullResultPendingM21 are gone: EveryToolContractTests now requires non-empty content and isError = !success from every tool.

*Sources: r1:R8, r2:R28, r1:MCP-14, r1:WC-7, r1:MCP-G10, r2:MSP-8*

### M2.2 Typed parameters and strict validation with real outcomes

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m2-tool-surface` (Done)

Unknown state, positioning, matchBy and monitorIndex values silently fall back to a different action that is then reported as success. Native failures carry no reason.

**Design.** Use C# enums with JsonStringEnumConverter so inputSchema lists the valid values: WindowStateRequest, MonitorPositioning, SnapPosition, LayoutMatchBy, KillMode, ImageFormat. This removes the fallbacks at WindowManagerTools.cs:121/358, WindowService.cs:274 and LayoutService.cs:293. Validate monitorIndex (InvalidMonitorIndex with the valid range); CalculateSnapBounds throws instead of falling back to the primary monitor at MonitorService.cs:113. Validate maxLength>0 and similar ranges. SetWindowState ignores ShowWindow's return value and reports success from the post-state (IsIconic/IsZoomed/IsWindowVisible polled for up to 300 ms). Mutating wrapper methods return (Ok, Win32Error) through Marshal.GetLastPInvokeError, mapping 5 to AccessDenied. Emit the unused MoveFailed, ResizeFailed, StateChangeFailed, FocusFailed and CloseFailed codes, and add NotResponding, Cloaked and AmbiguousWindow.

**As shipped (`m2-tool-surface`).** Typed parameters: WindowStateRequest (set_window_state and set_windows_state_batch items), MonitorPositioning, SnapPosition, LayoutMatchBy, CaptureFormat and CaptureOutputMode (the four captures) and DebugBrowser (launch_browser_with_debug). KillMode was not needed: kill_process takes a boolean forceKill. Refinement: the SDK's own string-enum converter lists the values but matches custom names case-sensitively, takes no aliases and accepts any number, so each enum is marked [ToolEnum(errorCode, noun)] with JsonStringEnumMemberName canonical names and [ToolValueAlias] aliases, and Infrastructure/ToolJson binds it with a strict converter (any case, '_', '-' and spaces ignored, numbers refused) and writes the values back into the schema, which a custom converter hides from the exporter. The tools are registered with WithServerTools<T>() (McpServerTool.Create with those serializer and schema options), which reproduces WithTools<T>()'s tools/list exactly otherwise. An unknown value fails before the tool runs with the parameter's own errorCode (InvalidState, InvalidPositioning, InvalidMatchBy added; InvalidSnapPosition, InvalidFormat, InvalidOutputMode, UnsupportedBrowser reused) and a message listing the allowed values; documented aliases stay accepted and are listed in the schema (minimized, maximized, normal, jpg), undocumented spellings (top_left, Left-Half, full) are accepted but not listed, and an explicit JSON null for an optional parameter binds to its default ([ToolEnum(NullMeans = ...)]), as the string parameters took null. Batch item fields stay strings marked [ToolValues(typeof(T))] so one bad item fails alone, and still list their values. Validation: monitorIndex is checked by the window service (InvalidMonitorIndex with the valid range) and CalculateSnapBounds throws instead of using the primary monitor; width/height below 1, get_browser_content maxLength below 1 and a debug port outside 1-65535 are InvalidArguments. LayoutService's own monitor fallback for saved placements is left to M2.8. Outcomes: IWindowService's changes return WindowChangeResult (Success, ErrorCode, Error); INativeWindowWrapper.SetWindowPos returns Win32CallResult, so 5 is AccessDenied, 1400 InvalidHandle and anything else MoveFailed or ResizeFailed with the Win32 message; SetWindowState ignores ShowWindow's return value and succeeds only when IsIconic/IsZoomed/IsWindowVisible show the requested state within 300 ms (restore follows the Windows Restore command: a window minimized from maximized returns to maximized), else StateChangeFailed; the batches report NotResponding and the other codes per item and every item's newBounds/newState. move_window_to_monitor 'maximize' restores, moves the window into the target work area, maximizes it and verifies the monitor (MoveFailed otherwise); M2.3 still owns the rest of the geometry work. The INP-13 input mapping: a send_keys syntax error (an unclosed brace, SendKeysParser's KeySyntaxException) is InvalidKeySpecification instead of UnknownKey, and a failed SetCursorPos is the new InputErrorCode CursorPositionFailed (41) instead of SendInputFailed. Not done here: NotResponding and Cloaked already existed (M1.3); AmbiguousWindow and HandleMismatch-for-selectors belong to M2.4 (HandleMismatch is added by M2.10 for confirmed closes); InvalidCoordinates stays unused.

*Sources: r1:R34, r2:R28, r1:WC-7, r1:WC-8, r1:WC-9, r1:MCP-6, r1:MCP-9, r1:PAL-15, r2:WC-7, r2:MSP-8, r2:MSP-9, r2:INP-13, r2:PAL-10*

### M2.3 Correct, verified and hang-proof geometry operations

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** L · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m2-geometry` (Done; final-review fixes on `m2-fix-integration`)

Fixes several placement defects: maximize on the wrong monitor, moving or snapping a maximized window, minimized windows shrinking to 160x28, gaps from the invisible DWM frame, double DPI scaling, and calls that hang on 'Not Responding' windows. Every geometry result carries the observed state.

**Design.** PrepareForGeometryChange: if the window IsIconic or IsZoomed, call SetWindowPlacement(SW_SHOWNORMAL, rcNormalPosition = target rect in workspace coordinates), or SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE/SW_RESTORE at most twice for restore-to-maximized; measure only afterwards. MoveWindow uses SWP_NOSIZE and ResizeWindow SWP_NOMOVE, and neither ever activates; bringToFront is explicit. MoveWindowToMonitor takes the size from rcNormalPosition and clamps it to the target work area. It then does a two-phase move: SWP_NOSIZE onto the target monitor, settle, and re-read GetWindowRect after the app has applied WM_DPICHANGED, then set the final size. Never pre-scale by the DPI ratio. 'maximize' happens only after the move, and MonitorFromWindow==target is verified. Place by the visible frame: inflate the rect by the margins between GetWindowRect and DWMWA_EXTENDED_FRAME_BOUNDS. SnapMath.Calculate(workArea, position, frameInsets) is a pure function with edges at X+W*k/n so parts tile exactly; it sits behind INativeMonitorWrapper, and the test copy CalculateSnapBoundsInternal is deleted. Results report Bounds (visible frame), OuterBounds and State. Hang safety: IsHungAppWindow returns NotResponding; otherwise use SWP_ASYNCWINDOWPOS/ShowWindowAsync and poll up to timeoutMs. The tools become async and batches use Task.Delay. A new settleMs uses SettleAndRead, which waits for two equal extended-frame reads.

**As shipped (`m2-geometry`).** Restore before placing: every placement (move, resize, bounds, snap, move to a monitor, the batches, and apply_layout through SetWindowBounds) restores a minimized or maximized window with ShowWindow(SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE), at most twice for a window minimized from maximized, waits up to 300 ms for each step, and measures only afterwards; a window that stays minimized or maximized fails with MoveFailed or ResizeFailed and nothing is placed. Refinement: SetWindowPlacement is not used. Its rcNormalPosition is in workspace coordinates of whichever monitor Windows picks, which is ambiguous for a cross-monitor target, while SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE gives the same non-activating restore and the placement then uses screen coordinates; the cost is a restore animation before the move. move_window uses SWP_NOSIZE and resize_window SWP_NOMOVE, and no placement activates (bringToFront only raises with SWP_NOACTIVATE); the only activating case left is move_window_to_monitor's 'maximize' (SW_MAXIMIZE has no non-activating form; documented). Visible frame: FrameInsets.Between(GetWindowRect, DWMWA_EXTENDED_FRAME_BOUNDS), clamped at 0 per side and ignored above 64 px; move_window, resize_window, set_window_bounds (IWindowService.SetWindowFrame), snap_window, move_window_to_monitor and the bounds and snap batches place the visible frame. IWindowService.SetWindowBounds keeps the outer rect (BoundsFrame.Outer) because LayoutService saves and applies GetWindowRect; a layout that switched only one side would drift by the borders on every save and apply. DPI: a placement whose first SetWindowPos changed the window's monitor scale waits for two equal frame reads (at most 500 ms) and places the same target again, measured anew (FancyZones); move keeps the size the application chose. move_window_to_monitor measures the restored window, and across a scale change moves it first with SWP_NOSIZE (centred on the target work area, so that the target holds most of it), settles, then clamps the size the application chose to the target work area and positions it; it never pre-scales. It verifies MonitorFromWindow (GetMonitorIndex) == target for every positioning, MoveFailed otherwise; 'maximize' moves first, then maximizes, and verifies. Size source: the restored GetWindowRect rather than rcNormalPosition; after the restore they are the same size, and for an Aero-snapped window the current size is what the user sees. SnapMath.Calculate(workArea, position) is a pure function with edges at X + W*k/n (thirds of 2560 are 853, 853, 854), used by MonitorService.CalculateSnapBounds (already behind INativeMonitorWrapper since M0); the insets are added by WindowService for every placement, not by SnapMath. The test copy CalculateSnapBoundsInternal is deleted; SnapMathTests cover odd sizes, negative origins and exact tiling. Results: WindowOperationResult and WindowResultItem replace newBounds/newState with bounds (visible frame, the window rect when DWM cannot report it), outerBounds, state and monitorIndex, on every geometry tool, set_window_state and the batch items, also when the change failed; a minimized window has no bounds. WindowInfo gains VisibleBounds (read in every listing; one DwmGetWindowAttribute per window); find_windows still reports the outer rect as bounds (left for M2.4's get_window_info). settleMs (0-5000, default 0) on the six single-window tools waits for two equal frame reads 20 ms apart (IWindowService.WaitForSettledFrame). Hang safety: WindowCallRunner is kept (IsHungAppWindow refusal, 5 s deadline per call, abandoned-call limits), so SWP_ASYNCWINDOWPOS and ShowWindowAsync are still not used: an asynchronous SetWindowPos returns before the change and without its Win32 error, so AccessDenied could not be reported, while the runner already bounds the wait. The settle waits only read (GetWindowRect, DwmGetWindowAttribute) and never block on the window's thread. The single tools stay synchronous; the batches were already async with Task.Delay. No new native wrapper members or error codes.

**Final review (`m2-fix-integration`).** Two placements still activated or showed windows they should not have. apply_layout restored a maximized window with SetWindowState(Restore) before SetWindowBounds, and SW_RESTORE activates; it now leaves that to SetWindowBounds, which restores with SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE, so the claim above (no placement activates) now holds for apply_layout too. And SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE showed a hidden window that was minimized or maximized (a tray app hides its window after minimizing it): such a placement now fails with MoveFailed or ResizeFailed, sends nothing and leaves the window hidden, as the capture restore refuses to show it (M2.9). move_window_to_monitor's 'maximize' sends SW_MAXIMIZE (which activates) only once the window is on the target monitor; an app that keeps or restores its own position is left unmaximized on its old monitor, and the call fails with MoveFailed. find_windows, get_foreground_window and get_window_info 'standard' report the visible frame as `visibleBounds` beside `bounds` (the window rect, unchanged), so an agent can pass a listed window's frame to set_window_bounds; the placement descriptions point at it.

*Sources: r1:R33, r1:R35, r2:R27, r2:R26, r2:crit:R27, r1:R5, r1:WC-2, r1:WC-3, r1:WC-6, r1:WC-10, r1:PAL-11, r1:SCR-9, r1:WC-missed-1, r1:WC-missed-3, r2:WC-2, r2:WC-4, r2:WC-5, r2:WC-6, r2:WC-9, r2:WC-G6, r2:MSP-8, r2:WC-missed-minimized-shrink, r2:WC-missed-restore-to-maximized, r2:MSP-missed-maximized-not-restored, r2:MSP-missed-ShowWindow-return*

### M2.4 Window selectors, ambiguity errors and get_window_info

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** improve · **Branch:** `m2-selectors` (Done; final-review fixes on `m2-fix-integration`)

Every tool needs a raw HWND, matching is substring-only, and layouts silently pick windows[0]. A shared selector resolves to exactly one window or returns an explicit error.

**Design.** WindowSelector/WindowQuery: Handle, TitleContains, TitleEquals, TitleRegex (100 ms timeout), ProcessName (exact and case-insensitive; contains is opt-in), ProcessId, ClassName, Foreground, Index, IncludeCloaked, IncludeUntitled and Limit. It is shared by FindWindows, LayoutService and every tool. WindowService.ResolveWindow returns one handle, NoMatch, or AmbiguousMatch with up to 5 candidates, and never windows[0]. Title matching uses the browser-aware normalizer from M0.7 (strips Unicode Cf characters such as U+200B and ' and N more pages'). An optional window object is added to move, resize, bounds, snap, state, focus, close, move_to_monitor, batch items, captures, get_ui_elements, input and browser tools; handle is kept for compatibility. A stale-handle guard returns HandleMismatch when the expected PID or title no longer matches. New get_window_info(window, detail). find_windows gains sort='zorder' and limit.

**As shipped (`m2-selectors`).** Models/WindowSelector is the `window` argument: Handle, TitleContains, TitleEquals, TitleRegex, ProcessName (+ ProcessNameContains), ProcessId, ClassName, Foreground, Index, IncludeCloaked and IncludeUntitled, with JsonUnmappedMemberHandling.Disallow, so an unknown or misspelled field is an InvalidArguments tool error instead of a wider match. WindowQuery (find_windows) gains TitleEquals, TitleRegex, ProcessNameContains, IncludeUntitled, Sort (WindowSort zorder|title|process, a strict ToolEnum; title and process sorts are stable, so z-order breaks ties) and Limit. Refinement: Limit is on the query only; a selector names one window, so `index` picks one of several in z-order instead. Title rule: Services/WindowTitles holds M0.7's normalizer (Unicode Cf characters such as U+200B removed, whitespace runs collapsed), moved out of CdpTargetMatcher, which now calls it, together with Edge's " and N more pages" pattern; Contains, AreEqual and Matches compare the title with and without that segment, so `titleEquals: "Inbox - Personal - Microsoft Edge"` finds the window titled "Inbox and 2 more pages - Personal - Microsoft Edge". Profile and browser suffixes are not stripped: titleContains and titleRegex do not need it, and titleEquals compares the whole title the user sees. CompileTitleRegex is IgnoreCase and CultureInvariant with a 100 ms match timeout; an invalid pattern, or one that times out on a title, is InvalidArguments. Process rule: Services/WindowMatching.ProcessNameMatches (app process or UWP host; the whole name without .exe, part of it only when asked) is shared by FindWindows, the selectors and LayoutService. Behaviour change, documented under "Changes for existing clients": find_windows' processName is the whole name now (`Code` no longer matches `Code - Insiders`; processNameContains brings back the old match). LayoutService uses the shared title and process rules (a captured preset's process name stays exact and a hand-written one a part, as M2.8 shipped, and `msedge.exe` now matches too), but not ResolveWindow: a placement picks the best of several candidates by score, one to one, which is the opposite of refusing ambiguity, and M2.8 already reports a placement it cannot match. IWindowService.ResolveWindow returns WindowResolution: InvalidArguments for an empty selector, a handle or processId below 1, a negative index, index or foreground beside a handle, or foreground with index; a handle is read unfiltered (InvalidHandle when it is no window) and checked against the other filters, each difference named, HandleMismatch when one fails (the stale-handle guard: `{"handle": h, "processId": p}`); foreground is GetForegroundWindow checked the same way (WindowNotFound when it does not match); otherwise FindWindows(selector.ToQuery()) (visible windows, minimized included, the listing filter unless className): one match is found, none is WindowNotFound with hints (whole process name, includeCloaked, includeUntitled), several are AmbiguousWindow with up to 5 candidates in z-order, and index picks a match (WindowNotFound with the candidates past the end). It never takes windows[0], and only reads. Error codes: WindowErrorCode WindowNotFound (35) and AmbiguousWindow (36) are appended; HandleMismatch (32, M2.10) is reused. Refinement: AmbiguousWindow rather than AmbiguousMatch, naming what is ambiguous as CdpTargetAmbiguous does, and WindowNotFound rather than NoMatch, the name LayoutErrorCode and UIElementErrorCode already use. The candidates (WindowCandidateDto: handle, title, processName, processId, className) travel on ToolErrorException into ToolErrorResultDto.candidates, and on WindowResultItem.Candidates in the batches; both are left out otherwise. Tools/WindowTargeting turns `handle` or `window` into one handle (exactly one of them, else InvalidArguments; a selector with only a handle is that handle, unresolved, so a dead handle is reported as before). `window` is on move_window, resize_window, set_window_bounds, set_window_state, focus_window, snap_window, move_window_to_monitor, close_window, close_application, get_owned_windows, get_window_info, capture_window, capture_with_elements, get_ui_elements, get_browser_url, get_browser_content and the seven input tools (beside windowHandle), and on the items of the four window batches, whose Handle became optional; `handle` keeps working but is no longer required in the schema. get_active_browser_url is unchanged (it reads the foreground browser). Gate: the seven single-window changes and the three placement batches moved from WindowChangingTools to SelfGatedTools: they refuse a pause (PausedByUser) before resolving, resolve outside the gate, then take it with InputGateToolFilter.EnterAsync (InputBusy and PausedByUser as before) and run the precheck (IsWindow, target policy) and the change inside it; the batches resolve every item first and check the policy inside. The close tools resolve before their question and their gate, the input tools in the tool before InputSimulationService takes the gate, and the read tools before their work. Resolution only changes where the handle comes from: the resolved window goes through the window service's checks, the close guards, the input guard and its host rule (allowHostTarget is passed through unchanged) and the capture policy exactly as a handle does. Results: WindowOperationResult and CloseWindowResult gain `handle`, the window acted on. Audit: AuditRecord.WindowHandles also reads `window.handle` (top level and in close-batch items), and the target falls back to the result's `handle` when the arguments named none, so a selector call records the window it resolved to (its title and process are not read before the call; input calls still record receivingWindow). New read-only tool get_window_info(handle or window, detail compact|standard|full) in the windows toolset: no listing for a handle; full adds visibleBounds, clientBounds (INativeHitTestWrapper.GetClientAreaOnScreen), isForeground, zOrder (the EnumWindows index among all top-level windows, null for a child window), isTopmost (WS_EX_TOPMOST), isEnabled (WS_DISABLED), isResponding (IsHungAppWindow) and dpi (GetDpiForWindow), none of which waits on the window's thread. Not done: restoreBounds, isElevated (the input guard's TargetElevated covers it), isOnCurrentDesktop (M7), AltTabOnly, excludeTitle and a `fields` projection (the detail levels keep the payload small: 86 tokens for full). Docs: a Window selectors section in TOOLS.md (fields, title rule, outcomes, how to handle AmbiguousWindow, guards), get_window_info, error rows and reference entries; SKILL.md prefers a selector to a copied handle and says what to do after AmbiguousWindow, WindowNotFound and HandleMismatch; the server instructions gain a selector example and AmbiguousWindow (19 lines). Tests: WindowServiceSelectorTests and WindowTitlesTests over a fake desktop (FakeWindowDesktop), WindowSelectorToolTests (resolution before the gate and the change inside it, pause before resolving, policy and close guards on the resolved window, batch items failing alone, input targets with allowHostTarget), MCP contract tests for the selector's JSON binding, an unknown field, the candidates of AmbiguousWindow and find_windows' new parameters, an audit test, a layout test for Edge titles, and the regenerated tools/list snapshot (54 tools) and docs. After review: a bare `{"foreground": true}` names no window the caller chose, so the input tools pass it as no windowHandle (DestructiveChordNeedsTarget and the host rule apply as without one) and the close tools refuse it (InvalidArguments); allowHostTarget beside a selector without `handle` is InvalidArguments, so the host rule is lifted only for a window named by its handle. The window tools and close tools check a resolved selector again inside the gate, just before they act (WindowTargeting.Recheck over WindowMatching.Mismatches, now shared with ResolveWindow): a window that no longer matches its filters after the gate wait is HandleMismatch, per item in the batches; the input tools resolve right before InputSimulationService takes the gate, and (since the M2 integration review) the input guard checks the selector again inside the gate, before the first batch (InputTarget.SelectorRecheck; InputErrorCode.HandleMismatch, nothing sent). A titleRegex timeout is InvalidArguments on every path (the handle and foreground checks no longer report it as a mismatch). A filter whose text is blank once normalized (" ", U+200B, a processName of only `.exe`) is InvalidArguments; an empty string is a filter left out. AmbiguousWindow candidates carry isAgentHost, and its message no longer offers index as a remedy (z-order can change before the next call). get_window_info describes zOrder as the position among all top-level windows, not window.index.

**Final review (`m2-fix-integration`).** The input tools resolved a selector before waiting up to 30 s for the gate and never checked it again: the resolved target now carries a recheck (InputTarget.SelectorRecheck) that the input guard runs inside the gate, before the first batch, and a window that no longer matches is refused with the new InputErrorCode.HandleMismatch (43), with nothing sent. close_window refuses a pause (PausedByUser) before it resolves its selector, as the other window tools do. The audit log names a selector's window by the title and process the tool noted when it resolved it (M2.17). A titleRegex that timed out during the input recheck was at first reported as HandleMismatch with the regex message, where the window tools report InvalidArguments; `m2-last-fixes` keeps the recheck's own code (InputTarget.SelectorRecheck returns a SelectorRefusal with the window tools' errorCode), reported as the new InputErrorCode.InvalidArguments (45).

*Sources: r1:R36, r2:R29, r1:crit:R61, r1:WC-G2, r1:WC-G4, r1:BRW-G8, r1:SCR-G8, r2:WC-G2, r2:WC-G3, r2:WC-G5*

### M2.5 Verified close, owned-dialog discovery and close_application

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** improve · **Branch:** `m2-close-verify` (Done; final-review fixes on `m2-fix-integration` and `m2-fix-privacy`)

close_window cannot tell 'closed' from 'still open' from 'blocked by a Save changes? prompt'. There is no graceful app-level close.

**Design.** close_window and close_windows_batch gain waitMs (default 2000, max 30000). They post WM_CLOSE and poll IsWindow. If the window is still open, find the blocker: GW_ENABLEDPOPUP != h, owned #32770 windows (EnumWindows plus GW_OWNER), or a modal UIA dialog (WindowPattern.IsModal). Status is closed, stillOpen or promptShown, with prompt{handle, title, className, text, buttons}. New get_owned_windows(window). New close_application(window|processId|processName, timeoutMs) sends WM_CLOSE only to the Alt-Tab set, never to Default IME, MSCTFIME, GDI+ hook or hidden tray windows. Packaged apps are closed through the ApplicationFrameWindow that hosts their CoreWindow, with UIA WindowPattern.Close as the fallback. It never forces, and it reports remainingWindows and promptDetected.

**As shipped (`m2-close-verify`).** close_window and close_windows_batch take waitMs (default 2000, 0-30000, outside it InvalidArguments) and report `status` closed, hidden, stillOpen, promptShown (with `prompt` {handle, title, className, text, buttons, kind}) or requested (waitMs 0, the old behaviour); success is true for closed, hidden and requested, and the new WindowErrorCode StillOpen (33) and PromptShown (34) mark the rest, so a window behind a save prompt is now isError. Refinements over the design: (1) the close message is sent inside the input gate, but the wait runs outside it, so close_window moved from WindowChangingTools to the self-gated tools (ThrowIfPaused, EnterAsync only around the PostMessage) and a 30 s wait cannot block every input call; the batch waits for all its windows at once after the last message. (2) A `hidden` status: tray apps hide their window on WM_CLOSE, which is neither closed nor stillOpen. (3) GW_ENABLEDPOPUP != h counts only while the window is disabled (WS_DISABLED), and an owned #32770 only while the window or the dialog's owner is disabled, because GW_ENABLEDPOPUP also returns a non-modal Find dialog or palette of an enabled window; the owned search follows GW_OWNER through any number of owners. (4) A Win32 prompt must be seen in two consecutive checks (200 ms apart) and then ends the wait early; the UI Automation search for an in-window modal dialog (a Window element with WindowPattern.IsModal, kind inWindowDialog) runs once, when the wait ends, on the UiaWorker with a 1.5 s deadline. Text and buttons come from the dialog's Static and Button controls (accelerator & removed), and through UI Automation when they have none (task dialogs); the title bar's Close button is left out; text is capped at 1000 characters and 10 buttons. New read-only get_owned_windows(handle, includeHidden) lists a window's owned windows in z-order with isDialog and isBlocking (handle, not M2.4's window selector, which does not exist yet). New close_application(handle | processId | processName, timeoutMs default 5000, confirm) in the process toolset: it closes only the Alt-Tab set of the target processes (IWindowService.GetApplicationWindows: visible, uncloaked, unowned or WS_EX_APPWINDOW, no tool windows, no shell, no IME/MSCTFIME UI/GDI+ Hook Window Class, not the server's own), resolves a UWP frame to its app's process (never ApplicationFrameHost's), refuses the whole app before anything is asked or sent when a window would be refused by the close guards (ProtectedWindow, with no force) or the target policy, or when it has no such window (NoClosableWindow), asks the user through elicitation above 3 windows like close_windows_batch (confirm otherwise) and pins each confirmed window to its process (HandleMismatch), never forces, falls back to WindowPattern.Close (IUIAutomationWrapper.CloseWindow, an E2E WindowAction) only for an ApplicationFrameWindow whose WM_CLOSE Windows refused with CloseFailed, and reports status, per-window `windows`, remainingWindows, promptDetected, prompt and, once every window is gone, exited and runningProcessIds (up to 2 s within timeoutMs). IUiaTree gained ReadIsModal (a property read). After review: `hidden` needs a window that was shown before the close (IWindowCloseVerifier.ShownWindows is read before the message is sent; an already hidden or cloaked window that stays is stillOpen); the windows still open when the wait ends are looked at concurrently, so a call ends about one UIA deadline after the wait; every PromptShown text and description tells the agent not to answer the prompt itself but to ask the user (not every client loads the skill); a full message queue (ERROR_NOT_ENOUGH_QUOTA) is NotResponding, not CloseFailed, so the UIA fallback never runs against a hung app, and IUIAutomationWrapper.CloseWindow checks the deadline right before WindowPattern.Close (a call that timed out counts as sent and is waited for); close_application leaves out WS_EX_NOACTIVATE windows without WS_EX_APPWINDOW, takes only File Explorer windows (CabinetWClass) from the shell's process, closes a UWP frame whose app cannot be told only by its own handle and alone (naming ApplicationFrameHost is NoClosableWindow), keeps going when one window's close throws, and treats timeoutMs 0 like waitMs 0 (status requested); get_owned_windows marks the same blocking dialog the close tools report. Not done: wait_for_process_exit (r2) as a tool; cloaked windows on other virtual desktops are not closed (reported since the final review, below); the E2E fixture test of the prompt is gated and was not run here.

**Final review (`m2-fix-integration`, `m2-fix-privacy`).** close_application reported `closed` while the app still had windows on another virtual desktop, and waited for processes that could not exit. It now lists them as `otherDesktopWindows` (IWindowService.GetApplicationWindowsOnOtherDesktops: the Alt+Tab set among windows the shell cloaks, DWM_CLOAKED_SHELL), leaves them open, does not wait for their processes and reports those in `runningProcessIds`; `status: "closed"` covers the current desktop only, and NoClosableWindow says the app may live only on another desktop. Known limit: the shell also cloaks some suspended or prelaunched Store app frames, which are then reported as on another desktop (a misreport; nothing more is closed). close_application checks inside the gate that each listed window still belongs to its process whether or not the user was asked (HandleMismatch in its item), not only after a question. close_windows_batch reports a window that got no close message (refused, or the send failed) as `notSent`, as close_application does. kill_process follows the close tools' gate pattern since the final review: the close request or the termination is sent inside the gate and the wait for the exit runs outside it (M2.10). `force` on a protected window is confirmed by the user when the client can ask (M2.10).

**Follow-up (`m3-m2-followups`, M2.F4).** The shell cloaks suspended and prelaunched Store frames as it cloaks windows on other desktops, so they showed up in `otherDesktopWindows` (a misreport; nothing extra was closed). GetApplicationWindowsOnOtherDesktops now asks the shell's IVirtualDesktopManager about the shell-cloaked candidates in one call (new INativeVirtualDesktopWrapper.GetPlacements: IsWindowOnCurrentVirtualDesktop, then GetWindowDesktopId for a window not on the current desktop; TYPE_E_ELEMENTNOTFOUND or GUID_NULL means no desktop). A window on no desktop or on the current one is left out; one the manager cannot place is kept, so a window with unsaved work is never dropped from the report. NativeVirtualDesktopWrapper creates the manager on an MTA thread of its own per call and waits at most 1 s (the manager answers from explorer.exe); while an abandoned call is still blocked, every call answers Unknown at once. Its COM interface declares only the two reads (MoveWindowToDesktop stays undeclared), which NativeCallGuardTests checks along with keeping [ComImport] interfaces in the Native namespace; the E2E catalog classifies GetPlacements as Read. Not validated on a real desktop with a prelaunched frame (no gated scenario was added: a fixture cannot make Windows prelaunch a Store app).

*Sources: r1:R37, r2:R10, r2:crit:R9, r1:WC-G6, r1:PAL-G7, r2:WC-11, r2:WC-G10, r2:PAL-G6*

### M2.6 Handoff-aware launch-to-window resolution

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m2-launch` (Done; final-review fix on `m2-fix-integration`)

Launches that hand off to another process return no window after the full timeout. This covers browsers, calc to CalculatorApp, code, explorer and Firefox. Single-instance and tabbed apps (Win11 Notepad, Terminal, VS Code, Explorer tabs) open no new window at all. Return the window that actually appeared, whichever process owns it.

**Design.** IWindowWaitService.WaitForNewWindowAsync(baseline, ownerPredicate, timeoutMs (max 60000), stableMs=300, ct). The baseline records top-level HWNDs, their titles and the foreground window before Start. It polls every 100 ms, running cheap filters first. A candidate must survive stableMs, which skips splash screens, and then a bounded WaitForInputIdle runs. The owner predicate matches the launched PID or a descendant (Toolhelp walk with creation-time checks), the same image path (QueryFullProcessImageNameW), a handoff map (wt to WindowsTerminal, code to Code, calc to CalculatorApp via the CoreWindow mapping), or a caller hint expectWindow{processName?, titleContains?}. If no new window appears, an owner-matching existing window that became foreground or changed its title after Start returns reusedExistingWindow or openedInTab. A null Process.Start under ShellExecute counts as a handoff, not ProcessStartFailed. Process.Start runs in Task.Run with a timeout so a UAC prompt returns awaitingUserConsent instead of blocking the call. LaunchResult gains launchStatus (windowObserved|reusedExistingWindow|openedInTab|possibleHandoff|exitedWithoutWindow|timedOut|awaitingUserConsent), window (a full WindowInfoDto with the real owner PID) and launcherExited. Batch launches start every process first, then wait concurrently against one deadline, with a per-item ErrorCode. Thread.Sleep loops become Task.Delay(ct).

**As shipped (`m2-launch`).** New IWindowWaitService/WindowWaitService: TakeBaseline (top-level handles with titles, and the foreground window) and WaitForNewWindowAsync(baseline, isOwner, WindowWaitOptions{TimeoutMs clamped to 0-60000, StableMs 300, ReuseSettleMs 1000, InputIdleMs 1000, DocumentHints, TryClaim}, ct), polling every 100 ms with Task.Delay on an injectable TimeProvider/delay. Cheap checks first: only windows new against the baseline go through IWindowService.IsUserFacingAppWindow (visible, titled, uncloaked, not tool or shell, not the server's), then the owner check on the app process (UwpFrames.AppProcessId, so a UWP frame is matched by its CoreWindow process; HostProcessId is the frame host). A candidate must survive StableMs; unowned windows go before owned ones, and a dialog whose owner is a new window still settling waits for it (refinement: a launch that shows its main window right after a dialog returned the dialog). Then a WaitForInputIdle bounded by InputIdleMs and the time left (INativeProcessWrapper.WaitForInputIdle). LaunchWindowOwner is the owner predicate: the started PID; a process whose parent links lead to it, each started after the launch (Toolhelp list via the new INativeProcessWrapper.GetProcessList plus start times, so it works after the launcher exited and refuses reused PIDs); the same image path (new GetProcessImagePath, QueryFullProcessImageNameW); the program's own name or a handoff-map name (calc to CalculatorApp/Calculator, wt, cmd, powershell, pwsh and wsl to WindowsTerminal, code to Code, ms-settings to SystemSettings, and, refinement, http/https to the common browsers); or the caller's expectWindow {processName?, titleContains?}, every field set must match, the process name also against a UWP window's frame host. Reuse, refined against the critic's 'foreground or title changed' rule: any title change counted would let an existing browser window whose tab title changes on its own pass for the launch, so an existing owner window counts only when it came to the foreground after the baseline (reusedExistingWindow, or openedInTab when its title changed) or when its title changed to contain a document hint (file or folder names and host names from the arguments; openedInTab), only while no new window is pending, and only after holding ReuseSettleMs, so a new window that appears meanwhile wins. In a batch, new windows are claimed (one per launch); reused windows are not, since two files can open as two tabs of one window. Review fix: a first-come claim let a wait that polled first take the window of another item's started process when both launched the same program (they match each other's windows by name and path), so two Notepad documents could come back swapped; a window whose process another item started (or its descendant, LaunchWindowOwner.StartedProcess) is now left to that item, while windows neither started (a running instance both hand off to) are still shared through the claim. Batch progress is counted and reported under one lock, so concurrent waits report increasing values. ProcessService: the start runs on a dedicated thread (TaskCreationOptions.LongRunning, not the thread pool, which a long UAC wait would hold and whose starvation could fake one) and is awaited at most StartTimeout (5 s): longer is awaitingUserConsent (success, no processId; the late handle is disposed and a late failure observed), Win32 error 1223 is the new LaunchErrorCode ElevationCancelled (14). Review fix: ShellExecuteEx also blocks on SmartScreen, the Mark-of-the-Web security warning and the Open with dialog (on the normal desktop), so the warning names a consent or security prompt, tells the agent not to answer it itself, and says that a decline after the 5 s is not reported; ElevationCancelled's message names a declined prompt, most likely UAC. Follow-up for the policy owner: smartscreen.exe is not on the default deny list (consent and CredentialUIBroker are). Without IWindowWaitService, ProcessService now creates its fallback WindowWaitService (and NativeWindowWrapper) in the constructor, so the E2E reference walk sees it. A null Process.Start is possibleHandoff, a success without processId (ProcessStartFailed is no longer returned; the member stays). LaunchResult gains Status (LaunchStatus: started, windowObserved, reusedExistingWindow, openedInTab, possibleHandoff, exitedWithoutWindow, timedOut, awaitingUserConsent), Window (WindowInfo) and LauncherExited; WindowHandle stays (it is Window.Handle), so LayoutService's launchMissing and launch_browser_with_debug get the handed-off window without changes. When the wait finds nothing, a visible main window of the started process still counts (an untitled main window, which the listing predicate skips). The statuses without a window carry a warning saying to look with find_windows or pass expectWindow and never to launch again. IProcessService gains LaunchApplicationAsync(LaunchRequest, waitForWindow, waitTimeoutMs, ct) (the old overload forwards to it), and LaunchApplicationsBatchAsync takes LaunchRequests: every item is planned and started in order, then all windows are awaited with Task.WhenAll against one deadline (waitTimeoutMs after the last start), progress reports 'i of n' as each item is done. The tools: launch_application gains expectWindow (LaunchWindowHint), batch items too; LaunchResultDto and LaunchResultItem gain launchStatus, window (WindowInfoDto via WindowFinderTools.ToDto) and launcherExited. The tool keeps its 30 s cap (it holds the input gate). Not done here: the wait_for_window tool (M3.7), launch-and-arrange (M3.13), AUMID activation for a real PID (M6.5), the elevation fields (M6.6), runAsAdmin, the BrowserDetectionService suffix heuristics, and the default browser read from the registry (the browser list is a heuristic; expectWindow covers other browsers).

**Final review (`m2-fix-integration`).** The review fix above left a window of a process another item started to that item only, so the second of two `code` or `wt` launches in one batch (whose launcher hands the request to the instance the first one started, then exits) always reported exitedWithoutWindow or timedOut. Such a window now counts for this launch once the other launch has its window and this launch's own process is gone (or it started none); the shared claim still gives each window to one launch. Known limit: the rule does not check that the launcher's exit was a handoff, so a launcher that fails at once (bad arguments, a crash) can be credited with a later window of the other item's instance (a second document or a what's-new window of the same program), as windowObserved where it used to be exitedWithoutWindow.

**Follow-up (`m3-m2-followups`, M2.F3).** The handoff credit above took any exited launcher as one that handed off, so a launcher that failed at once could be credited with a later window of another item's instance (windowObserved instead of exitedWithoutWindow). A batch item's handoff now needs proof: its launcher exited with code 0 (new IProcessHandle.ExitCode, null while running or unreadable, never throws; classified Read in the E2E catalog), or, when the code cannot be read, the window was not seen while the launcher still ran (ProcessService.LauncherHandoff records the candidate windows it is asked about while the launcher runs). A non-zero exit code never hands off, even for a window that appears after the exit. A launch that started no process is unchanged (Windows handed it over). The M3 review checked the concern that a Chromium launcher exits with a non-zero code (24) after a successful handoff: on Windows it exits with 0 on both of Chromium's handoff paths (the fast notify before chrome.dll loads, and the process singleton, whose result code ChromeMain maps to 0), so a browser item that hands off is credited.

**Follow-up (`m3-fix-launch`, M3 review).** The launch wait's reused-window signal follows M3.13's title rules (LaunchTitles, shared with reuseExisting): a window that was already open counts as the launch's (openedInTab) only when its title changed to show the launched file or folder as a whole name, after no other path than the launched one, and did not show it before the start. A title that only came to contain the name inside another word, or after another path, no longer counts, and neither does a web address's host in a page title, which the page sets (expectWindow.titleContains does); such a launch can now end possibleHandoff, exitedWithoutWindow or timedOut instead. This matters more since M3.13's placement moves and focuses the window a launch returns.

*Sources: r1:R48, r2:R37, r1:crit:R48, r1:crit-missing:single-instance-launch, r1:R52, r1:PAL-1, r1:BRW-3, r1:PAL-G1, r1:MCP-G3, r1:PAL-missed-6, r2:PAL-1, r2:PAL-12, r2:PAL-16, r2:BRW-10, r2:WC-12, r2:BRW-missed-detection-heuristics*

### M2.7 Launch path hygiene and protocol allowlist

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m2-launch` (Done)

Bare names are resolved against the server's working directory first (binary planting), and file:// skips every check. Settings pages and Store apps cannot be launched at all. The ms-settings: and shell:AppsFolder allowlist is pulled forward from the larger AUMID item.

**Design.** Resolve bare names ourselves: App Paths (HKCU, then HKLM), then a manual PATH enumeration with PATHEXT that skips relative, empty and '.' entries. SearchPathW cannot exclude the current directory. Pass the absolute path to ShellExecute, and default WorkingDirectory to the executable's folder, never the server's working directory. Add a regression test that plants a same-named exe in the working directory. Route file:// through ValidateFilePath. Allow ms-settings: and shell:AppsFolder\<AUMID>, plus schemes from WAM_ALLOWED_PROTOCOLS. Keep javascript:, vbscript:, data: and mshta: blocked.

**As shipped (`m2-launch`).** Already done in M0.4 (`m0-launch-and-kill`) and verified: ExecutableResolver resolves bare names itself, never from the current or working directory, with a manual PATH walk that skips empty, '.', relative and drive-relative entries, and passes the absolute path to ShellExecute; a name found only in the current or working directory is ExecutableInWorkingDirectory; file:// URIs are converted to their local path and validated as one (ValidateFileUri); the resolver had a real-file-system planting test. Kept on purpose: ShellExecuteEx's documented order (Windows directory, System32, PATH, then App Paths HKCU and HKLM), not App Paths first as designed, because a per-user App Paths key (writable by any program of the user) would then shadow System32. New: a bare name without an extension tries .com, .exe, .bat and .cmd in PATHEXT's order, lower-cased (unusable entries skipped); the planted-file check tries every PATHEXT type as well. Review fix: the first version resolved bare names to every PATHEXT type, so a default PATHEXT (.vbs, .js, .wsf, .msc, ...) turned names that ShellExecute's own search does not resolve into Windows Script Host scripts (System32 holds proxy-execution scripts such as SyncAppvPublishingServer.vbs and pubprn.vbs); a script now needs its full path. Refinement: a bare name found nowhere is refused with the new LaunchErrorCode ExecutableNotFound (15) instead of being passed to ShellExecute unresolved, whose own search starts in the current directory and tries .lnk/.pif, which the planted-file check did not cover (TOOLS.md documented that gap). Without workingDirectory, the program starts in its own folder (a document in its folder, a URI in the user's profile folder), never the server's current directory. ProtocolAllowlist: built in http, https, mailto, tel and ms-settings, plus shell:AppsFolder\<AppUserModelID> (a package family name and app ID, or a plain dotted ID; no paths, no GUID-folder entries) while the rest of shell: stays blocked; WAM_ALLOWED_PROTOCOLS adds schemes (separated by commas, semicolons or spaces; a trailing colon allowed), logged at first use, ignored entries as a warning; javascript, vbscript, data, mshta, cmd, powershell, pwsh, wscript, cscript, shell, ms-msdt, search-ms and search, and (review fix) ms-appinstaller, ms-officecmd, its, ms-its, mk, hcp, ms-cxh and ms-cxh-full can never be allowed; file is always validated as a path. ms-windows-store moved from blocked to unknown (allowable). URIs with control characters are InvalidUri, and http/https without '://' are InvalidUri. Review fix: many handlers are registered as app.exe "%1" (Outlook's mailto: as OUTLOOK.EXE -c IPM.Note /m "%1"), so a raw quote is InvalidUri in every URI, and whitespace or another character RFC 3986 does not allow (< > ` { } | \ ^) in every URI but http/https (browsers take the URI as one argument through --single-argument or -osint); the caller percent-encodes them. Regression tests: LaunchPlanningTests plants notepad.exe and a unique exe in a temporary folder used as both the working and the current directory, with the real resolver, and checks the installed Notepad's absolute path is planned, the default working directory is its folder, and the planted-only program is refused; nothing is started.

*Sources: r1:R49, r1:crit:R49, r2:R40, r2:crit-prio:R40, r1:PAL-missed-2, r1:PAL-missed-7, r2:PAL-G3*

### M2.8 Faithful layout apply with one-to-one matching and per-placement results

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m2-layouts` (Done; final-review fixes on `m2-fix-integration`)

Today N Chrome windows collapse onto one placement, an empty matcher matches every window, apply reports success when nothing matched, and a freshly launched untitled window causes an NRE. Recording a few identity fields at capture time is cheap and fixes the confirmed high-severity PAL-4.

**Design.** Apply takes one snapshot using the user-facing predicate and assigns windows one to one: a HashSet tracks assigned windows, and each placement takes the best unassigned candidate scored on title fragment, className, exe path and distance from the saved rect. Captured matchers compare processName exactly, case-insensitively. An empty matcher under title_only is notApplicable, not match-all, and unknown matchBy values are rejected. Capture records exact processName, className, a title pattern, an ordinal and WindowState as additive optional fields, the cheap identity subset of schema v2. Each placement runs in try/catch, and a null launched window becomes a per-placement error instead of the NRE at LayoutService.cs:227/254. Per-placement results are {status: applied|notFound|notApplicable|failed, window, errorCode}, and the overall result is Success=false or status 'partial'. Apply uses M2.3 geometry and M1.3 activation, and reports the focus result.

*Sources: r1:R57, r1:R58, r1:crit-prio:R58, r2:R38, r1:PAL-4, r1:PAL-6, r1:PAL-12, r1:PAL-13, r1:WC-missed-2, r2:PAL-4, r2:PAL-6, r2:PAL-8, r2:PAL-9, r2:MSP-10, r2:MSP-missed-layout-same-window*

**As shipped (`m2-layouts`).** Already done before this item: unknown matchBy values are rejected (M2.2, InvalidMatchBy), the NRE on a launched window is gone (M0's null check; GetWindowInfo now also describes untitled windows), and save/delete/storage results are honest (M0). Capture: each placement records, as additive optional fields, WindowMatcher.ExactProcessName (true), ClassName (the field existed but was never set or used), TitlePattern (the title, at most 260 characters; not recorded for a window the target policy denies, so a password manager's database name is not written to disk), ExePath (IProcessService.GetProcessPath, identity only: LaunchCommand stays null, so apply_layout's destructiveHint:false reasoning from M2.10 holds) and Ordinal (z-order position among the captured windows of the process), plus WindowPlacement.State; the preset JSON writes the state as text ("maximized") and older presets and older versions read the files unchanged. Apply: one GetAllWindows(includeMinimized: true) snapshot (the user-facing predicate). Refinement: instead of a per-placement greedy pick, every (placement, window) pair that passes the matchBy filter is scored and the pairs are taken best first (ties: placement order, then z-order), so a placement that recognizes its window by title is not robbed by an earlier placement that would take any window of the process; each window goes to one placement. Score: title equal 40, else 0.8 x 40 x the share of the pattern's parts (split at " - ", " | ", dashes) that are parts of the title; class equal +20, different -20; exe path equal +10, different -10; closeness to the target rectangle (L1 distance of the edges over twice the work area's width plus height) up to 8; same ordinal +4. Class, title pattern, exe path and ordinal only rank, never filter, so a renamed tab or a changed class never makes a window notFound; the matchBy criteria remain the only filters. Captured process names compare exactly (case-insensitive, also against the UWP frame host); hand-written presets keep the substring rule, so the samples ("Code", "chrome") still work. A placement with no criterion for the matchBy (title_only without titleContains, process_only without processName, or neither) is notApplicable with the new LayoutErrorCode.NoMatchCriterion (11). A monitorIndex that is not a monitor fails that placement with InvalidMonitorIndex (WindowService.MonitorIndexMessage) instead of falling back to monitor 0, in apply only (capture reads the index from the window service). Geometry goes through IWindowService: a maximized placement uses MoveWindowToMonitor(Maximize) (verified monitor and state); otherwise a window the snapshot shows maximized is restored with SetWindowState(Restore) before SetWindowBounds (M2.3 may make that redundant; it is harmless), and a hand-written minimized placement is minimized after placing.
