A window launched for a placement that turns out to be one another placement already has (a single-instance handoff) fails with LaunchFailed instead of being moved twice. Each placement runs in its own try/catch (cancellation still propagates). Results: LayoutApplyResult.Placements (order, status applied|notFound|notApplicable|failed, window {handle, title, processName}, launched, focused, errorCode, error) and Status (applied|partial|failed, derived from Success and WindowsArranged); apply_layout returns both, and success stays false whenever any placement has an errorCode. Per-placement and top-level codes stay at the layout level (WindowNotFound, LaunchFailed, ArrangeFailed with the window change's own code in the text, TargetDeniedByPolicy, plus NoMatchCriterion and InvalidMonitorIndex), so the existing error-reference rows still hold; a placement that was arranged but not focused (or not minimized) is applied with ArrangeFailed. The focus results are unchanged. get_layout shows the recorded fields. Payload: the typical apply_layout result (6 placements) grew from 72 to 277 tokens and get_layout (captured placements) from 257 to 529; the budgets are 420 and 800. Not done (left to R58 schema v2): monitor identity by device name with monitorFallback, GetWindowPlacement/SetWindowPlacement restore bounds and DPI-aware two-phase moves (M2.3), launch info and AUMIDs, a capture of minimized windows, and a version bump with migration (the new fields are optional, so none is needed yet).

**Final review (`m2-fix-integration`).** Arrange no longer restores a maximized window with SetWindowState(Restore) before SetWindowBounds: SW_RESTORE activates, and SetWindowBounds already restores with SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE (M2.3). A minimize that does not answer leaves the already placed window `applied` with ArrangeFailed ('could not minimize it (NotResponding)') instead of failing it. A placement that throws keeps its window and `launched: true`, and says NotResponding rather than the exception type. Each placement reports the `launchCommand` it ran (launchMissing), for the agent and the audit log, which records the placements as items with their status, `launched` and the launch program (redacted like a launch's executable, M2.17).

### M2.9 Capture health: never drop pixels, typed capture errors and warnings

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** fix · **Branch:** `m2-capture-health` (Done)

capture_with_elements drops the image on any UIA failure. Minimized, cloaked, hung, blank or off-screen captures all report Success=true. GDI misuse costs extra copies and can corrupt pixels.

**Design.** capture_with_elements validates the handle with IsWindow only, so untitled popups work. It captures pixels independently of the UIA walk and, if the walk fails or times out, still returns the image with ElementsError and Truncated. Add a ScreenshotErrorCode enum (WindowMinimized, WindowHidden, WindowCloaked, RegionOffScreen, CaptureFailed, EncodeFailed), plus Warnings[] and CaptureMethod. Pre-check IsIconic, IsWindowVisible, DWMWA_CLOAKED and IsHungAppWindow; a hung window gets a screen-DC BitBlt with a warning. Opt-in restoreIfMinimized: SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE, wait for two equal frames rather than a fixed 200 ms, capture, then re-minimize with SW_SHOWMINNOACTIVE, because SW_MINIMIZE activates another window. Add a blank-image warning from pixel sampling. Crop frame captures to DWMWA_EXTENDED_FRAME_BOUNDS. Intersect regions with the virtual screen using MONITOR_DEFAULTTONULL. Detect DPI-unaware targets and warn or scale. Call SelectObject(old) before GetDIBits, or use CreateDIBSection to drop two copies. Apply a 64 MP budget. Add WindowState to UIElementResult.

**As shipped (`m2-capture-health`).** Already done before this item: capture_with_elements validated the handle with IsWindow only (untitled popups worked since M1.2), the true capture origin, Format32bppRgb (opaque pixels) and CaptureMethod. New: ScreenshotErrorCode gains WindowMinimized (12), WindowHidden (13), Cloaked (14), RegionOffScreen (15) and EncodeFailed (16); CaptureFailed stays for a failed native capture. Refinement: the cloaked code is `Cloaked`, not `WindowCloaked`, and the hidden code shares `WindowHidden` with focus_window, so one condition has one name across the surface. ScreenshotService.CheckWindow refuses, before any pixel or element is read and in this order, a window that is not a window, a hidden one (IsWindowVisible), a cloaked one (DWMWA_CLOAKED of the window or its top-level window) and a minimized one (itself or its top-level window, WindowMinimized instead of M1's CaptureFailed). Review fix: hidden and cloaked come before minimized, and restoreIfMinimized refuses such a window before anything else (MinimizedWindowRestore checks again before ShowWindow), because restoring an app minimized to the notification area (iconic and hidden) showed it, and SW_SHOWMINNOACTIVE then left it visible with a taskbar button. PrintWindow runs through IWindowCallRunner (5 s deadline, refused at once for a window Windows reports hung); a window that does not answer is copied from the screen (IScreenCaptureWrapper.CaptureWindowFromScreen, captureMethod ScreenBitBlt) with a WindowNotResponding warning that gives the runner's reason (review fix: the runner also refuses at once while too many calls to other applications are stuck, which is not this window's fault). captureMethod values are ScreenBitBlt (screen copies, was BitBlt), PrintWindow and BitBltFallback (window DC copy, was BitBlt). Results gain `warnings` (strings that start with a code: ImageBlank from 32 x 32 sampled pixels within a tolerance of 2, WindowNotResponding, DpiScaledWindow when GetDpiForWindow differs from the monitor's DPI (warn, not scale), RegionClipped, RegionPartlyOffMonitors, WindowRestored, WindowStillPainting, ReminimizeFailed). Frame captures are cut to DWMWA_EXTENDED_FRAME_BOUNDS (read through INativeWindowWrapper, so the wrapper keeps to its allow-list) and report that origin. capture_region intersects the request with the virtual screen (RegionOffScreen when nothing is left), warns about parts on no monitor, and reports the monitor that contains the center or none (MONITOR_DEFAULTTONULL semantics, from the monitor list). The wrapper draws into a top-down CreateDIBSection and copies the bits out once after GdiFlush (GetDIBits and its declaration are gone, so no bitmap is read while selected); the service wraps the pinned array in the GDI+ bitmap without another copy, so a capture costs one copy instead of three (two for a frame capture, which is cropped). A 64-megapixel budget (MaxCapturePixels) and the 16384-pixel edge limit are checked before any allocation: InvalidRegion for capture_region, CaptureFailed for a larger window. The unused CreateCompatibleBitmap declaration and its allow-list entry are removed. A failure to build, scale or encode the image is EncodeFailed. capture_with_elements captures the pixels whatever the walk returns: a failed walk gives the image without overlays, success true, elementsError, elementsErrorCode (the get_ui_elements code) and truncated; a partial walk sets truncated and truncationReason (closing the M1.8 gap); an unknown control type still fails the call; an untitled window's title comes from the walk. restoreIfMinimized (capture_window and capture_with_elements, default false): the target policy is checked for the minimized window and every window it owns (they show again), then the tool takes the input gate (InputGateToolFilter.EnterAsync, so PausedByUser while paused, InputBusy when busy), ShowWindow(SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE) through the runner, waits until it is not iconic and two consecutive PrintWindow frames are equal (at most 1.5 s, else WindowStillPainting), captures (and for capture_with_elements walks) and minimizes again with SW_SHOWMINNOACTIVE. UIElementResult gains WindowState (Normal, Maximized, Minimized) of the top-level window. Not done: a window maximized before it was minimized is captured at its normal size, and its restore-to-maximized flag may be lost (documented in the parameter, TOOLS.md and the WindowRestored warning; keeping it needs GetWindowPlacement/SetWindowPlacement and a desktop check, because SetWindowPlacement only keeps the flag with SW_SHOWMINIMIZED, which activates); DPI-unaware windows are warned about, not rescaled; get_ui_elements of a minimized window still succeeds with an empty tree (now with windowState), rather than failing.

*Sources: r1:R16, r1:R20, r2:R15, r2:R14, r1:crit:R16, r1:UIA-7, r1:UIA-6, r1:SCR-6, r1:UIA-5, r1:SCR-2, r1:SCR-4, r1:UIA-9, r1:SCR-7, r1:SCR-9, r1:SCR-10, r1:SCR-G4, r1:SCR-missed-2, r1:SCR-missed-3, r2:SCR-6, r2:SCR-7, r2:SCR-11, r2:SCR-12, r2:SCR-G6*

### M2.10 Tool annotations, titles and human confirmation through elicitation

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** improve · **Branch:** `m2-tool-surface` (Done; final-review fixes on `m2-fix-privacy` and `m2-fix-integration`)

No tool sets ReadOnly, Destructive, Idempotent, OpenWorld or Title, so clients must treat find_windows like kill_process. Irreversible actions rely on a confirm flag that the model sets for itself.

**Design.** Every [McpServerTool] gets a Title and hints. ReadOnly: find/get/list/wait tools and browser reads. Captures: ReadOnly=false because they can write files, but non-destructive. Geometry tools: Destructive=false, Idempotent=true. Destructive=true: close*, kill_process, delete_layout, save_layout (it overwrites), send_keys, type_text, the click family and element_action. OpenWorld: launch tools and browser navigation. kill_process, close_windows_batch and close_application take McpServer and call ElicitAsync (SDK 2.2.0; test it on both protocol paths, as resume_input is) with names, PIDs and titles when the client advertises elicitation, falling back to confirm otherwise. This lands after the M0.5 concurrency fixes, because ReadOnly hints make clients parallelize more. The contract test enforces annotations on every tool.

**As shipped (`m2-tool-surface`).** Every [McpServerTool] sets Title, ReadOnly, Destructive, Idempotent and OpenWorld explicitly; tools/list carries the title twice (tool and annotations). Read-only: the find/get/list tools, the browser reads and get_control_status (no wait tools exist yet). Destructive: close_window, close_windows_batch, kill_process, delete_layout, save_layout, the click, type and key tools (element_action does not exist yet), launch_application and launch_applications_batch (any program with any arguments, a shell command included; review fix) and the four captures (overwrite:true replaces the file at outputPath; review fix); mouse_move, release_all_input, resume_input, launch_browser_with_debug (a fixed browser with a fresh profile; made destructive by the final review, below) and apply_layout (it starts only launch commands the user wrote into a preset) are not. Idempotent: the read-only tools and the geometry changes, focus, apply_layout, save/delete_layout, mouse_move, resume_input and release_all_input; set_window_state and its batch are not (restore twice can un-maximize), nor are the captures, which can write a new file each call (ReadOnly=false). OpenWorld: the three launch tools, apply_layout (launchMissing runs a preset's launch commands; review fix) and, refinement, get_browser_content, whose text comes from a website; browser navigation does not exist yet. ToolAnnotationContractTests enforces a title and all four hints on every tool, unique titles, read-only tools that are neither destructive nor gated, and the exact destructive, read-only and open-world sets. Human confirmation: kill_process and close_windows_batch (above 3 windows) take an McpServer and, when the client advertises form elicitation, ask the user through UserConfirmation.AskAsync (a confirm checkbox, 2-minute answer timeout) with the process name, PID, window titles and forceKill's child processes, or each window's title, process, PID and an agent-host mark; a decline, dismissal, unticked answer, timeout or failed question is ConfirmationDeclined (new WindowErrorCode 31 and ProcessErrorCode 16) and nothing is done, and the model's confirm no longer suffices on such a client; without elicitation the confirm flag works as before. The wave-A hazard is designed out: the two tools moved from WindowChangingTools to a new InputGateToolFilter.SelfGatedTools list, check the pause first (ThrowIfPaused), ask outside the gate, and only then take it with InputGateToolFilter.EnterAsync, which waits, refuses (InputBusy) and reports a pause (PausedByUser) as the filter does. A kill that would be refused anyway is refused without asking (IProcessService.CheckKillTarget runs KillProcessAsync's checks without acting, and for a graceful close also refuses a process without a main window, NoClosableWindow), and the confirmed name is pinned as expectedProcessName so a reused PID is ProcessNameMismatch; a confirmed window whose handle now belongs to another process, or that was no window when the user was asked and is one now, fails with the new HandleMismatch (32) and is not closed. Titles in the questions go through UserConfirmation.Quote (one line, at most 80 characters), so an app's title cannot rewrite the question. UserConfirmationContractTests runs on both protocol paths, with an elicitation handler that takes the input gate while the question is open; a mutation that takes the gate before asking fails it. close_application does not exist yet (M2.5) and should follow the same pattern (it does: M2.5 asks the same way above 3 windows).

**Final review (`m2-fix-privacy`, `m2-fix-integration`).** launch_browser_with_debug is destructive (MCP-S-1): the "no command of the agent's choosing" reason did not hold, because the agent chooses the profile folder and the URL, and the URL is a separate argument the browser reads as a switch when it starts with '-' or '/'. Such a `url` (or one with a control character) and a `userDataDir` inside a folder named `User Data` (also as its 8.3 short name), where Chrome, Edge and Brave keep the user's profiles, are InvalidArguments tool errors; a real profile kept elsewhere cannot be told apart. ToolAnnotationContractTests' destructive set includes it. UserConfirmation.Quote also drops format characters (bidi overrides and isolates, zero-width characters) and shows single-quote look-alikes as double quotes, so a title cannot fake a process name or PID in the question (PRIVA-5). `m2-last-fixes`: Quote reads code points, not UTF-16 units, so format characters outside the BMP (the tag characters U+E0000 to U+E007F) are dropped too, lone surrogates are dropped, and the 80-character cut never splits a surrogate pair. `force` on a protected window is the user's decision when the client can ask (PRIVA-6): close_window first tries without force and asks only after a ProtectedWindow refusal (outside the gate; ConfirmationDeclined, or HandleMismatch when the handle changed hands meanwhile), and close_windows_batch asks when one of its windows is protected, whatever the number of windows, and passes force on only after a yes; a client that cannot ask keeps the old force. The batch question lists the first 20 windows and counts the rest; at first a protected window past the 20th was covered only by the general 'force is set' line, and `m2-last-fixes` names every protected window (title, process, PID, marked protected) wherever it is in the list. kill_process: CheckKillTarget reads the process's start time while it holds the process open, so a confirmed kill is refused (ProcessNameMismatch) when the PID now belongs to a process that started at another time, even one with the same name, and a forced kill when the tree gained a process the user was not shown (new ProcessErrorCode.ProcessTreeChanged, 17); the close request or the termination is sent inside the gate and the gate is released before the wait for the exit (up to 60 s, KillSafeguards.BeforeWait), as the close tools do.

**Follow-up (`m3-force-confirm`, M2.F1).** Decided by the user on 2026-10-11: `force` on a protected window takes two explicit decisions with and without elicitation, so a prompt-injected agent cannot close the desktop shell or its own host with one flag. With elicitation nothing changes (the user is asked; `confirm` does not count). Without it, close_window and close_windows_batch need `confirm=true` too, where `force` alone used to close a protected window (in a batch of 3 or fewer; close_window always). close_window gains a `confirm` parameter (after waitMs) and, as on the elicitation path, first closes without force; a ProtectedWindow refusal without `confirm` becomes ConfirmationRequired in its result (the protection reason, without the "Pass force=true" advice, and nothing sent). close_windows_batch checks every resolved window with CheckClose when `force` is set and either the client can ask or `confirm` is false; a window protected now without `confirm` refuses the whole batch with the ConfirmationRequired tool error, which names the protected handles and, past 3 windows, both reasons, so an ordinary window in that batch is not closed either. Refinement: `force` is passed on without elicitation only with `confirm` (before: always), so `force` on a batch with no protected window needs no `confirm` and a window that becomes protected between the check and the send is refused with ProtectedWindow, as on the elicitation path, where `force` is passed on only after the user's yes. close_application has no `force` (it refuses a protected window outright), so it is unchanged. No new error code: WindowErrorCode.ConfirmationRequired (24) now covers both cases. Breaking for clients without elicitation, listed in docs/TOOLS.md's "Changes for existing clients".

*Sources: r1:R9, r2:R50, r1:R14, r1:MCP-G1, r1:WC-G7, r2:MSP-G4*

### M2.11 Server instructions, get_server_info and an observe-act-verify skill

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** improve · **Branch:** `m2-server-info-toolsets` (Done; follow-ups on `m2-fix-privacy` and `m2-fix-integration`)

Clients without the plugin get no guidance at all. Nothing reports the build, integrity level, DPI mode, host windows or pause state. SKILL.md needs a rewrite around verified actions.

**Design.** Set options.ServerInstructions (15-20 lines). Coordinates are physical virtual-screen pixels and may be negative. Map image to screen with imageToScreen or captureId, never with ScaleFactor. Always pass a window target to input tools. Prefer element tools over coordinates. Use waits, not sleeps. Explain the error codes (TargetIsAgentHost, TargetElevated, BlockedByModal, PausedByUser, TargetDeniedByPolicy) and state that destructive chords need a verified target. Add a read-only get_server_info returning version and gitSha, sdkVersion, integrity and elevation, effective dpiAwareness (GetAwarenessFromDpiAwarenessContext(GetThreadDpiAwarenessContext())), inputDesktopAvailable, sessionLocked, foregroundLockTimeout, monitors with DPI, host windows, pause and hotkey state, deny policy, layouts and audit paths, and enabled toolsets. DPI: declare PerMonitorV2 in app.manifest only and drop the API call, which always fails with ACCESS_DENIED once the manifest sets awareness; log the effective awareness. Rewrite SKILL.md around locate, activate/verify, act, wait, verify, with browser and failure-mode sections. This item does not depend on generated docs.

**As shipped (`m2-server-info-toolsets`).** ServerIdentity sets the server's name, version and instructions (Program.cs passes ServerIdentity.Configure to AddMcpServer): 17 lines on the locate, activate and verify, act, verify loop (windowHandle on every input tool, element bounds over image points, close_window, waiting by re-reading state, since the wait tools come with M3.7 and M3.8), the coordinate rules (physical virtual-screen pixels, possibly negative; imageToScreen, never scaleFactor), what PausedByUser, TargetIsAgentHost, TargetElevated, BlockedByModal, TargetDeniedByPolicy, DestructiveChordNeedsTarget and SessionLocked/DesktopUnavailable mean and what to do, untrusted text, and get_server_info first. ServerInstructionsTests keeps them at 15-20 lines that name only served tools and never the Alt+F4 chord. get_server_info (titled, ReadOnly, not destructive, idempotent, closed-world; in the always-on server toolset, outside the input gate) reports server (version, gitSha, sdkVersion, runtime, os), connection (the negotiated protocolVersion and the client's name and version, read from the request, so they are right on every revision), process (integrity level, elevation, session, effective dpiAwareness), desktop (inputDesktopAvailable, sessionLocked, foregroundLockTimeoutMs), monitors with their DPI, host (the console and startup-foreground windows and the server, ancestor and console-server processes the host guard recorded, by name), input (pause and hotkey), safety (target policy and destructive chords), paths (layouts; auditLog stays null until M2.17), toolsets (M2.14) and warnings: one sentence for each thing that will make tools fail or coordinates wrong (an awareness other than PerMonitorV2, a locked or unreachable desktop, integrity below medium, a pause, a hotkey that is not registered or not shared, ignored toolset entries). Its typical report is 543 tokens (budget 850). DPI: app.manifest declares PerMonitorV2 (PerMonitor before Windows 10 1703, true/pm before 1607) and keeps asInvoker; the SetProcessDpiAwareness call and its P/Invoke are gone, and a test reads the built apphost's bytes for the manifest. NativeSystemInfoWrapper reads the effective awareness (GetThreadDpiAwarenessContext, and AreDpiAwarenessContextsEqual to tell v2 from v1) and SPI_GETFOREGROUNDLOCKTIMEOUT (the only SystemParametersInfoW caller, pinned by NativeCallGuardTests), and ServerStartupLog logs the versions and the awareness at start, with a warning when it is not PerMonitorV2; the E2E harness gives the test host the same awareness. SKILL.md is rewritten around the loop, with a failure-mode section, a toolsets section, the browser section starting from locating and verifying the page, and the workflows as loop steps; its generated regions are unchanged. The initialize and server/discover results, which carry the instructions, are snapshotted per revision (M2.12 follow-up). Not done: a per-window Dpi field on WindowInfo (WC-14; with M2.3's cross-DPI moves), per-window integrity and hung flags (M6.6), and rewriting every tool description to state its result fields (the descriptions stay as they are; titles and annotations are M2.10's).

**Follow-ups (`m2-selectors`, `m2-fix-privacy`, `m2-fix-integration`, `m2-docs-pass`).** The instructions are 18 lines: a selector example and AmbiguousWindow (M2.4), the act rule naming scroll and the window selector (MCP-S-4), and the clipboard among what the server acts on. get_server_info's `safety` gains `userClipboardAllowed` (WAM_ALLOW_USER_CLIPBOARD) and `allowedProtocols` (the built-in schemes, then those WAM_ALLOWED_PROTOCOLS adds), its warnings name a clipboard value that was not understood and the protocol entries that were ignored, and ServerStartupLog logs both settings at start (the extra schemes were logged only at the first launch, at a level the log filter dropped).

*Sources: r1:R11, r1:R13, r2:R51, r2:crit:R51, r2:crit:R26, r1:MCP-G9, r1:MCP-G5, r1:WC-14, r2:MSP-G7, r2:MSP-G8, r2:WC-14*

### M2.12 In-process MCP contract tests, shared DI and generated docs

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** improve · **Branch:** `m2-foundation` (Done; follow-up on `m2-server-info-toolsets`)

Test the server the way an agent sees it, over MCP. Snapshot the tool surface, enforce payload budgets and catch stale binaries. Generate the tool tables in the docs so they stop drifting. This is the safety net for M2's enum and schema changes and for the SDK upgrade.

**Design.** Add services.AddWindowsAppManager(options) in ServiceCollectionExtensions, used by Program.cs and by the tests; this fixes DependencyInjectionTests missing seven types. Contract tests host AddMcpServer().WithStreamServerTransport over in-memory pipes with mocked services and connect an McpClient. They (a) snapshot tools/list (name, title, annotations, inputSchema) into tools.snapshot.json, (b) call every tool and assert non-empty content, isError==!Success and an image block first for captures, (c) run reflection guards for nint members and annotations, (d) make about 50 parallel read calls, and (e) measure payload sizes and assert per-tool token budgets. A CI guard starts the committed bin exe, calls tools/list and diffs the result against the snapshot, which catches the stale binaries a version check cannot. tools/DocGen, or an UPDATE_SNAPSHOTS=1 test run, rewrites marker regions in README.md, docs/TOOLS.md and SKILL.md, and CI fails on git diff --exit-code.

**As shipped (`m2-foundation`).** Already done before M2.12: the shared registration is ServiceRegistration.AddWindowsAppManagerServices and AddWindowsAppManagerTools (M0), used by Program.cs, the DI tests and the tool catalog, so DependencyInjectionTests no longer copies Program.cs; a contract test serializes every result type with all members populated (M0.3); the reflection guard for nint members is that serialization test plus ToolResult_JsonSchema_HasNoUnsupportedTypes. New: (a) Contract/tools.snapshot.json holds every tool object of tools/list as an in-process server with the production registrations sends it (name, title, description, annotations, inputSchema, outputSchema, sorted by name), not just the listed fields: parameter descriptions are inside inputSchema anyway, and the stale-exe guard should catch a description-only change too. ToolSurfaceSnapshotTests names the added, removed and changed tools and JSON paths, and rewrites the file with UPDATE_SNAPSHOTS=1. (b) EveryToolContractTests calls all 47 tools over MCP through the production tool and filter registrations with mocked services: the typical call returns JSON with success true, a capture its image block first; the failing call returns isError = !success. The 27 tools that still return isError false on failure, and get_foreground_window's null, are listed as M2.1's to-do, and the lists can only shrink. About 50 parallel read calls through one server all succeed with identical results. (c) ToolPayloadBudgetTests measures each tool's typical result on a TypicalDesktop (15 windows, 40 processes, a 120-element tree, a 15,000-character article) and holds it to a per-tool budget of about 1.5 times the measured size (text at 4 bytes per token; images at width x height / 750, at most 3,300 tokens at the default 1568 pixels). Measured in October 2026: capture_with_elements metadata 6,026 tokens, get_ui_elements 5,397 (above M6's 4k aim), get_browser_content 3,815, list_processes 1,328, find_windows 989; everything else under 300. (d) The tool tables of README.md, docs/TOOLS.md and SKILL.md are marker regions (tools:begin/tools:end) that the same UPDATE_SNAPSHOTS=1 run regenerates from tools/list and a ToolDocCatalog (category, summary, key parameters) in the test project, rather than a tools/DocGen console: the test project already hosts the server. GeneratedToolDocsTests fails on a stale region, so CI needs no git diff step. Refinement: docs/TOOLS.md's parameter tables take names, order, types, required flags and defaults from the inputSchema but keep their hand-written Description cells and worded null defaults ("primary", "temp file"), which are written for people and differ from the model-facing text; a new parameter starts with its schema description. GeneratedToolDocsTests replaces ToolDocumentationTests with the same checks. (e) build.yml runs scripts/Test-ToolsSnapshot.ps1, which starts an exe with WAM_PAUSE_HOTKEY=none, lists its tools over stdio and compares them with the snapshot: always for the exe it publishes, and with -OnlyIfCurrent for the committed bin exe, which is compared only when Get-PluginBinaryProblems (moved from Test-PluginBinary.ps1 into ReleaseCommon.ps1) finds it current and is otherwise skipped with a notice. Not done here: Title and annotations on every tool and their reflection guard (M2.10, which will show in the snapshot), and driving the E2E fixture suite through the in-process MCP client.

**Follow-up (`m2-server-info-toolsets`).** UPDATE_SNAPSHOTS=1 is refused when CI, GITHUB_ACTIONS or TF_BUILD is true, and a regenerated file replaces the old one in one step. ToolSurfaceSnapshotTests also snapshots the result of a client's first request per revision (Contract/initialize.snapshot.json: initialize on 2025-06-18 and 2025-11-25, server/discover on 2026-07-28, serverInfo.version masked), so a capability or revision an SDK upgrade adds shows up. scripts/Test-ToolsSnapshot.ps1 resolves relative paths against the PowerShell location, asks for 2025-11-25 (-ProtocolVersion; a test keeps it equal to the revision the SDK answers an unknown request with) and starts the exe with WAM_TOOLSETS=all and without WAM_EXCLUDE_TOOLS and WAM_READ_ONLY.

**Follow-up (`m3-m2-followups`, M2.F6).** TypicalDesktop's windows now carry `visibleBounds` (the frame without the 7-pixel invisible borders), as the service reports for every window, and the payload budgets were re-measured: find_windows and get_all_windows 1,213 tokens (989 before; budget 1,850), get_foreground_window 72 (budget 110), get_window_info 100, launch_application 103 (budget 160) and launch_applications_batch 242 (budget 370). The measured sizes of the captures (take_screenshot 125, capture_region 112, capture_window 120, capture_with_elements 6,052) and get_server_info (598) had already grown in M2 without their comments being updated; their budgets still hold. TESTING.md has the new figures.

*Sources: r1:R3, r1:R4, r2:R4, r2:crit:R51, r1:crit:R2, r1:crit:R12, r1:MCP-G7, r1:MCP-2, r1:MCP-14, r1:MCP-10, r2:MSP-G5, r2:MSP-7, r2:PAL-17, r2:BRW-15*

### M2.13 Upgrade the MCP SDK to stable; cancellation and progress everywhere

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** improve · **Branch:** `m2-foundation` (Done; follow-up on `m2-server-info-toolsets`)

Move off ModelContextProtocol 0.5.0-preview.1 right after the contract tests exist, before M3-M5 add about 30 tools. Porting 42 tools now is cheaper than porting about 75 later.

**Design.** Upgrade to the current stable ModelContextProtocol line, with the M2.12 contract tests as the safety net. Expected breaks are isolated in helpers: ImageContentBlock bytes in ToolImages, IList collections, and StructuredContent as JsonElement?. Every potentially long-running tool becomes async with a CancellationToken linked to its own timeout. Replace the unlinked CancellationTokenSources at UIElementTools.cs:73 and ScreenshotTools.cs:190, and turn Thread.Sleep into Task.Delay(ct). Add IProgress<ProgressNotificationValue> to apply_layout, the batch launch, deep UIA walks and the wait tools.

**As shipped (`m2-foundation`).** ModelContextProtocol 2.2.0, the newest stable release (Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting and DependencyInjection move to 10.0.10, the abstractions version it requires). Only two of the expected breaks applied: ToolImages builds image content with ImageContentBlock.FromBytes (Data is now base64 UTF-8 bytes, DecodedData the image), and the two call-tool filters register through WithRequestFilters, since the AddCallToolFilter extensions on IMcpServerBuilder were removed; the first filter added is still the outermost. Elicitation (resume_input), the error filter, call logging, the input gate, stdin-EOF release and the hosted services needed no change. The tools/list snapshot changed only where the SDK's schema generation did: a nullable parameter (string?, string[]?) is typed as a union with "null" (18 parameters), and launch_application's arguments items as ["string", "null"] (null items were already refused with InvalidArgumentValue); the generated tables show the non-null type. Refinement: 2.x speaks the 2026-07-28 revision (server/discover, no initialize handshake, elicitation as a multi-round-trip request), which its own client prefers, as well as the earlier revisions with the initialize handshake that stdio clients use; ProtocolRevisionContractTests checks that both list the snapshot's tools (the CI guard lists with 2025-06-18), and the elicitation, cancellation, stdin-EOF and progress contract tests run on both. A blank elicitation capability is pinned to mean form support. Cancellation: get_ui_elements and capture_with_elements link the request token with their timeout (UIElementTools.ReadTreeAsync), so a client cancellation stops the walk and is reported as cancelled, while a timeout still returns what was read; the UIA worker waits asynchronously (IUiaWorker.RunAsync), which made the walk and the two inspection tools async. The launch path (launch_application, launch_applications_batch, launch_browser_with_debug, apply_layout's launches) waits for a window with Task.Delay on the call's token; the four window batches pause with Task.Delay(ct) outside their per-window try/catch; kill_process waits for the exit with IProcessHandle.WaitForExitAsync. A cancelled call throws, so the SDK sends no result and the call log says cancelled; what was done stays done, and the input gate is freed at once. Progress: services report OperationProgress (a model type) and ToolProgress forwards it, best effort, as ProgressNotificationValue: a UIA walk every 100 elements read (never after it was asked to stop), launch_applications_batch after each application, apply_layout after each placement with its outcome. The server writes them in order and before the result; the SDK client dispatches notifications concurrently and drops late ones, so the tests check the wire (InProcessMcpServer.ServerMessages). Not done: ForegroundService's polls keep Thread.Sleep on purpose (they run on the dedicated activation thread, bounded by the activation deadline); the browser tools' UI Automation fallback still reads synchronously without a deadline (IUIAutomationWrapper, outside the UIA worker); the wait tools do not exist yet; elicitation for kill_process and close_windows_batch stays with M2.10, and resources and prompts with M6.9.

**Follow-up (`m2-server-info-toolsets`).** The elicitation, progress, cancellation and stdin-EOF theories run on 2025-11-25 too (InProcessMcpServer.Revisions(): the SDK client's default, 2025-06-18 and 2025-11-25), the revision Claude Code most likely negotiates. The cancellation tests check the server's output for no result or error with the cancelled id (the SDK client ends its own call when it sends notifications/cancelled, so the call is no evidence); the SDK honours it on all three revisions. The fixture's message recording keeps a UTF-8 decoder across writes.

*Sources: r1:R14, r1:crit-prio:R14, r2:R52, r1:MCP-G10, r1:INP-G8, r1:UIA-3, r2:MSP-G8*

### M2.14 Toolsets with a curated default and a read-only mode

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m2-server-info-toolsets` (Done; follow-ups on `m2-wave-b-followups` and `m2-fix-privacy`)

This roadmap grows the surface from 42 to about 70-75 tools, and every schema sits in context on every turn. A curated core of about 20-25 tools by default, with everything else opt-in, keeps tool selection reliable.

**Design.** Program.cs reads WAM_TOOLSETS / WAM_EXCLUDE_TOOLS and WAM_READ_ONLY=1 before the WithTools registrations. The default core covers desktop state, find/act/wait, windows, capture, guarded input, launch and server info. Opt-in groups: ocr, visual, browser-actions, layouts-v2, workspace and process. Read-only mode registers only ReadOnly=true methods through McpServerTool.Create. Disabled groups are also blocked inside run_steps. Document plugin.json env profiles, for example an 'observer' profile. This must land before M3-M5 add tools.

**As shipped (`m2-server-info-toolsets`).** Program.cs reads ToolsetSelection.FromEnvironment() before the tool registrations and passes it to AddWindowsAppManagerTools: WAM_TOOLSETS (separated by commas, semicolons or spaces, any case; `default`, `all`, toolset names, and `+name`/`-name`, which change the default when no toolset is named plainly), WAM_EXCLUDE_TOOLS (tool names) and WAM_READ_ONLY (1, true, yes or on; any other value that is not off turns it on, with a warning, to be safe). Toolsets.Server puts every tool in one of ten toolsets (eleven since M2.16 added `clipboard`): server (always on: get_server_info and the pause controls), windows, monitors, capture, elements, input, launch, process, layouts and browser, all on by default today, so an unconfigured server still serves every tool; the opt-in groups (ocr, visual, browser-actions, layouts-v2, workspace) come with their tools in M3-M6, and a fixture catalog tests the opt-in path now. Refinement: rather than registering the methods one by one through McpServerTool.Create, the tool classes are registered as before and a PostConfigure of McpServerOptions removes the unselected tools from the tool collection after the SDK collected them, so tool creation stays the SDK's. Read-only mode keys on each tool's readOnlyHint, which [McpServerTool(ReadOnly = true)] sets (M2.10 sets the hints on the existing tools; until it lands, read-only mode serves get_server_info alone), and a contract test checks that every tool annotated read-only is a read-only tool of the input gate. A tool that is not served is missing from tools/list and an unknown tool when called. An entry that is not a toolset or tool is ignored with a warning (logged at start by ServerStartupLog with the number of served tools, and reported by get_server_info); WAM_TOOLSETS without one valid toolset keeps the default. AddWindowsAppManagerTools() without a selection serves every toolset, which the snapshot and the tests check, and the CI guard starts the exe with WAM_TOOLSETS=all. docs/TOOLS.md has a Toolsets section with a table generated from the catalog and env profiles (observer: WAM_READ_ONLY=1; arranger; no process control). Not done: blocking disabled groups inside run_steps, which does not exist yet; M6.2 must ask ToolsetSelection.Serves for each step.

**Follow-ups (`m2-wave-b-followups`).** WAM_TOOLSETS fails closed instead of open: an unknown `-name` (meant to remove a toolset), or an unknown plain name with no valid plain name beside it (meant to replace the default), serves only the always-on `server` toolset, with a warning in the start log and in get_server_info; an unknown `+name` is still ignored, since ignoring it serves less. Refusing to start was considered and rejected: the client would only see a failed connection, with the reason on a stderr the user may never read, while a server that keeps get_server_info lets the agent read the warning and tell the user. get_server_info is the catalog's always-served tool (ToolsetCatalog.AlwaysServedTool): WAM_EXCLUDE_TOOLS ignores it with a warning, so the tool the instructions send the agent to first is always there. The instructions are toolset-aware: when the user turned off a toolset that is on by default, read-only mode or single tools, WithToolsets appends one line to the server instructions that names them and tells the agent to skip the steps whose tools are missing and tell the user instead of working around them (ToolsetSelection.InstructionsNote; an unconfigured server's instructions, and the initialize snapshot, are unchanged); SKILL.md says the same. Read-only mode: the annotations are merged, so docs/TOOLS.md's read-only list is now a generated region (`read-only`, from the readOnlyHint annotations), and ToolsetContractTests also refuses read-only annotations on the gate-free tools that write files (save_layout, delete_layout, the four captures) and checks that read-only mode with every toolset serves none of them or any input, launch, kill, close or window-changing tool. apply_layout is no longer idempotent (a second call with launchMissing can start another instance while the launched window does not match yet), completing the review fix that made it open-world; its launchMissing description says it runs the preset's launchCommand. DPI: per-monitor v1, the manifest's fallback before Windows 10 1703, gives this windowless server physical pixels too, so DpiAwarenessNames.GivesPhysicalPixels (was IsExpected) accepts it and neither the start log nor get_server_info warns about it. Smaller items: UPDATE_SNAPSHOTS=1 is refused when a CI variable has any value but empty, `false` or `0`; Publish-Plugin.ps1 and Test-PluginBinary.ps1 check the published exe's bytes for the PerMonitorV2 manifest (a single-file publish was checked to carry it); the docs no longer promise that get_server_info never fails, and the Everything profile no longer claims an unset WAM_TOOLSETS includes the opt-in toolsets.

**Final review (`m2-fix-privacy`).** WAM_EXCLUDE_TOOLS also takes toolset names (`process` leaves out every tool of the process toolset) and fails closed like WAM_TOOLSETS (MCP-S-2): an entry that is neither a tool nor a toolset serves only the `server` toolset, and get_server_info reports `source` `WAM_EXCLUDE_TOOLS` with a warning, so a typo never leaves a tool served that the user meant to turn off. M2.16 added the eleventh toolset, `clipboard` (get_clipboard, set_clipboard), on by default.

*Sources: r1:R10, r1:crit-prio:R10, r2:R53, r1:MCP-G6, r2:MSP-G9*

### M2.15 scroll primitive (pulled forward)

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m2-scroll-clipboard` (Done; final-review fix on `m2-fix-privacy`)

Most list items in Settings, Explorer or Outlook cannot be reached without scrolling. The wheel constants already exist, and scroll needs only the M1 guard, not the M4 keyboard rewrite.

**Design.** scroll(window?, x?, y? (default: client centre), deltaY in notches, deltaX, space?) sends one SendInput batch: MOVE|ABSOLUTE|VIRTUALDESK to the normalized point ((x - SM_XVIRTUALSCREEN) * 65535 / (SM_CXVIRTUALSCREEN - 1)), then MOUSEEVENTF_WHEEL/HWHEEL with mouseData = unchecked((uint)(notches*120)). It goes through the M1 guard, host guard and policy, and returns the window under the cursor, since the wheel follows the 'scroll inactive windows' setting. Element-level scrolling comes with element_action (M3.6).

**As shipped (`m2-scroll-clipboard`).** `scroll(windowHandle?, x?, y?, deltaY, deltaX, allowHostTarget, releaseHeldModifiers)` in the input toolset, annotated destructive (the wheel over a combo box, slider or number box changes its value) and not idempotent. deltaY counts down as positive, as a web page's wheel event does; at most 100 notches per direction, and anything else (only one of x and y, neither a point nor windowHandle, no notches) is an InvalidArguments tool error. Without a point it turns at the centre of windowHandle's client area (new read `INativeHitTestWrapper.GetClientAreaOnScreen`: GetClientRect plus MapWindowPoints). It runs the click path: input gate, pause, held modifiers and buttons, `CheckMouse`, SetCursorPos, a 10 ms settle, `RecheckMouse`, the pointer check and the modifier and button re-checks, then one SendInput batch with the move to the point and one WHEEL/HWHEEL event per notch (WHEEL_DELTA each, not one large delta, because some applications scroll one step for any delta). Two refinements of the design: the normalized point uses the existing `ToVirtualDesktopAbsolute` (the pixel-centre mapping clicks use), not the (SM_CXVIRTUALSCREEN - 1) formula; and 'returns the window under the cursor' was not enough, because with "Scroll inactive windows" off the wheel goes to the focus window, which can be the agent's own terminal. So the server reads SPI_GETMOUSEWHEELROUTING (new `INativeSystemInfoWrapper.GetMouseWheelRouting`; NativeCallGuardTests now also pins every SPI_ constant to a GET action) and, unless the wheel follows the pointer, requires the window at the point to be the foreground window, before the pointer moves and again right before the wheel (new `InputErrorCode.WheelFollowsFocus` = 42; an unreadable setting counts as focus routing). `InputEvent` gained `MouseData` (the E2E fixture policy now requires the fixture in front for any wheel batch). `space?` was left out: every coordinate is already physical virtual-desktop pixels.

**Follow-ups (`m2-selectors`, `m2-fix-privacy`).** scroll takes a `window` selector like the other input tools (M2.4); a bare `{"foreground": true}` without a point is an InvalidArguments tool error instead of an UnhandledException (MCP-S-3), since it names no window whose client area could be used.

*Sources: r2:R34, r2:crit-prio:R34, r1:R29, r1:INP-G3, r2:INP-G2, r2:UIA-G8*

### M2.16 Clipboard get/set with a privacy guard (pulled forward)

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m2-scroll-clipboard` (Done; final-review fixes on `m2-fix-privacy`)

Copy-then-read is the cheapest way to extract text from apps with poor UIA (terminals, Electron, canvases, RDP). Reading the user's clipboard could leak passwords, so reads are guarded.

**Design.** Run on a dedicated STA thread, because MCP calls arrive on MTA threads. Use System.Windows.Clipboard, or OpenClipboard with CF_UNICODETEXT, retrying on CLIPBRD_E_CANT_OPEN. set_clipboard(text) also sets ExcludeClipboardContentFromMonitorProcessing so the text stays out of Win+V history. get_clipboard(format=text|html|files, maxLength=20000) refuses or masks content that carries ExcludeClipboardContentFromMonitorProcessing, CanIncludeInClipboardHistory=0 or 'Clipboard Viewer Ignore'. By default it returns content only if GetClipboardSequenceNumber changed after a server-initiated copy in this session, and otherwise reports clipboardNotFromAgent. Reading the user's own clipboard is an opt-in through server config or elicitation, not a flag the model sets. Paste-mode typing comes in M4.4.

**As shipped (`m2-scroll-clipboard`).** `get_clipboard(format text|html|files, maxLength 1-1000000, default 20000)` (read-only, no input gate, so it may ask the user) and `set_clipboard(text)` (destructive: it replaces what the user copied; idempotent; inside the input gate, so it waits for a paste in progress and is refused while input is paused), in a new `clipboard` toolset that is on by default. Raw Win32, not System.Windows.Clipboard: the server references no WPF, and the raw calls sit behind a new `INativeClipboardWrapper` (NativeClipboardWrapper over a new `NativeMethods.Clipboard` class that NativeCallGuardTests pins to it; tests use `FakeClipboardWrapper`; the E2E catalog classifies Read and WriteText as a new `Clipboard` kind refused in every tier, and only the sequence number as a read). Each call runs on a dedicated STA thread with a message-only owner window (without an owner, EmptyClipboard leaves the clipboard ownerless and SetClipboardData fails), retries OpenClipboard for about 300 ms, and gives up after 5 s on an owner that does not answer (ClipboardBusy; a write that timed out may still land, and says so). set_clipboard writes CF_UNICODETEXT with ExcludeClipboardContentFromMonitorProcessing, CanIncludeInClipboardHistory = 0 and CanUploadToCloudClipboard = 0. get_clipboard refuses content carrying any of the three privacy markers (ClipboardPrivate; a marker that cannot be read counts as set; private content is not even read into the server) except set_clipboard's own text, which carries them on purpose. Server-initiated copies: `IClipboardOrigin` (ClipboardOrigin) records set_clipboard's sequence number, and InputSimulationService tells it about every batch that can copy (Ctrl+C, Ctrl+X, Ctrl+Insert or Shift+Delete with any other modifiers, or a left-button press, as on a Copy menu item); a change of GetClipboardSequenceNumber within 5 s after such a batch is the agent's copy. A timer reads the sequence number every 50 ms during that window, because the window must bound when the clipboard changed, not when the agent reads it (agents often read many seconds later); a read right after the copy keys waits up to 1 s for the application to copy. Everything else is ClipboardNotFromAgent, without reading it. The user's opt-in is `WAM_ALLOW_USER_CLIPBOARD=1` (server environment) or a ticked yes to an elicitation question that shows no content; the yes is pinned to the sequence number at question time, so content copied while the user answers is not read. No model-set flag exists. New `ClipboardErrorCode` (ClipboardNotFromAgent, ClipboardPrivate, FormatNotAvailable with availableFormats, ClipboardBusy, ClipboardFailed, ConfirmationDeclined, InvalidFormat). HTML returns the CF_HTML fragment and its sourceUrl (read whole up to 16 MiB, since Office puts long style sheets before the fragment); files returns the CF_HDROP paths. Paste-mode typing stays with M4.4.

**Review follow-up (`m2-scroll-clipboard`).** Two reviews found the copy window too wide: every left click opened a renewable 5 s window in which the latest change won, so a copy the user made shortly after any agent click or copy keys was returned as the agent's without consent (and a prompt-injected agent could keep the window open with harmless clicks), while a later click hid the agent's finished copy as pending. ClipboardOrigin now narrows the attribution: a mark carries its trigger (copy keys or click) and the process of the window the input went to (the guard's receiving window, for UWP the app's process), and a change counts only when the clipboard's owner window (new read `INativeClipboardWrapper.GetOwnerProcessId`: GetClipboardOwner plus GetWindowThreadProcessId, no open, classified Read in the E2E catalog) belongs to that process, read between two equal sequence numbers. Only the first such change counts, plus further changes by the same process within 250 ms; any other change after it ends the window. The window is 5 s after copy keys and 2 s after a click, only copy keys make a read wait (IsCopyPending), and copy keys that copied nothing are the new `ClipboardErrorCode.NothingCopied` = 8 without a question to the user, also when the clipboard still holds an earlier agent copy (returning it would look like the new copy). Known limit: a WebView2 app's clipboard is written by its msedgewebview2 helper process, so it falls back to the consent question. The set_clipboard exemption for marked content now needs both the number and the text: the wrapper reads marked content only while the open clipboard has the recorded number (`Read(format, max, privateContentSequence)`), the write reports 0 when its owner window no longer owns the clipboard after the number was read, and the service returns content numbered as set_clipboard's only when it is that text (no HTML or files). A user's no is remembered for that content: get_clipboard does not ask again until the clipboard changes. The scroll argument check no longer overflows on int.MinValue, and the scroll docs say that under focus routing the focused control in the window receives the wheel (the guard does not compare controls).

**Final review (`m2-fix-privacy`).** Four privacy findings. (1) A paste followed by a read (get_ui_elements, a capture, get_browser_content) disclosed what get_clipboard refuses, a password manager's copy included (PRIVA-1). New ClipboardPasteGuard: input that pastes (Ctrl+V with any other modifiers, Shift+Insert, the SYN character U+0016 in type_text, and a right click in a terminal: ConsoleWindowClass, Windows Terminal, PuTTY, KiTTY, mintty, ConEmu) is sent only when the clipboard holds set_clipboard's text, the agent's own copy, or content the user let get_clipboard read (a yes for that content, or WAM_ALLOW_USER_CLIPBOARD), and never content marked private unless set_clipboard put it there; otherwise the new InputErrorCode.PasteNotAllowed (44), nothing sent. Win+V (clipboard history) needs WAM_ALLOW_USER_CLIPBOARD; send_keys cannot send it today (no Windows-key modifier), so that branch is a safety net. A clipboard that cannot be checked refuses the paste. Not covered, and documented: a paste from an application's menu, drag and drop, custom paste shortcuts, clipboard history opened by clicks (the emoji panel's or touch keyboard's clipboard tab), and a right click in terminals outside the class list (VS Code's integrated terminal and other Electron terminals, Alacritty, WezTerm). The check reads the clipboard before the call's input; a call that copies and then pastes was at first refused only when the clipboard held content the agent may not paste, so `^c^v` after set_clipboard pasted the new copy unchecked. `m2-last-fixes` refuses every call in which copy keys (or the ETX or CAN character) come before a paste, whatever the clipboard holds, and a paste while copy keys of an earlier call have not copied yet (they wait ahead of it in the application's queue); a copy and its paste in separate calls are checked as any paste. (2) The agent's copy input could keep a copy window open (PRIVA-2): a window now runs from the first input of a run and later input to the same application joins it without moving its start, so it lasts at most 5 s (2 s with only clicks); after a window that ends without a copy, copy input to that application opens no new window until it has been quiet for the window's length, doubled for each further empty window in a row, at most 60 s (empty click windows hold back only clicks), and a copy the application makes for the agent clears the backoff; a copy counts only while the window the input went to (or its target, or a menu's owner) is in front. A real copy clearing the backoff means mixing real copies with clicks can still keep windows open, limited to copies the user makes in the window the agent drives, a documented residual risk. (3) One get_clipboard question is open at a time, server-wide (PRIVA-3): a second call while one waits returns ConfirmationDeclined without asking. (4) get_clipboard is audited (PRIVA-4, M2.17).

**Follow-up (`m3-m2-followups`, M2.F2).** A right click pasted unchecked in terminals whose window class is not their own. ClipboardOrigin.IsRightClickPasteWindow(class, process) now also counts WezTerm's default class `org.wezfurlong.wezterm` (from its CLI docs; `--class` replaces it) and, by process name (RightClickPasteProcesses, any window of the process): `Code`, `Code - Insiders`, `VSCodium`, `Cursor`, `Windsurf`, `Hyper` and `Tabby` (their class, Chrome_WidgetWin_1, is every Chromium browser's, so the class alone cannot decide), `alacritty` (Alacritty sets no class on Windows, so winit's default `Window Class`, shared by every winit app, is its class) and `wezterm-gui`. The server cannot tell an editor's terminal panel from its editor, nor read a terminal's right-click setting (VS Code's `terminal.integrated.rightClickBehavior` defaults to copyPaste on Windows; whether Alacritty, WezTerm, Hyper or Tabby paste depends on their mouse settings), so the rule errs on the side of refusing: a right click anywhere in those windows follows the paste rule. ClipboardPasteGuard reads the receiving window's process name only for a right click. Not covered: an Electron terminal or editor of another name, and a terminal renamed or started under another process name.

*Sources: r1:R31, r1:crit-prio:R31, r1:crit:R31, r2:R35, r2:crit-prio:R35, r2:crit:R35, r1:INP-G5, r2:INP-G5*

### M2.17 Audit log of injected input and destructive actions

**Priority:** P2 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m2-audit-log` (Done; final-review fixes on `m2-fix-integration` and `m2-fix-privacy`)

When an action does damage, neither the user nor the agent can reconstruct what was sent where. A cheap append-only log builds trust in a server that types into the desktop.

**Design.** The call filter writes JSONL to %LOCALAPPDATA%/WindowsAppManagerMcp/audit/<date>.jsonl, one line per input, close, kill, launch or layout action: {ts, tool, target handle/title/process, receivingWindow, outcome, errorCode, durationMs}. It never logs type_text or set_clipboard content, only its length, and never logs secrets. Retention rolls over 7 days, and get_server_info reports the path.

**As shipped (`m2-audit-log`).** Infrastructure/AuditLogToolFilter is the outermost call-tool filter (added before ToolErrorReporting), so a line records the result the agent got: an exception the error filter turned into an error result, an InvalidArguments binding failure, and a call the gate filter refused while paused, each with its errorCode; a cancelled call is `cancelled` and a protocol error (a tool turned off) `protocolError`. It does nothing when no IAuditLog is registered, so tests with their own services write nothing. Audited (AuditedTools): the six input tools plus release_all_input, close_window, close_windows_batch, kill_process, the three launch tools, apply_layout, and, refinement, save_layout and delete_layout (they replace or remove a preset, so they count as layout actions), plus scroll, set_clipboard and close_application from M2.15, M2.16 and M2.5, audited as soon as they are served. The captures are left out on purpose (destructive only through overwrite:true, and observation called too often for their lines to help). Contract tests require every input tool and every destructive tool but the captures to be audited, no read-only tool to be (get_clipboard is the one exception since the final review, below), and every audited name to be served or one of the three planned ones; every audited tool's typical call writes exactly one line with none of its string arguments in it. Services/AuditLog (IAuditLog) reads the targets before the call through INativeWindowWrapper (IsWindow, GetWindowText, GetWindowThreadProcessId, GetProcessName; no new native member), because a closed window or killed process cannot be read afterwards, and writes `<folder>\<yyyy-MM-dd>.jsonl` (local day): {ts (local, with offset), tool, outcome, errorCode, durationMs, target {handle, title, process, processId, executable}, receivingWindow, items (batches, with titles read before the call), args, result, serverPid, requestId}. Refinement on secrets: Infrastructure/AuditRecord summarizes the arguments default-deny against the tool's declared input schema (the filter reads it from the server's tool collection) rather than per tool: an undeclared argument is only counted (`unknownArgumentsCount`, so a client-chosen key never reaches the file), a number or boolean is kept only where the schema declares one (a PIN sent as a number for `text` becomes `textLength`), strings only when they name a process, preset or fixed value (NamedStringArguments) and otherwise as `<name>Length`, arrays as `<name>Count`, objects dropped. `executable` keeps a program name or file path; a URL keeps only its scheme, host and port (a path can be a bearer secret: webhook, password-reset, magic-login and share links; an opaque `data:` or `javascript:` body is content), a scheme-less `www.host/...` only its host, with `executableLength` when something was cut. So type_text, send_keys and set_clipboard text, URL paths and queries, working directories and launch arguments never reach the file, and a new tool or parameter cannot leak by default. The filter also guards IAuditLog's Begin and End itself, so even a log that throws cannot fail a call, and End runs at most once. The E2E ServerHarness writes its audit log inside its temporary layouts folder. Of a result, only top-level numbers and booleans, errorCode, receivingWindow and batch items are kept. Several servers share a day's file: each line is one write while the file is open for writing by that writer alone (FileShare.Read|Delete), retried for about half a second on a sharing violation, so lines never interleave. Retention: the first line of each day deletes day files 7 or more days old (only names matching yyyy-MM-dd.jsonl). Nothing touches the disk before the first line. A failed write never fails or changes the call: LastError keeps the reason, stderr gets one warning per run of failures, and get_server_info warns. The folder is %LOCALAPPDATA%\WindowsAppManagerMcp\audit or WAM_AUDIT_PATH (AuditLogOptions; AddWindowsAppManagerServices takes an options factory, as for layouts), and get_server_info reports it as paths.auditLog. Not done: an opt-out switch and a configurable retention; the stderr call log (ToolErrorReporting) is unchanged.

**Final review (`m2-fix-integration`, `m2-fix-privacy`).** The log read only a batch's `results` and the handles in the arguments, so close_application lines named no window, apply_layout lines no placement or launch, and a close named by a selector only a bare handle. AuditScope lets a tool note the windows it learns of while it runs (the window a selector resolves to, close_application's targets) in the call's record before it acts on them, with nothing extra read; AuditRecord reads close_application's `windows` and apply_layout's `placements` as items, with status, `launched` and the launch program (redacted like `executable`), and names a selector's window by its noted title and process. Writing: one background writer per server appends the lines in order, and End waits for its line at most 2 s (AuditLog.MaxCallDelay), so a slow or unreachable folder never holds up a call; a line beyond 1000 waiting is dropped with LastError. The half-second retry on a file another server holds stays. get_clipboard is audited (PRIVA-4): the one read-only tool that can return the user's own clipboard, recorded with its `format` and the result's `origin` and `length`, never the content; AuditLogContractTests allows exactly that read-only tool. Known limit: End blocks a thread-pool thread for up to 2 s while the writer, also on the pool, may wait for one, so under heavy parallel load a call can wait the full 2 s; the wait is bounded.

**Follow-up (`m3-m2-followups`, M2.F5).** End blocked a pool thread for up to 2 s (Enqueue(...).Wait) while the writer waited for a pool thread too, so under heavy parallel load an audited call could wait the full 2 s. IAuditLog.End is now EndAsync: the call awaits its line with Task.WaitAsync(MaxCallDelay), holding no thread, and the writer runs on a background thread of its own (Thread.UnsafeStart, so the call's audit scope does not flow; the pool only if no thread can be started). AuditLogToolFilter awaits it and also bounds it at MaxCallDelay, so even a log that breaks its contract (a faulted or never-ending task) cannot hold up or break a call. The bounded wait, the order of lines and the never-break-a-call guarantees are unchanged.

*Sources: r2:crit-missing:audit-log, r1:R8*

## M3 Semantic find-act-wait

The biggest lever. Move from blind coordinates to selector-addressed, pattern-first UI Automation, so 'click Save in app X' becomes 1-2 calls that can each be verified. Elements are found by name, automationId and type, including in menus, popups, dialogs, Chromium/Electron apps and virtualized lists. Agents act through UIA patterns with verified post-state, read text and tables directly, and wait server-side instead of sleeping.

M3 is complete on the integration branch `m3-semantic-find-act-wait` (PR #6): all 14 items, built in four waves of parallel branches, each with its own implementer and an independent reviewer (A: `m3-uia-engine`, `m3-wait-for-window`, `m3-launch-arrange`; B: `m3-chromium-warmup`, `m3-element-refs`, `m3-capture-registry`; C: `m3-popup-search`, `m3-element-action`, `m3-wait-for-element`, `m3-read-text-tables`; D: `m3-virtualized`, `m3-desktop-state`, `m3-dialogs`, `m3-set-of-marks`), the M2 follow-ups (`m3-m2-followups`, `m3-force-confirm`), the fixes from a review of the integrated branch (`m3-fix-policy`, `m3-fix-refs`, `m3-fix-reading`, `m3-fix-action`, `m3-fix-dialogs`, `m3-fix-waits`, `m3-fix-surface`, `m3-fix-launch`, `m3-fix-release-gate`, `m3-fix-failclosed`) and a docs pass. The server serves 64 tools (54 in 1.14.0). The safe suite (desktop tests skipped) has about 6,450 unit, contract and integration tests and about 200 E2E harness tests; the 55 gated fixture scenarios run only with the opt-in. None of the M3 behaviour has run against a real desktop yet: every new UI Automation path is tested through fakes, and the gated fixture scenarios wait for the user's runs.

- M3 is to ship in release 1.15.0. PR #6 is merged with a merge commit, not a squash (the release check must find the build commit), and the release commit rebuilds bin/WindowsAppManagerMcp.exe (scripts/Set-Version.ps1, then scripts/Publish-Plugin.ps1), as for 1.13.0 and 1.14.0. **Release blocker:** the default UI Automation engine is now uia3 (M3.2), which has never read a real desktop. Before the release the user runs the whole gated no-input tier twice on a real desktop, once with WAM_UIA_ENGINE unset (uia3) and once with WAM_UIA_ENGINE=uia2, as [TESTING.md, "Release gate: the UI Automation engines"](../TESTING.md#release-gate-the-ui-automation-engines) describes; an agent never runs it. If uia3 and uia2 differ, UiaEngineOptions.Default goes back to uia2 for the release. docs/TOOLS.md lists what a client sees change under "Changes for existing clients".
- Thirteen reviewers looked at the integrated branch (at 8c2db66), each by area and lens, and a second reviewer checked every finding: 88 held up. The fix round, by area: the target policy, popups and privacy (`m3-fix-policy`, then `m3-fix-failclosed`), element refs and search (`m3-fix-refs`), the text and table reads (`m3-fix-reading`), element_action and the input guard (`m3-fix-action`), the dialog tools and menu paths (`m3-fix-dialogs`), the waits (`m3-fix-waits`), captures, the UI Automation engine, get_desktop_state and the tool surface (`m3-fix-surface`), launch and web content (`m3-fix-launch`) and the release-gate scenarios (`m3-fix-release-gate`). It fixed the findings except the few listed below, which it deferred or the repo owner left for later; the items below say what changed.
- The repo owner's decisions applied in the fix round: the UI Automation reads of a denied app's window are refused (TargetDeniedByPolicy), and SecHealthUI and CredDialogHost joined the default deny list with CHXSmartScreen and smartscreen (M1.12); respond_to_dialog presses only in a dialog the agent named, except cancel (M3.5); element_action's `name` criterion is audited as its length (M3.6); a ref in a recycled list row goes stale, and a ref moved to a re-created element is reported (refMoved) and element_action refuses it (M3.4); reuseExisting reuses a window that shows only the name with a warning, and never reuses by a page's host without expectWindow.titleContains (M3.13); the acting tools refuse an argument they do not declare (M3.6); the release gate is two full no-input runs, and the fixture app has a PasswordBox (M3.2). Left for later: a stableMs for wait_for_element and a guard on the programs launch_application runs (BACKLOG.md).
- Deferred from the review, with the reason, in BACKLOG.md under the M3 follow-ups: element_action's keyboard path is not atomic (it needs a focus recheck inside the input gate, M3.6), the maxScrolls fallback needs exactly one visible list (it needs a list parameter, M3.10), and WebView2 in visual hosting gets no warm-up (it needs a real desktop and a WebView2 fixture pane, M3.3). A security prompt drawn inside a shared process such as explorer.exe cannot be denied by process name (M1.12).
- Moved on: event wake-ups for both waits (M7.2), the UI Automation path of the browser reading tools and the warm-up there (M5.7, D-BRW-15), drag and mouse_down with captureIds (M4.3), a diagnostic for an elevated target's empty tree (D-UIA-07, M6.6), placing apply_layout's launches by monitor identity (M6.7, M6.8), and the follow-ups listed under M3 in BACKLOG.md.

### M3.1 Bounded, cached UIA walks with partial results and compact output

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** improve · **Branch:** `m3-uia-engine` (Done)

Each element currently costs 8-10 uncached cross-process calls, a timeout throws away everything, and payloads are unbounded. Make reads fast, bounded and useful even when incomplete.

**Design.** Build one CacheRequest covering Name, ControlType, AutomationId, ClassName, BoundingRectangle, IsEnabled, IsOffscreen, IsKeyboardFocusable, RuntimeId and the Is*PatternAvailable flags, activated around the walk. Navigate with ControlViewWalker and read cached values. Walk breadth-first with maxElements (default 300) and a deadline, returning Success=true with Truncated, TruncationReason and SkippedElements. Pre-check IsHungAppWindow and SendMessageTimeout(WM_NULL, SMTO_ABORTIFHUNG, 500) to return WindowNotResponding. Reads run on one dedicated UIA worker with a bounded queue; pattern calls that can block never run there (see M3.6). format=flat|tree|text, where text is one line per element, e.g. `w1e12 Button 'Save' aid=SaveBtn @812,44 80x24 [disabled]`. interactableOnly = IsEnabled && (IsKeyboardFocusable || any action pattern).

**As shipped (`m3-uia-engine`, with M3.2).** Already done before this item (M1.8, M1.9, M2.13): partial results on a timeout and on an abandoned walk (truncated, truncationReason Timeout or ElementsSkipped, skippedElementCount), one bad element skipped instead of failing the walk, empty or non-finite bounds as null, interactableOnly as enabled and (keyboard-focusable or an action pattern), the walk on a UiaWorker thread with the deadline plus a 2 s grace, and the MCP request's cancellation linked with the timeout. New: one cache request per walk. Each element's children are first listed by reference (IUiaTree.GetChildren, one FindAll that reads no property). A level of at most 200 children (UIElementService.CachedLevelLimit) is then fetched with the walk's properties (Name, ControlType and LocalizedControlType, AutomationId, ClassName, BoundingRectangle, IsEnabled, IsOffscreen, IsKeyboardFocusable, RuntimeId and the 15 Is*PatternAvailable flags) in one more cross-process call (IUiaTree.GetChildElements: FindAllBuildCache under UIA3, FindAll under an activated CacheRequest under UIA2), so interactableOnly costs no further call. A wider level is read one element at a time (one BuildUpdatedCache each under UIA3), so maxElements and the timeout stop the walk in the middle of it. Refinement of the design (review of `m3-uia-engine`): the design navigated with ControlViewWalker. One fetch per level is far fewer calls, but its cost grows with the level: a fetch of thousands of children with 25 properties each could outlast the 5 s transaction timeout or the deadline and lose the level, or fail the call when it is the window's own level. So it is only used for a level the cheap listing showed to be small. The listing keeps what a walker would lose (a child that disappears during the read cannot end the level early); its own cost still grows with the level, but it reads no property, as the walk before M3 did. When the one-call fetch fails (a provider that fails for one child can fail it), the children are read one at a time as before, so one bad element still costs only itself; a TimeoutException is not retried, so a slow provider is not waited for twice. The walk is breadth first, and get_ui_elements stops it at maxElements (default 300, 1-5000) returned elements with the new truncationReason MaxElements, set only when something was left unread: an element of the level being read, or a child of an element still waiting, which the walk checks for (one call per waiting element, until one has a child; when the deadline passes or a call fails first, truncation is assumed; since the M3 review a first-child read, see below). A level fetched in one call when the timeout passes is kept whole (it costs no further call); elements read one at a time stop with the timeout. Hung-window precheck before any UI Automation call: IsHungAppWindow, then SendMessageTimeout(WM_NULL, SMTO_ABORTIFHUNG, 500 ms) through the new INativeWindowWrapper.RespondsWithin (WM_NULL only; allow-listed as a window query in NativeCallGuardTests and classified Read in the E2E catalog; it returns true, false only for ERROR_TIMEOUT, and null for any other failure, such as UIPI's ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED, which says nothing about the thread, so the read goes ahead under its deadline), failing with NotResponding (UIElementErrorCode 10, the name the window tools already use, so the error code reference gains no row). A handle that is no window is left to the lookup (WindowNotFound), and a check that throws does not stop the read. capture_with_elements gets the precheck through the same service (the image is still captured, elementsErrorCode NotResponding) but keeps its walk unlimited. format=tree|flat|text (UIElementFormat, strict, InvalidArguments otherwise): flat adds depth and parent (an index) to each element, text replaces elements with one line per element (Tools/UIElementTextFormat; the control type comes first so the refs of M3.4 can go in front of it). Refinement: the design's single dedicated UIA worker with a bounded queue is not adopted. UiaWorker keeps a thread per read with per-application stuck counts, because one shared worker would let a read stuck in a hung application delay every read of every other application (and, with M3.6, an Invoke blocked by its own modal), while the UIA3 transaction timeout now returns a stuck read in about 5 s. Not done: per-node HasMoreChildren at the depth limit (one more call per element at the limit; depthReached equal to depth already says there may be more), collapseStructural and leaving out default-valued fields, and warnings for minimized or elevated targets (D-UIA-07; an elevated window is left to M6.6); BACKLOG.md lists them under the M3 follow-ups. capture_with_elements got maxElements with M3.11 part 2 (default 80) and no text format: its `format` stays the image format, and `detail` (legend or full) decides what its metadata carries. TOOLS.md lists what clients see change under "Changes for existing clients".

**Review fixes (M3 review: `m3-fix-surface`, `m3-fix-policy`, `m3-fix-reading`).** The MaxElements check asks each waiting element for a first child (new IUiaTree.HasChildren: a control-view tree walker's first child under both engines, so at most one element is read however wide the level), for at most 32 waiting elements (UIElementService.MaxChildProbes), and then assumes something was left unread; it used to list each waiting element's whole level, and IUiaTree.GetChildren no longer calls that listing cheap for a wide level. The hung-window precheck (UiaTargets.NotRespondingReason, which every UI Automation read shares) also probes the CoreWindow a UWP frame (ApplicationFrameWindow) hosts, with the same IsHungAppWindow and WM_NULL checks (new UwpFrames.FindHostedCoreWindow): the frame's thread is ApplicationFrameHost's, which answers for every UWP app, while the app's elements are served by its CoreWindow's thread. The reads refuse a window of an app the target policy denies, and one whose process cannot be named while the policy denies anything, with TargetDeniedByPolicy before the warm-up or any UI Automation call (UiaTargets.ReadVerdict, M1.12). Every tool result is written with the relaxed JSON encoder (M3.9), so element names in other scripts and the quotes of the text format are no longer six-byte escapes, and the description of get_ui_elements' format shows the ref first and fw= in its example line, as the text format writes them.

*Sources: r2:R20, r1:R47, r1:R12, r1:crit:R44, r1:UIA-4, r1:UIA-G7, r1:UIA-G8, r2:UIA-2, r2:UIA-4, r2:UIA-5, r2:UIA-7, r2:UIA-G7*

### M3.2 UIA3 (FlaUI) backend behind a widened seam, before refs, actions and waits

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** L · **Kind:** improve · **Branch:** `m3-uia-engine` (Done)

The managed client has no per-call timeouts, cannot read newer control-type ids and has no LegacyIAccessible. Building refs, patterns, events and waits on it and porting them later doubles the work, so swap the engine behind the seam first. This is a time-boxed spike.

**Design.** Widen IUiaTree to cover patterns, events and cache requests, not just traversal. Implement it on FlaUI.UIA3 or a prebuilt Interop.UIAutomationClient, not a COMReference, which dotnet build cannot resolve. Set IUIAutomation2 ConnectionTimeout to about 1000 ms and TransactionTimeout to about 5000 ms, which gives hard deadlines for hung providers and for an Invoke blocked by its own modal. Use BuildUpdatedCache(TreeScope_Subtree), read raw control-type ids (50039/50040) and add LegacyIAccessible DoDefaultAction. A config switch keeps the managed UIA2 adapter as the fallback; tool contracts do not change, and the M1.11 fixture suite runs against both adapters. If the spike fails, M3.4-M3.8 proceed on the managed adapter behind the same seam.

**As shipped (`m3-uia-engine`).** Package: Interop.UIAutomationClient 10.19041.0 (MIT, from the FlaUI author; one managed interop assembly of UIAutomationClient.idl, about 150 KB, no native code and no dependencies) rather than FlaUI.UIA3 5.0.0 (MIT too, but it adds FlaUI.Core's element, pattern and cache-request layer over the same interop assembly, which would duplicate the IUiaTree seam). Its build target keeps EmbedInteropTypes off; a framework-dependent single-file `dotnet publish` (win-x64, as scripts/Publish-Plugin.ps1 runs it) bundles the assembly into the exe and still produces only the exe and its pdb, and built-in COM interop needs no trimming exceptions. The seam: IUiaTree gains Engine, GetChildElements, FindAll(root, scope, UiaCondition) (property conditions on Name, AutomationId, ClassName, ControlType by id, FrameworkId, ProcessId, IsEnabled, IsOffscreen and IsKeyboardFocusable, with And, Or, Not and ignore-case), ReadSubtree (BuildUpdatedCache with a subtree scope, capped breadth first), GetRuntimeId, AreSameElement and GetPattern<T> over 15 pattern interfaces (Invoke, Toggle, Value, ExpandCollapse, SelectionItem, ScrollItem, Scroll, RangeValue, Window, Text, Grid, Table, ItemContainer, VirtualizedItem and LegacyIAccessible, each with its state reads and actions; a read-only GridItem joined them in the M3 review, M3.9); the cached reads return UiaElement snapshots (properties, class, focusability, UiaPatterns flags, runtime id and the raw control type id). Native/Uia3Tree implements it over CUIAutomation8: IUIAutomation2 ConnectionTimeout 1000 ms and TransactionTimeout 5000 ms, set when the client is created on first use (lazily, under a lock, on an MTA thread); four cache requests (walk, subtree, interaction, identity) with ControlViewCondition as TreeFilter and Full mode, and ControlViewCondition as the find condition; Read, ReadIdentity, ReadInteraction and IsInteractable cost one BuildUpdatedCache each instead of a call per property; raw control type ids name 50039 (SemanticZoom) and 50040 (AppBar) in every display language (UiaControlTypes.GetName(int)) and conditions compare them; the all-zero BoundingRectangle of an element without geometry is the empty rectangle, so it has no bounds in either engine; COM errors become the seam's exceptions (UiaComErrors: UIA_E_ELEMENTNOTAVAILABLE, RPC disconnects and a destroyed window become UiaElementUnavailableException, UIA_E_ELEMENTNOTENABLED InvalidOperationException, UIA_E_NOTSUPPORTED NotSupportedException; the runtime already maps UIA_E_TIMEOUT to TimeoutException). Native/UiaTree, the managed client, implements the widened seam too, except LegacyIAccessible (null) and conditions on AppBar and SemanticZoom (NotSupportedException). AutoSetFocus stays at its default (on, as the managed client behaves); M3.6 decides for its actions. The switch is WAM_UIA_ENGINE=uia3|uia2 (UiaEngineOptions, read at start; another value warns and keeps the default), reported by get_server_info as uiAutomation (engine, source and the two timeouts). Default: uia3.
