The spike found no blocker in the build, the packaging, the license or the adapter's unit tests, and the hard per-call deadlines, raw ids and one-call cached reads are what M3.4-M3.10 build on; the user can fall back to uia2 without a new build. Guards: NativeCallGuardTests lets only UIAutomationWrapper and the two adapters call UI Automation actions and only Uia3Tree call the COM client, and keeps UIElementService, ElementInspectionService and WindowCloseVerifier off both clients; the E2E WrapperCatalog classifies every new member, with a new UiaAction kind for the pattern actions that no tier allowed until M3.6's fixture rule (the input tier, on elements of the fixture process only), and the guard wraps the pattern objects GetPattern<T> returns (WrapperGuardTests). Tests: Uia3Tree is unit tested over Moq mocks of the COM interfaces (no real client is created), the ids against the managed client's own, and FakeUiaTree supports the widened seam. Gated E2E scenarios (FixtureUiaEngineTests, not run in this change) read the fixture with both engines and compare the trees; ServerHarness and E2ESession take the engine. Limits of the spike: nothing ran against a live desktop, so the control view that FindAllBuildCache(Children, ControlViewCondition) returns compared with the managed FindAll, bounds equality and the behaviour against a hung provider rest on those scenarios. Release blocker: no release ships the uia3 default until the gated no-input tier has passed with both engines on a real desktop, run by the user (since the M3 review two full runs of the tier, see below); if they differ, UiaEngineOptions.Default goes back to uia2 for that release (BACKLOG.md, M3.F1). Review fixes: Uia3Tree.GetPattern<T> builds the wrapper of the requested pattern type, not of the first pattern interface the COM object answers for (a table pattern object is often a grid pattern object too), and ReadIsModal reads only CurrentIsModal, one call as with the managed client, so a provider that fails the Window pattern's other properties no longer ends WindowCloseVerifier's in-window prompt search. IUiaTree documents that FindAll and ReadSubtree are bounded only in what they return, not in what they read. Not done: events (CoalesceEvents; M3.7 and M3.8 poll, and event wake-ups are left to M7.2) and moving UIAutomationWrapper's browser reads onto the seam (M5.7). LegacyIAccessible's DoDefaultAction became element_action's fallback for invoke and click (M3.6).

**Review fixes (M3 review: `m3-fix-surface`, `m3-fix-release-gate`).** Both engines raise the same seam exceptions: the managed client passes most UI Automation HRESULTs on as a COMException, which UiaTree now translates as the UIA3 engine does (UiaComErrors.Translate: UIA_E_NOTSUPPORTED becomes NotSupportedException, RPC_E_DISCONNECTED and the other disconnects UiaElementUnavailableException). The managed client's AppBar and SemanticZoom conditions throw a new UiaConditionNotSupportedException, which the searches answer by testing the control types on the server, so a NotSupportedException now only means a provider that does not support a call: a search skips and counts that part (PartsSkipped), where a search of the named window failed with OperationException, and element_action answers PatternNotSupported under both engines. The release gate was made able to see a difference. FixtureUiaEngineTests compared only display properties of the walk, so a real-desktop run never read what only the UIA3 engine reads from its own cache requests (the process, window handle and password flag, focus, patterns, the cached runtime id a ref needs, and the IgnoreCase, RuntimeId and control type conditions). The tree comparison now includes each element's framework and whether it has a ref; a new theory checks with each engine that find_ui_elements at detail full finds every fixture control by control type and the check box by its automation id in other letter case, each with the fixture's process, a click point in its bounds and a ref that resolves live; a new BothEngines_ fact compares the two engines' results field by field (patterns by name without LegacyIAccessible, which uia3 reports on nearly every element and uia2 never does); and the virtualized list theory checks the cached runtime id against the live one. The fixture app has a WPF PasswordBox, so the run shows that each engine reports the password flag that keeps find_ui_elements and read_element_text from reading a password. FixtureUiElementsTests runs each scenario with both engines (it read with the server's default only), E2ESession.Start writes the engine and its source to each scenario's output and refuses an unknown WAM_UIA_ENGINE, the warm-up scenario checks only the fixture's own elements (the WPF window's title bar comes from the Win32 proxy and names its own framework), and ungated Uia3TreeTests pin ReadIdentity, ReadInteraction and IsInteractable. The gate became two full runs of the no-input tier (`Category=DesktopNoInput`), with WAM_UIA_ENGINE unset and with WAM_UIA_ENGINE=uia2, so the scenarios that leave the engine to the server run under both ([TESTING.md, "Release gate: the UI Automation engines"](../TESTING.md#release-gate-the-ui-automation-engines)): a test that fails in one run only, or a BothEngines_ fact that fails, is a difference, and the release then ships with uia2 as the default.

*Sources: r1:R47, r1:crit-prio:R47, r2:R25, r1:crit:R42, r1:UIA-G9, r2:UIA-G9, r1:UIA-2, r1:UIA-3*

### M3.3 Chromium, Electron and WebView2 accessibility activation

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m3-chromium-warmup` (Done)

VS Code, Slack, Teams, Discord, the new Outlook and WebView2 panes build their web accessibility tree lazily. The first query sees an empty Chrome_RenderWidgetHostHWND, and the agent wrongly concludes the control is absent.

**Design.** In the UIA adapter, detect Chrome_WidgetWin_*, Chrome_RenderWidgetHostHWND and WebView2 host classes. On the first query of such a window, touch the Document element, then poll for up to about 1.5 s in 200-500 ms steps until the Document has children or its child count stops changing. Report AccessibilityWarmup or accessibilityNotReady instead of returning a silently thin tree. launch_application gains forceAccessibility, which appends --force-renderer-accessibility for Chromium and Electron executables. An optional, opt-in enable_screen_reader_flag sets SPI_SETSCREENREADER; it is documented as a global side effect and restored on shutdown. Element results carry frameworkId (Chrome, XAML, WPF, Win32, WinForm).

**As shipped (`m3-chromium-warmup`).** New IAccessibilityWarmup/AccessibilityWarmup (Services), used by UIElementService (get_ui_elements, capture_with_elements) and ElementInspectionService (get_focused_element on the foreground window, get_element_at_point on the hit window, before the hit test), registered once in ServiceRegistration and passed as an optional constructor parameter (the services work without it). It runs inside the read on the UiaWorker thread, under the read's token, so the per-application stuck count and the deadline cover it. Detection is Win32 only (no message to the app): a window counts when it, or a visible window inside it (EnumChildWindows, at most 2000 windows, at most 4 pages), has the class Chrome_RenderWidgetHostHWND. Refinement of the design: it keys on the render widget rather than on Chrome_WidgetWin_* alone, because a Chrome_WidgetWin_* window without one (a menu, a popup or a tooltip of the browser frame) has no page and would wait 1.5 s for nothing; a WebView2 pane is found the same way inside any host (its render widget belongs to msedgewebview2.exe, reported as host WebView2), hidden render widgets (background tabs) are skipped, and the desktop window (#32769) is never warmed up, since its children are every application's windows. The warm-up asks UIA for each render widget's element (which makes Chromium turn its page tree on), takes it as the Document or finds the Document within three levels of panes, groups and customs below it (at most 12 cached child reads), and counts the Document's children; a count above zero at the first check is taken at once (the tree was already built), otherwise it checks again after 200, 300, 400 and then 500 ms steps, cut to the budget, until the count is above zero and equal to the previous one. Budget: min(1.5 s, a third of the call's timeout) (AccessibilityWarmup.BudgetFor; UIElementFilter.AccessibilityWarmupBudget, set by UIElementTools.ReadTreeAsync, so capture_with_elements gets it too), and the token ends it sooner. A provider TimeoutException ends the wait at once; any other failure counts as "not found yet"; the warm-up never throws. Result: AccessibilityWarmupInfo {status Ready|Partial|NotReady, host Chromium|WebView2, waitedMs, polls, documentChildCount, warning} as `accessibilityWarmup` on UIElementResult (also on a timeout or an abandoned walk), FocusedElementResult, ElementAtPointResult (also on ElementNotFound) and capture_with_elements' metadata (with or without overlays). It is not an error: success and isError are unchanged; the warning says to read again and, for Chromium, to relaunch with forceAccessibility. Cache: windows that got a page (Ready or Partial) are remembered and not warmed up again (no field then; since the M3 review a page shown since is, see below); at most 256, dead windows dropped on every insert, the oldest dropped when full, and an entry whose handle now belongs to another process is dropped on lookup. A window that stayed empty is waited for again at most three times (MaxWaitingAttempts), then only checked, so an empty page does not cost every read 1.5 s. frameworkId: UiaNodeProperties gains FrameworkId (both engines' walk cache requests read it, so a cached level costs no extra call; UIA2's one-element Read reads one more property). get_ui_elements reports the window's at the top (UIElementResult.FrameworkId) and an element's only where it differs from the returned element it is inside (filtered-out elements pass nothing on), `fw=` in the text format; this keeps the payload flat for one-framework windows (the ToolPayloadBudgetTests figures do not move). UIElementAncestor gains FrameworkId; UIElementDetails already had it. launch_application `forceAccessibility` (LaunchRequest.ForceAccessibility, also honoured by LaunchApplicationsBatchAsync; launch_applications_batch's items do not expose it yet): ChromiumPrograms recognizes a resolved, existing .exe with Chromium's icudtl.dat and resources.pak next to it (Electron, CEF) or in a version-named folder next to it (Chrome, Edge, Brave), never by name alone, and never an installer, updater or helper in the same folder (unins*, Update.exe, crashpad_handler, chrome_proxy, ...). PlanLaunch then puts --force-renderer-accessibility in front of the arguments (Chromium reads no switch after "--"), once (an existing --force-renderer-accessibility[=...] is kept); for a batch file (code.cmd), a URI, a document or any other program nothing is added, the launch goes ahead and the warning says why. The flag is added after argument validation and only for native executables, so the batch-file metacharacter rule is unaffected. LaunchResult/LaunchResultDto gain forceAccessibilityApplied (null when not asked or refused; false for alreadyOpen, a failed start or a non-Chromium target; true with a warning when the launch went to a running instance: possibleHandoff, reusedExistingWindow, openedInTab or launcherExited, which keeps its mode). enable_screen_reader_flag: deferred, not implemented. SPI_SETSCREENREADER is session-wide: every running app (Office, browsers, Electron apps) changes behaviour and cost while it is set; a server that is killed (the usual end of a stdio server) or crashes cannot restore it, so it would stay on for the session; two servers would race to restore it; and the SPIF_SENDCHANGE broadcast reaches every top-level window. With the warm-up and forceAccessibility covering the Chromium cases, the gain does not justify that, even behind a server-side opt-in. No new tools, error codes or native wrapper members. With M3.4 merged: ElementSearchService (find_ui_elements) takes the warm-up too (optional, after the window wrapper) and runs it after the window lookup, before withinRef and the search, within AccessibilityWarmup.BudgetFor of the search's timeout; FindElementsResult gains accessibilityWarmup. UIElementService and ElementInspectionService take the warm-up before the element registry, and a walked element carries both its ref and its frameworkId (where it changes). Gated scenario: FixtureUiElementsTests.GetUIElements_NamesTheWpfFramework_AndWarmsNothingUpInANativeWindow (not run). Not done: a WebView2 pane in the fixture app and a gated scenario against real Chromium content (the warm-up's poll against a live Chromium is unverified: whether FromHandle on the render widget returns the Document directly, or a pane above it, is handled both ways); forceAccessibility for launch_applications_batch items; the browser reading tools (M5.7) do not use the warm-up yet (BACKLOG.md, M3 follow-ups). M3.8 wait_for_element calls it once per wait.

**Review fixes (M3 review: `m3-fix-launch`, `m3-fix-policy`).** The warm-up remembered a window and then never looked at it again, but Chromium builds a hidden page's tree only once the page is shown, so a tab switched to right before a read was read cold, with no accessibilityWarmup. It now remembers the render widgets it warmed up with each window, still lists a remembered window's shown render widgets on every read (Win32 only, no UI Automation call), and warms up one it has not warmed up, such as a tab shown since; a window whose shown pages were all warmed up keeps the free path. forceAccessibility warns when the switch most likely has no effect: a launch that did not wait for its window reads the process list right before the start and warns when a process with the program's image path is running, since the new process hands its request to that instance, which keeps its mode; and both warnings say that no process of the app may be left, not only no window, since Edge's Startup boost and Chrome's background apps keep one running without a window. A window of an app the target policy denies is never warmed up: the reads refuse it first (M1.12). Left open (BACKLOG.md): WebView2 in visual hosting (CoreWebView2CompositionController, which the WinUI 3 WebView2 control uses) has no Chrome_RenderWidgetHostHWND, so its content is read cold with no signal; covering it needs a real desktop and a WebView2 fixture pane.

*Sources: r1:crit-missing:chromium-warmup, r2:crit-missing:web-accessibility, r1:R62, r1:R63, r2:R48, r2:BRW-missed-renderer-accessibility, r1:BRW-missed-2*

### M3.4 Element registry with stable refs and find_ui_elements

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m3-element-refs` (Done)

Search on the server by name, automationId, controlType and className, and get back refs that later calls can use. Stale or ambiguous refs return explicit errors, and a ref never silently resolves to a different element.

**Design.** IElementRegistry singleton. Refs are globally unique and never reused within a session (e.g. w<hwndSeq>e<n>), and the same element (same RuntimeId) keeps its ref across snapshots. Entries hold {element, RuntimeId, hwnd, selector, fingerprint (AutomationId, Name, ControlType, ClassName, index path), generation, lastUsed}, LRU of about 5000, dropped once !IsWindow(hwnd). Resolve order: a live read such as Current.ProcessId (GetRuntimeId is memoized and cannot detect staleness), then FindFirst by RuntimeIdProperty under the window, then re-run the stored selector and verify the fingerprint, then ElementStale ('call find_ui_elements again'). get_ui_elements and capture_with_elements emit refs. find_ui_elements(window, name?, nameMatch=exact|contains|regex, automationId?, controlType? (list), className?, withinRef?, scope (M3.5), visibleOnly=true, enabledOnly=false, maxResults=20, detail=minimal|standard|full) runs provider-side PropertyConditions, so it is not limited by maxDepth. It returns MatchCount, Truncated, refs, ClickPoint (TryGetClickablePoint, falling back to the bounds centre) and Patterns[]. detail=standard adds Value (at most 200 characters, masked when IsPassword), ToggleState, ExpandCollapseState, IsSelected and HasKeyboardFocus. detail=full adds FrameworkId, ProcessId, NativeWindowHandle, HelpText, AccessKey and LocalizedControlType. Under the managed client, unknown control types are matched client-side by class or LocalizedControlType.

*Sources: r1:R43, r2:R21, r1:crit:R43, r1:R46, r2:R24, r1:UIA-G2, r1:UIA-G3, r1:UIA-G5, r1:MCP-G2, r2:UIA-G1, r2:UIA-G2, r2:UIA-G5, r2:MSP-G1*

**As shipped (`m3-element-refs`).** *Registry* (Services/ElementRegistry.cs behind IElementRegistry, a singleton in ServiceRegistration). Refs are `w<window>e<element>`: a window gets its number the first time one of its elements is registered and the element numbers count up within it, so no ref is given twice in a session. Refinement: window numbers start at a random value in 1,000,000-8,999,999 per server session (ElementRegistry.FirstWindowNumberMin/Max; seven digits, which cost about one token per ref), so a ref an agent kept from before a restart is ElementStale with "not issued by this server session" unless the new session happens to number the same window: about 1 in 80,000 per remembered window for a session that numbers 100 windows. That is a probability, not a guarantee, and the docs say so; nothing of the old session survives to make it one. The same element keeps its ref: the key is the runtime id under the window, and a re-registration is accepted as the same element only with the same identity: control type, automation id and class (class compared only when both reads have one: a walk's one-at-a-time path has none), and for a ListItem, TreeItem or DataItem without an automation id also the name, because virtualizing lists (WinUI, WPF with recycling, web lists) reuse an item's element and runtime id for another row (ElementRegistry.NameIsIdentity). Another control that took a gone element's runtime id, or a recycled item showing another row, gets a new ref and the old one is dropped. An element inside a recycled item whose own identity does not change (a row's check box) cannot be told apart this way; since the M3 review it is checked against its row (below). Entries hold the node, runtime id, window record (handle, process and window class), the selector (find_ui_elements' identity criteria, without visibleOnly and enabledOnly, which are states; kept only when the search proved them unique, see below), the fingerprint (name, control type, automation id, class, index path), the generation of the read that last saw it and lastUsed; an LRU of 10,000 (ElementRegistry.DefaultCapacity, twice get_ui_elements' maxElements limit of 5000, so one large walk cannot expire the refs from before it). A window's entries are dropped when IsWindow fails or its handle now names a window of another process or class, checked on every lookup and resolution and at every read's NextGeneration (a reuse by a window of the same process and class is not told apart; handle values come back only after a slot's 16-bit reuse counter wraps). Window records are dropped with their window; "issued by this session" is the range of window numbers handed out, so nothing grows per window. Resolution, in the design's order with these refinements: (1) a live read of the stored node through the new IUiaTree.ReadElement (BuildUpdatedCache/GetUpdatedCache, which ask the provider), accepted only when it reads the stored runtime id and the stored identity (a proxy that now answers for another element, a recycled item, or a read without a runtime id is not accepted); (2) the window searched for the runtime id (the new UiaProperty.RuntimeId condition, int[] in both engines) with a FindAll through the bounded search rather than the design's FindFirst, so a match is also checked for the stored identity; (3) only for a ref with a proven-unique selector: the selector re-run and every result verified against the fingerprint (the name too when there is no automation id): one result moves the ref to it (reported as refMoved since the M3 review, below; and when that element already has a ref of its own, that ref is returned and the old one dropped, so one element never has two refs), several are AmbiguousElement (with the count), none ElementStale, and a search that did not finish is OperationTimedOut rather than a verdict. Refinement after review: the design's step 3 would move a ref to a sibling whenever automation ids repeat (every row's DeleteButton: after row A is deleted, row B's button is the only one left), so a selector is kept only when find_ui_elements proved it fits no other element of the window with the element's fingerprint (ElementOrigin.Unique): a complete search of the whole window (not below withinRef); without visibleOnly or enabledOnly, every match was kept and none shares its fingerprint with another; with them (they can hide an offscreen or disabled twin), a single match is checked by one more search of the window for its identity alone, at most 1 s (ElementSearchService.UniquenessBudget). A later search that finds a twin drops the selector. A tree walk, the focused element and the element at a point keep none, so their refs are ElementStale once the element is gone (the fingerprint-made selector of the first version is removed). Step 2 that did not finish, with no selector to try, is OperationTimedOut. The index path is recorded from walks and shown in the stale message, but never used to pick among candidates: choosing by position would be the silent guess the item forbids. Internal API for M3.6 element_action, M3.8 wait_for_element, M3.9 the reads and M3.11 marks (documented on IElementRegistry): Register (no UI Automation call), Lookup (window and fingerprint, no UI Automation call), Resolve (on a worker thread, inside the caller's own read), ResolveAsync (the window's hung check, then Resolve on the UiaWorker under a timeout) returning ResolvedElement(Ref, WindowHandle, Node, fresh UiaElement, Via Live|RuntimeId|Selector), and NextGeneration. New UIElementErrorCode values ElementStale (11) and AmbiguousElement (12), appended. *Bounded search* (Services/ElementSearch.cs), answering the wave A note that FindAll is not bounded: the top of the tree is expanded breadth first (at most 16 nodes with several children plus up to 32 single-child nodes, so the wrapper panes of a browser or Electron window do not use up the split; a level of more than 50 children kept whole) until it has 12 parts, the expanded levels' elements tested on the server by UiaConditionEvaluator (the seam's conditions on cached properties, as a provider evaluates them), then one provider-side FindAll per part, with the deadline (Timeout) and a limit of 2000 provider matches before the name filter (SearchLimit) checked between parts, and, when a name filter (contains, regex) runs, also between the elements of a part's results (without one, counting what the provider already returned is free and keeps the counts exact); a part that fails is skipped and counted (PartsSkipped), a TimeoutException (the UIA3 transaction timeout) ends the search with what it found, a condition on properties a walk does not read (ProcessId, FrameworkId) is searched in one call. One part is still one call that cannot be stopped (a level wider than 50, such as a list of ten thousand rows, is one part); the split makes the deadline and the limit real between provider calls and keeps partial results. Under UIA3 the transaction timeout (about 5 s) ends such a call; under WAM_UIA_ENGINE=uia2 only the worker's deadline abandons it, and the stuck thread counts against the application. Searching a wide level child by child was not done: it turns one call into thousands. ReadSubtree is not used. *find_ui_elements* (UIElementTools, `elements` toolset, read-only, idempotent, closed world, on the input gate's read-only list, not audited; ElementSearchService behind IElementSearchService): handle or window or withinRef; name with nameMatch exact (provider, ignoring case)|contains|regex (server side; regex ignores case and runs at most 100 ms per name like titleRegex, InvalidArguments when invalid or slower); automationId and className (provider, ignoring case); controlType, one or a comma list (an OR by id; Unknown, and AppBar/SemanticZoom under WAM_UIA_ENGINE=uia2, which throws NotSupportedException, are tested on the server); withinRef (its descendants; its window is searched; a stale ref fails with its own code; the result's withinRef is the ref the element has now, which differs when it was found again under a ref of its own); visibleOnly=true and enabledOnly=false as provider conditions on each element (unlike get_ui_elements' visibleOnly, an offscreen element does not hide its subtree); maxResults 20 (1-100); detail minimal|standard|full; timeoutMs 5000 (1000-30000). At least one of name, automationId, controlType and className, else InvalidArguments. Result: matchCount (in the part searched), truncated with truncationReason MaxResults|Timeout|SearchLimit|PartsSkipped, elements with ref, name, controlType, automationId, className, bounds, clickPoint (IUiaTree.GetClickablePoint, new, else the centre of the bounds; none for an offscreen element), isEnabled, isOffscreen and patterns (all supported patterns, action patterns first, without LegacyIAccessible, which UIA3 reports on nearly everything); standard adds value (200 characters, valueTruncated; deviation as in M1.9: a password field's value is never read, isPassword says so), toggleState, expandCollapseState, isSelected (through the pattern objects, only when the element has the pattern), hasKeyboardFocus and isKeyboardFocusable; full adds frameworkId, processId, nativeWindowHandle, helpText, accessKey, acceleratorKey and localizedControlType (IUiaTree.ReadDescription, new). The hung-window precheck (shared with get_ui_elements, now Services/UiaTargets.cs) and the UiaWorker deadline with a 2 s grace apply; a search the worker abandons still returns its matches, described from what the search read (no call on the caller's thread). No timeout with no match yet is OperationTimedOut. *scope* was not reserved: M3.5 added `scope`, whose default also searches the app's popups while it is in front. *Refs in the read tools* (additive): get_ui_elements in every format (the text format puts the ref first: `w4120035e17 Button 'Save' ...`), capture_with_elements (each annotated element), get_focused_element and get_element_at_point (element.ref, registered under the window the result reports); `ref` is serialized first. A walk registers the elements it returns, with their index path; an element read on its own gets its runtime id from GetRuntimeId (answered from memory, no further call). Server instructions: the locate line names find_ui_elements and the act line its click points (net 4 characters shorter, so sibling branches keep their room). Payload: get_ui_elements' typical result grew from 5,397 to 6,000 tokens and capture_with_elements' from 6,052 to 6,544 (about 5 tokens per seven-digit ref); find_ui_elements' is 165. Guards: the new seam members ReadElement, GetClickablePoint and ReadDescription are reads in the E2E WrapperCatalog, and NativeCallGuardTests keeps ElementRegistry, ElementSearch and ElementSearchService off both UI Automation clients. Tests: ElementRegistryTests, ElementSearchTests (split, limits, partial results, the evaluator), ElementSearchServiceTests, ElementRefReadTests (refs from the walk and the single-element reads), FindUIElementsToolTests, Uia3Tree tests of the new members, and the contract tables (annotations, toolsets, payload budgets, enums, docs). A gated no-input E2E scenario (FixtureElementRefsTests, not run in this change) finds fixture controls by automation id and control type, checks click points against bounds and that the walk gives the same ref. Not done: provider-side `contains` through PropertyConditionFlags_MatchSubstring (UIA3 on Windows 10 1809 and later; unverified on a real desktop, so contains stays a server-side test), and real-desktop runs of the split search, clickable points and resolution (FixtureElementRefsTests); both are in BACKLOG.md.

**Review fixes (M3 review: `m3-fix-refs`, `m3-fix-policy`, `m3-fix-surface`).** *Owned windows.* UI Automation's HWND proxy shows a window that a visible top-level window owns (a dialog, a menu) as a child of the owner's element, ahead of its own children. So a search of the owner found a dialog's elements as the owner's, the default windowAndPopups scope found them twice (matchCount 2, so every criteria action on a dialog's button was AmbiguousElement), and the owner's copy could keep a proven selector after the dialog closed. The window searches (find_ui_elements' search and its uniqueness proof, searchVirtualized's list search and the registry's resolution searches) now leave out a child of the window's root that the window owns (OwnedWindows: GetWindow GW_OWNER of the children with a window handle of their own), so an owned window is searched only as a window of its own. *Recycled rows.* A control inside a recycled list row keeps its runtime id, type, automation id and class when the row shows another item, so it resolved Live to the control of the row now shown. The registry now records an element's row, its nearest ListItem, TreeItem or DataItem ancestor at most 8 levels up and not past a list, tree, grid, table or window (ElementRows: a tree walk knows it, find_ui_elements reads it up the parent chain), and checks it again on the Live and RuntimeId paths: a row with another runtime id, or another name when it has no automation id, makes the ref ElementStale ("its row now shows another item"). A re-created element is moved to only within its own row, and a ref moved into a re-created row keeps that row, so its next resolution is live. *Moved refs.* A resolution through the stored selector (path 3) looked exactly like a live hit. ResolvedElement.Moved now marks it until a read returns the element again, and the tools that take a ref report it (refMoved and a warning: read_element_text, read_table, wait_for_element, find_ui_elements for withinRef, capture_window's ref crop, menu_path), while element_action refuses a moved ref (ElementStale with refMoved) until find_ui_elements returns the element, so an action never lands on an element that only its description found (the repo owner's decision). *Unrealized twins.* A tree search cannot see the rows a virtualizing list has not realized, so a list, tree or grid item named by its text (no automation id) that the tree proved alone is also checked with its list, the nearest ancestor with the ItemContainer pattern at most 3 levels up (two FindItemByProperty calls by its name, in the same 1 s budget): another item of that name makes it not unique, and a virtualized item whose list cannot be asked is not proven. *Smaller fixes.* A ref whose window is still open but cannot be read by UI Automation is kept and reported as WindowNotFound, not ElementStale (only IsWindow, or a process or class read that differs, drops a window). A provider that does not support a search fails only its part (PartsSkipped, M3.2). find_ui_elements refuses a window of a denied app (TargetDeniedByPolicy), and its windowAndPopups scope leaves out a popup of a denied app or of the agent's host and counts it in windowsExcluded (M3.5, M1.12). Not done (BACKLOG.md): the tree walks (get_ui_elements, capture_with_elements, get_desktop_state) still list an owned dialog inside its owner and register its elements under the owner (those refs resolve only Live and are stale once it closes), and refs from get_focused_element, get_element_at_point and get_active_popups record no row.

### M3.5 Popup-, menu- and dialog-aware element search

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m3-popup-search` (part 1), `m3-dialogs` (part 2: respond_to_dialog, menu_path; get_desktop_state's scope with M3.12) (Done)

Context menus (#32768), ComboLBox dropdowns, WPF Popups, WinUI flyouts and WebView2 popups are separate top-level windows outside the target's UIA subtree. An agent can open a menu today but cannot find 'Delete' in it.

**Design.** Add scope=window|windowAndPopups|desktop to find_ui_elements, wait_for_element and get_desktop_state; the default is windowAndPopups when the target is in the foreground. windowAndPopups searches under one deadline: the target window; every visible top-level window of the target's PID and thread, including owned, tool and untitled ones; GW_ENABLEDPOPUP; desktop children matched provider-side by PropertyCondition(ProcessIdProperty, pid); and popups from descendant processes such as msedgewebview2.exe. New get_active_popups(window?) returns open menus, dropdowns and dialogs with refs. element_action expand, and any invoke that opens a popup, returns openedPopup{ref, handle} after a short wait. respond_to_dialog(window, button) reads the dialog text and buttons (UIA, or #32770 children) and invokes the chosen button, addressed by AutomationId or ClassName where possible so it works on localized Windows. A menu_path action ('File>Save As') uses ExpandCollapse plus Invoke.

*Sources: r1:crit-missing:popup-scope, r2:crit-missing:popup-search, r2:WC-G10, r1:R19, r1:R37*

**As shipped, part 1 (`m3-popup-search`).** *Popup service* (Services/PopupService.cs behind the new IPopupService, a singleton in ServiceRegistration; the internal API for M3.6 and M3.8 is documented on the interface). GetPopups(window) reads Win32 only (no UI Automation call, no message to a window): the visible, uncloaked top-level windows with a size, other than the target, front first (z-order), at most 16 (PopupService.MaxPopups), that (1) the target owns directly or through other owned windows (GW_OWNER, so its GW_ENABLEDPOPUP and an owned window of another process are included; the design's separate GW_ENABLEDPOPUP step is this rule, which also marks isBlocking when the target is disabled), (2) belong to the target's process and are not tied to another of its main windows: unowned and popup-styled (no caption, or a tool window, without WS_EX_APPWINDOW), unowned dialog boxes (#32770) while the process shows no other main window, or owned by a window whose chain ends at a hidden or popup-styled window (a WinForms parking window, a Delphi or Qt application window, a palette), or (3) are popup-styled windows of a direct child process of the target's, never for the shell (explorer.exe). Refinements against the design: (a) "every visible top-level window of the target's PID" would make every Chrome, Explorer, Win11 Notepad or VS Code window a "popup" of the others (one process, many main windows), so a captioned, unowned, non-dialog window of the same process is not a popup, and neither is a window whose owner chain ends at such a window (review fix: a dialog or tooltip of Word document B, or of a second Explorer window, is not document A's, although under the default scope it would have been searched first and its refs registered for a window the agent never named); the rules start from the input guard's InputTargetGuard.BelongsToTarget but attribute rather than accept, so they are narrower for popups of another main window and wider for dialog boxes; the IPopupService remarks state the exact difference (every popup the service names that has no caption, or that the target owns, the guard accepts too; the guard was not changed, to keep this branch off a file the element_action branch touches); (b) the thread is covered by the process (a thread's windows are its process's); for a UWP frame the process is the app's (UwpFrames.AppProcessId), not ApplicationFrameHost, which every UWP app shares; (c) "desktop children matched provider-side by ProcessId" was replaced by this Win32 enumeration: the desktop root's UI Automation children are the top-level windows, which EnumWindows lists without a cross-process call, and searching window by window registers each popup's elements under the popup's own handle, the window a ref resolution searches (a root-level FindAll would return elements without their window, and IUiaTree has no root accessor); (d) descendant processes are limited to direct children, as the guard has it (WebView2's msedgewebview2.exe browser process, which draws the popups, is the host's child), read from one process snapshot (GetProcessList) taken only when a popup-styled window of another process exists, with start times so a reused parent id does not count (a bounded, safe read: beyond the snapshot only the start times of the target and its direct children are read); (e) the input helper windows (IME, MSCTFIME UI, GDI+), the desktop and the taskbars are never popups. Kinds (PopupKind, PascalCase in results): Menu (#32768), Dropdown (ComboLBox, ComboBoxEx32ListBox), Tooltip (tooltips_class32, Xaml_WindowedTooltipClass), Flyout (Xaml_WindowedPopupClass, Microsoft.UI.Content.PopupWindowSiteBridge and DesktopChildSiteBridge), Dialog (#32770, or an owned captioned window that is no tool window), Window (another captioned window) and Popup (the rest); get_active_popups sharpens a plain Popup from its element's control type (Menu, List, ToolTip), since WPF popups and Chromium menus have no telling class. IsInForeground(window): the foreground window is the window, a window it owns, or a window of its (app) process. GetDesktopWindows(first, limit): the visible top-level windows front first, the named one first and never excluded; the others are left out (and counted) when the target policy denies their process (for a UWP frame also its app's) or the host guard calls them the agent's host windows (fail-safe: a guard exception excludes), never the server's own windows. Decision on the policy question, as part 1 shipped: reading a named window stays allowed (consistent with get_ui_elements and find_ui_elements on a handle, which the policy had never covered), but a desktop sweep the agent did not aim does not read denied or host windows. The M3 review replaced it (the repo owner's decision, see the review fixes below and M1.12): every UI Automation read refuses a window of a denied app, named or not, and a popup counts as a window nobody named. WaitForNewPopupAsync(window, before, wait) polls every 50 ms on an injectable TimeProvider and returns the first popup not in `before` that is no tooltip (a tooltip of the control under the pointer is not what an action opened), reading the target's child processes at most once a second (PopupService.ChildProcessRefresh) rather than a process snapshot per poll: element_action's openedPopup (take GetPopups before acting, act, wait). GetPopupList(window) adds whether more than 16 popups were open. *Scope* (ElementQuery.Scope on the shared request, so wait_for_element gets it by reusing ElementQuery; strict ToolEnum ElementSearchScope window|windowAndPopups|desktop, nullable on the tool): left out, windowAndPopups when IsInForeground, else window (a menu of an app not in front has closed); always window with withinRef (the tool refuses another explicit scope with withinRef as InvalidArguments); desktop may name no window (windowHandle 0). Deviation from the M3.4 note that omitting scope should keep the window-only search: the foreground default was asked for; for an app in front a search can now return more matches, popups first, which the Changes section of docs/TOOLS.md says. ElementSearchService searches the popups front first and the named window last (owned and popup windows sit above it), under one deadline, one maxResults and one candidate limit: each window's ElementSearch gets what the windows before it left (new optional ElementSearch maxCandidates), and the popups searched before the named window leave it half of the limit (ElementSearchService.NamedWindowCandidateReserve, 1000), so a large owned palette cannot crowd it out; the deadline is checked between windows and stops the rest (Timeout), while another window's own stop (its candidate share, a provider timeout with time left) does not end the search. Only the named window gets the hung check that fails the call (NotResponding), the warm-up and withinRef; another window that does not respond, closed, whose provider fails, or that times out (opening it or searching it) while the deadline has not passed is skipped and counted (PartsSkipped), keeping the matches of the other windows. Each match is registered under its own top-level window and carries FoundElement.WindowHandle when that is not the named window; uniqueness (M3.4) is judged per window, and the extra proof of a single visible match runs for each window with one, the named window's first, under one shared 1 s budget. Result: Scope (always), WindowsSearched and WindowsExcluded for the wider scopes, truncationReason WindowLimit (new, 5) for desktop beyond 40 windows (PopupService.DesktopWindowLimit) and for windowAndPopups beyond 16 popups. The search is one worker read counted against the named window's application (the unknown-target bucket for a desktop search without one), so a provider stuck in another window of the scope counts against it; hung windows are skipped before they are read (documented on ElementSearchService). No new error code. *get_active_popups(handle or window, timeoutMs 5000)* (Tools/PopupTools.cs, `elements` toolset, read-only, idempotent, closed world, on the input gate's read-only list, not audited): without a window, the top owner of the foreground window (a dialog in front lists as its app's popup); returns windowHandle, windowTitle, isForeground and the popups front first with ref (the popup window's own element, registered under its handle, read on the UiaWorker under the timeout after each popup's hung check; a popup not read is listed without one), handle, kind, title, className, processId, processName, ownerHandle, bounds, controlType and isBlocking, truncated over 16; InvalidHandle, or WindowNotFound with nothing in front. Coverage: Win32 #32768 menus, ComboLBox dropdowns, WinUI windowed flyouts and WebView2 child-process popups are covered by unit tests with fakes (PopupServiceTests, ElementSearchScopeTests, FindUIElementsToolTests, PopupToolsTests); WPF context menus and combo box popups by the gated input-tier E2E FixturePopupTests (right-click the fixture's context menu target and find contextMenuDelete with the default scope under the popup's window; click the combo box open and find "Charlie" with windowAndPopups), not run in this change. Flyouts drawn inside the app's own window (a WinUI ContentDialog, an in-window XAML popup) are in its tree already and need no scope. Guards: PopupService reads UI Automation only through IUiaTree (NativeCallGuardTests); no new wrapper member. Done later: respond_to_dialog and menu_path (part 2, below), get_desktop_state's scope (M3.12) and, at integration, `scope` with the criteria of wait_for_element, read_element_text and element_action (ElementQuery.Scope, the same default as find_ui_elements). Not done: making the input guard call the service and element_action's openedPopup through it (both kept apart on purpose: see the IPopupService remarks and M3.6), and real-desktop runs (BACKLOG.md).

**As shipped, part 2 (`m3-dialogs`).** *respond_to_dialog(button?, handle or window?, allowHostTarget, timeoutMs 5000)* (Tools/DialogTools.cs over IDialogService/DialogService; `input` toolset, destructive, not idempotent, closed world, on the input gate's SelfGatedTools list, audited with `button` kept as a named string). *Which dialog* (DialogService.Locate, Win32 reads only): the window named, or the foreground window; while it is disabled by a modal popup of its own (IPopupService.GetPopups' isBlocking, GW_ENABLEDPOPUP), that popup, down a chain of at most 4 (a Save As dialog's replace prompt); then the window itself when it is a dialog box (#32770, or PopupService.Classify's Dialog: an owned captioned window without WS_EX_TOOLWINDOW), else its frontmost Dialog popup; else DialogNotFound (an unowned window is no dialog even when named by handle; the message says to use element_action). *Reading* (ReadAsync): two searches through IElementSearchService (find_ui_elements' own search and ElementQuery, scope window, visible only): Button, SplitButton and TitleBar (review fix: the control view includes the title bar's caption buttons, which made "close" hit the title bar's X and the only-button rule dead for message boxes; buttons inside a TitleBar's bounds are dropped, as WindowCloseVerifier skips the TitleBar; at most 20 kept, with refs, names cut to 100, `standard` from the AutomationId) and Text (at most 20 elements, each cut to 500 characters and 2,000 in all, blanks, repeats and button captions left out, `textTruncated`); the window title (200). Decision on "UIA, or #32770 children": UI Automation only. Its built-in Win32 proxy exposes a dialog box's children with their control ids as AutomationIds (a MessageBox's Yes is "6"), and a task dialog's common buttons as "CommandButton_6", so one path reads #32770 dialogs, message boxes, task dialogs, WPF, WinForms and WinUI dialogs; a Win32 child walk (EnumChildWindows, GetDlgCtrlID, WM_GETTEXT, which blocks on a hung dialog) would need new INativeWindowWrapper members and guard entries for no reach UI Automation lacks. *Which button* (DialogButtons.Match): (1) a standard name (ok, cancel, abort, retry, ignore, yes, no, close, help, tryagain, continue = IDOK 1 to IDCONTINUE 11; spellings such as "try again" accepted) or a control id ("6"), matched against the AutomationId's id ("6" or "CommandButton_6"), so "yes" presses Ja or はい; (2) an AutomationId, exactly; (3) the visible text, ignoring case, `&` and a trailing ellipsis; the first rule with a match decides, two matches are AmbiguousElement, none ElementNotFound (with the buttons in the result and the message); last resort, "ok" presses a dialog's only button (a localized MB_OK box's button may carry IDCANCEL so Esc closes it), with a warning; since the M3 review only when that button carries the id of Cancel or Close. *Read only* (review follow-up): without `button` the call locates, checks and reads the dialog and presses nothing (allowed while paused, as a read; the target checks still apply), so an agent reads the dialog in front, decides, then answers it by `dialogHandle` instead of pressing blind in whatever dialog is in front. *Guards:* the pause first (PausedByUser; only with `button`), the dialog noted for the audit line, then IInputTargetGuard.CheckTarget on the dialog before anything is read (decided: a dialog of a denied app, such as a credential prompt, or of the agent's host is neither read nor answered, since the tool acts and its read is not the agent's choice of window), then the press is IElementActionService.ActAsync(invoke) on the button's ref with every element_action guard (gate, BlockedByModal, NotResponding, the element's process, audit through this tool's line); allowHostTarget only for a window named by handle, as element_action. *After the press:* InvokedPendingModal (a Win32 button waits for the dialog it opened) gives dialogClosed false and nextDialog = openedPopup; otherwise the dialog is polled (50 ms, up to 3 s, at most timeoutMs) for IsWindow/IsWindowVisible; still open gives a warning and nextDialog when a new non-tooltip popup of the dialog appeared. A dialog that stays open is not a failure (Help, Apply, refused input). A press that returns ActionTimedOut (sent, its handler had not returned) counts as sent: the dialog is watched the same way and the result succeeds with a warning and dialogClosed, rather than saying "was not pressed" and inviting a second press. Untrusted text: returned as data, bounded, never followed; the descriptions and SKILL.md say so, and the SKILL.md keeps "a question the user has not decided goes to the user". The dialog is noted for the audit line (AuditScope) right after it is located, so a guard refusal's line names it too. *menu_path* (element_action `action: "menu_path"`, `value` "File>Save As"; MenuPaths.Parse: `>`-separated, trimmed, 1-8 items of at most 200 characters, no empty item, InvalidArguments otherwise; separator fixed, not configurable: no shipped menu name needs `>`, and such a path can be walked step by step). The tool carries it out through the new IMenuPathService/MenuPathService (ElementActionService.ActAsync refuses `menu_path` as InvalidArguments, since it is no action on one element): the start is the window (handle or window alone is allowed for menu_path, with `scope` for the first search), a ref (its registry window; a stale ref fails with the registry's code), or the one element the criteria find (WithinRef below it). Each item is found as the only visible MenuItem matching MenuPaths.NamePattern (regex, case-insensitive: the name, blanks, an optional trailing "..." or "…", an optional accelerator after a tab), searched (first item) below the start or in the window with the scope, (further items) in the submenu window the previous item opened (element_action's openedPopup, else IPopupService.WaitForNewPopupAsync up to 1 s), then WithinRef the previous item (WPF and WinForms show a submenu's items under it), then, only when no submenu window was seen, the window with windowAndPopups (review fix: an item missing from the submenu no longer matches a same-named item of another menu); several matches are AmbiguousElement, a search that did not finish and found none OperationTimedOut. Every item but the last is element_action expand; PatternNotSupported there stops the path with that code and the item is not invoked (review fix: an item without ExpandCollapse is a command, so the former Invoke fallback ran a command such as Delete when the path was wrong); the last is invoke (click's patterns when PatternNotSupported, for a checkable item's Toggle); each is a full element_action call with its guards. Result: the last item's ElementActionResult with action "menu_path" and the new `steps` (item, ref, method, openedPopup per item; also on a failure). A failure stops the path; menus the earlier items opened are left open and the error says how to close them (decided: closing them would be one more action after a refusal such as PausedByUser). Each search and action is bounded by timeoutMs, and the whole path by timeoutMs per item, at most 60 s (MenuPathService.MaxTotalTimeout; each step gets what is left, at least 1 s; OperationTimedOut with the steps done); at most 8 items. `fallback: mouse` is refused for menu_path by ElementActionService.ArgumentError (mouse is only for invoke, click and select), not ignored. *New error codes* (UIElementErrorCode, appended): DialogNotFound = 21, DialogNotCompleted = 22, FileExists = 23 (M3.14; the same name as ScreenshotErrorCode.FileExists, one meaning). Coverage: DialogButtonsTests, DialogServiceTests, MenuPathServiceTests, DialogToolsTests, ElementActionToolTests (menu_path routing), the contract tests (annotations, audit, payload budget 220 for respond_to_dialog at 144 measured), DependencyInjectionTests (all three act only through the one element action service). Gated input-tier E2E FixtureDialogTests (not run here): the fixture document's save prompt (a Win32 MessageBox) answered `cancel` by its id with the document staying open, the WPF modal answered "Cancel" by text from its disabled owner, and a one-item menu path on the open context menu. Not done: a Win32 menu bar (#32768 submenus) and task-dialog scenario (no fixture surface) and real-desktop runs (BACKLOG.md). get_desktop_state's scope came with M3.12.

**Review fixes, part 1 (M3 review: `m3-fix-policy`, `m3-fix-refs`).** A popup was taken on its relation to the target alone, so the reads took in what a desktop search leaves out: get_active_popups without a window read the popups of a denied app or of the agent's host in front, a credential prompt of another process owned by the named app was searched under windowAndPopups, and get_desktop_state walked it. A popup is never a window the caller named, so the reads now follow the desktop scope's rule for it: IPopupService.GetPopupList marks a popup whose process (for a UWP frame, also its app's) the target policy denies, or that the host guard calls a window of the agent's host, and find_ui_elements' windowAndPopups scope (with the criteria of read_element_text and wait_for_element), get_active_popups and get_desktop_state's popups and walk leave it out and count it (windowsExcluded, popupsExcluded). GetPopups stays the raw list, so respond_to_dialog and file_dialog still find a denied dialog and refuse it as a target, and element_action and menu_path keep the host's popups (ElementQuery.IncludeHostPopups), whose windows the input guard checks and allowHostTarget may name. get_active_popups refuses a named window of a denied app (TargetDeniedByPolicy). An owned dialog is searched only as a window of its own, so the default scope no longer finds it twice (M3.4).

**Review fixes, part 2 (M3 review: `m3-fix-dialogs`, `m3-fix-policy`, `m3-fix-action`).** respond_to_dialog presses only in the dialog the agent read. A press needs `handle` or `window`, except `cancel`, which it may press in the dialog in front unnamed; any other button there is InvalidArguments, with nothing read or pressed (read the dialog, then answer it by its dialogHandle; the repo owner's decision). A dialog named by handle or selector that a modal dialog of its own now disables is refused with BlockedByModal, naming that dialog, instead of being followed to it; a named application window that is no dialog still stands for its modal dialog, and the read-only call follows the chain from any window. `ok` presses a lone button only when it carries the id of Cancel or Close (the localized MB_OK case it was written for), never a lone command such as "Restart now". Button AutomationIds are cut to 100 characters like the names, and the next dialog's title to 200. The audit line names the dialog that was answered as its target (handle, title and process), also when the call named its owner, a selector or no window; it used to name only a handle given in the arguments. menu_path refuses an item whose only match comes from a search that did not finish (OperationTimedOut), as element_action's criteria do. A dialog of the server's own process (a security prompt Windows showed for a launch) is refused even with allowHostTarget, and the SmartScreen prompt is on the default deny list (M1.12). respond_to_dialog, like element_action, refuses an argument it does not declare (M3.6).

### M3.6 element_action: pattern-first actions with verified post-state

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** L · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m3-element-action` (Done)

Act on elements through UIA patterns, which work on background and occluded windows without fighting the foreground lock, and fall back to a guarded mouse click only when needed. Each call returns the before and after state so the agent can verify in the same call.

**Design.** One tool: element_action(ref | window+selector, action, value?, fallback=mouse|none, timeoutMs=5000). Actions: invoke, click, double_click, right_click, focus, set_value, append_text, toggle, select, add_to_selection, remove_from_selection, expand, collapse, scroll_into_view, scroll, set_range_value, realize, window_close, window_minimize, window_maximize. Pre-validate IsEnabled and IsOffscreen and return actionable errors ('offscreen: use scroll_into_view'). Pattern paths: Invoke; Toggle looped up to 3 times until the target state; ValuePattern.SetValue after IsReadOnly and IsPassword checks, then read back; ExpandCollapse; SelectionItem; ScrollItem, or ScrollPattern on the nearest scrollable ancestor (returns percent and atEnd); RangeValue; WindowPattern; LegacyIAccessible DoDefaultAction on UIA3. Invoke, Toggle and ExpandCollapse run on a throwaway thread outside the shared UIA worker, because a Win32 Invoke blocks until the modal it opened closes; in-flight threads are capped. On timeout the result is invokedPendingModal with a window-snapshot diff, so the agent can read and dismiss the modal. The set_value keyboard fallback (focus, select-all, type) runs only when FocusedElement equals the target and the M1 foreground guard passes, otherwise FocusNotOnElement; set_value takes mode=value|type|paste. The mouse fallback is a guarded click at ClickPoint after EnsureForeground. The result reports the method used, before and after state (value, toggle, expand, selected, name, enabled), outcomeVerified and openedPopup. Every call passes through the policy and the audit log.

*Sources: r1:R44, r2:R22, r1:crit:R44, r1:UIA-G1, r1:INP-G1, r1:MCP-G2, r2:UIA-G3, r2:INP-G1, r2:MSP-G1, r2:UIA-G8*

**As shipped (`m3-element-action`).** New tool `element_action` (Tools/ElementActionTools.cs; `input` toolset, because a user who turns input off means the agent must not act on applications; destructive, not idempotent, closed world; audited with `value` as a length and, since the M3 review, the `name` criterion as nameLength, since an element's name can say what the user works on (a file name, an e-mail subject); on the input gate's SelfGatedTools list) over IElementActionService/ElementActionService (Services/ElementActionService.cs and .Plans.cs). *Naming the element:* `ref`, or `handle`/`window` plus find_ui_elements' criteria (name, nameMatch, automationId, controlType, className) passed as find_ui_elements' own request model (ElementQuery) to IElementSearchService with visibleOnly and enabledOnly false (so the action can say what is wrong with an offscreen or disabled element) and maxResults 2: exactly one match in a search that covered the whole window, else AmbiguousElement (with the first refs), ElementNotFound or OperationTimedOut; the match's ref is then resolved like any ref (IElementRegistry). allowHostTarget only with `ref` or a window named by handle, as the input tools, and never for a window of the server's own process (M1.12). *Order of a call:* the arguments' fit to the action (InvalidArguments before any search); the ref's lookup (no UI Automation); the pause (PausedByUser) and the input gate (InputBusy), held while it reads and acts; the new IInputTargetGuard.CheckTarget on the element's window (desktop probes, InvalidHandle, TargetElevated, TargetDeniedByPolicy, so the default deny list keeps password managers and credential prompts out, TargetIsAgentHost unless allowHostTarget, BlockedByModal: a pattern on a window a modal disables would act behind the dialog's back); one read on the UiaWorker that does the hung check (NotResponding), resolves the ref (since the M3 review a ref moved to a re-created element is refused, below), reads the identity (the password flag; fails closed: an identity that cannot be read is ElementAccessFailed, so a password field cannot pass for another field and a top-level window for an in-app one), finds the own window of an element of another process (a child or owned window of another program; none is ElementAccessFailed) for the new IInputTargetGuard.CheckElementWindow (policy, elevation and host checks on that window itself; since the M3 review before the element's state is read, so a refusal carries no name or value of it), reads the before-state through the patterns, refuses a disabled (ElementNotEnabled) or offscreen (ElementOffscreen, with "use scroll_into_view", or "restore the window" when it is minimized) element except for scroll_into_view, scroll, realize and window_*, and chooses the method; then the pause is checked again, since that read can take up to timeoutMs. *Methods:* invoke = Invoke, else LegacyIAccessible DoDefaultAction when it has a default action (UIA3; since the M3 review only the default action's name is read, and never for a password field, since MSAA's other properties include the value), else fallback; click = SelectionItem.Select, Invoke, Toggle, ExpandCollapse (expand or collapse), DoDefaultAction, else fallback (SelectionItem first: an Explorer or ListView item's Invoke is its double click, which opens the file, and a check-box row toggles only on its box); toggle = Toggle, once or until `value` on/off/indeterminate in at most two toggles (a three-state box reaches any state in two; three before the M3 review), stopping when the element comes back to its state before, each further toggle only after the state changed from the last one (polled on the action thread up to 500 ms, at most a quarter of timeoutMs, because Chromium, Electron and WebView2 toggle asynchronously and a stale read would toggle once too often; no change, no further toggle; ActionFailed when not reached); set_value = ValuePattern.SetValue after IsReadOnly (ElementReadOnly), then read back; append_text = SetValue of the whole value read again on the action's thread plus the text (refused for a password field, whose value is never read); select, add_to_selection, remove_from_selection = SelectionItem; expand, collapse = ExpandCollapse (a leaf node is PatternNotSupported); scroll_into_view = ScrollItem; scroll = the Scroll pattern of the element or its nearest ancestor with one (16 levels), direction and amount page|line, the result's `scroll` {horizontalPercent, verticalPercent, atEnd, containerRef (the container registered as a ref)}; set_range_value = RangeValue within its range (InvalidArguments outside it); realize = VirtualizedItem; window_close/minimize/maximize = WindowPattern, refused (InvalidArguments) for a top-level window (its handle is the ref's window or a root window, it is the window's own element without a handle, or it is a UWP CoreWindow), which has close_window (shell and host guards, confirmation, verified close) and set_window_state, so window_close never bypasses the close guards (invoke or click on a title bar's Close button is not refused, as the click tool allows it); focus = verified activation (EnsureForeground) then the new IUiaTree.SetFocus; an action whose state is already reached does nothing (method None). Without the pattern: PatternNotSupported listing the patterns the element has. *fallback* `none` (default) | `mouse`, the latter only for invoke, click and select. Default `none` (decided): a mouse fallback moves the user's pointer, takes the foreground, can hit what moved in between and turns a definite "no pattern" into an unverifiable click, so the agent opts in knowingly after PatternNotSupported. double_click and right_click are always guarded mouse clicks. *Mouse and keyboard paths* go only through IInputSimulationService (every input check: host guard, policy, occlusion, modifiers, pause, InputGate, paste guard) after EnsureForeground inside the gate; the gate is released before the input service takes it again. Click point, read again after the activation: since the M3 review the first of UI Automation's clickable point, the centre and the quarters' centres of the bounds where a hit test finds the element (below); before, the clickable point, else the bounds' centre, unchecked. set_value/append_text `mode` value|type|paste: type and paste take only a text field (an Edit, or a Document or ComboBox with a writable Value pattern; else PatternNotSupported, because Ctrl+A then Delete in a list or grid would select and delete its items), activate the window, SetFocus the element and check that keyboard focus is on the element itself, or for a ComboBox on its Edit child (FocusNotOnElement otherwise, before Ctrl+A could reach another control), send Ctrl+A (set_value) or Ctrl+End (append_text) through send_keys' guarded path, check focus again, then type_text, or for paste put the text on the clipboard inside the gate (IClipboardService.SetText, as set_clipboard) and send Ctrl+V, which the paste guard allows because the agent put it there (paste follows set_clipboard: refused when set_clipboard is not served, above 1,000,000 characters, and for a password field, which it would leave on the clipboard); "" deletes the selection. *Blocking actions:* every pattern action (not only Invoke, Toggle and ExpandCollapse: none may block the read worker) runs on the new IUiaActionRunner/UiaActionRunner, a background MTA thread per action with its own pending counts (2 per application, 6 overall: ActionsPending), apart from UiaWorker's stuck counts, so a Win32 Invoke blocked by its modal never stops the reads of that modal. At `timeoutMs` (default 5000, 1000-30000, also each read's bound), or when the UIA3 transaction timeout ends the call, the new IAppWindowProbe/AppWindowProbe (a before/after diff of the visible top-level windows of the element's and the window's processes, Win32 reads only; M3.5's popup service can replace it behind the interface) gives `status: InvokedPendingModal` with `openedPopup`, or ActionTimedOut (it may still take effect) when nothing appeared; openedPopup is reported after any action that opened a window. *Integration with M3.5 (decided):* the probe was kept rather than replaced by IPopupService.WaitForNewPopupAsync/GetPopups, because the popup service answers "the popups of one window" and openedPopup needs "any window of the application the action opened": a menu item's ref belongs to the menu's own window, and the dialog it opens is owned by the main window, which the service attributes to the main window, never to the menu, so the swap would lose InvokedPendingModal and openedPopup for the menu-item-opens-dialog case; a new main window (New Window) is no popup by design; and an element of another process has its process watched by the probe. The swap needs an "app window of this popup" mapping first (IAppWindowProbe's remarks). The criteria form takes find_ui_elements' `scope` (ElementQuery.Scope), with the same default, so `invoke` on an open menu's item works by criteria; a search that left popups or windows out (WindowLimit) is OperationTimedOut like a timed-out one. An application error is ActionFailed, a vanished element ElementStale. *Result:* success, action, ref, windowHandle, status Done|InvokedPendingModal, method (the pattern, Focus, Mouse, Keyboard or None), before and after (ElementState: name, isEnabled, isOffscreen, hasKeyboardFocus, value at most 200 characters, isPassword, toggleState, expandCollapseState, isSelected, rangeValue, scroll percents, windowVisualState; `after` absent when the element is gone), outcomeVerified (per action; null for invoke and mouse clicks and for a password field; read up to three times 100 ms apart while false), scroll, openedPopup, receivingWindow (mouse and keyboard paths), warnings. A password field's value is never read, before or after (other fields' after.value is the value read back); the audit log keeps `valueLength`, the result's `method` and `status`, and the new kept names `ref`, `action`, `mode`, `direction`, `amount`, `fallback`. New error codes, in UIElementErrorCode (Models/UIElementModels.cs; unified at integration with M3.9's PatternNotSupported = 13 and the shipped ElementNotFound = 8, which element_action reuses): ElementNotEnabled = 14, ElementOffscreen = 15, ElementReadOnly = 16, FocusNotOnElement = 17, ActionFailed = 18, ActionTimedOut = 19, ActionsPending = 20; the result's errorCode is a string, so the reused codes keep their names. *AutoSetFocus (wave A follow-up, decided):* Uia3Tree turns IUIAutomation6.AutoSetFocus off when it creates the client, so pattern actions do not move focus or take the foreground from the user (they are meant for background windows); focus and the keyboard path set focus on purpose after verified activation. The managed client (WAM_UIA_ENGINE=uia2) has no such switch. *Seam:* IUiaTree.SetFocus (the tree's one acting member, both engines; FakeUiaTree), classified UiaAction in the E2E WrapperCatalog. *E2E guard (wave A follow-up):* FixtureOnlyCallPolicy gains a UiaAction rule: a pattern action (identified by the GetPattern call that handed the pattern out, now carried as WrapperCall.Origin) or SetFocus is allowed only on an element whose process, read through the unguarded tree, is the fixture's; an element it cannot identify is refused; the no-input tier still refuses every UiaAction. Gated scenario FixtureElementActionTests (input tier, not run here): toggle the check box to on, set_value on the edit box, invoke the modal's button, BlockedByModal on the disabled main window, cancel through the modal's own button, each checked against the fixture's event log. Server instructions: the act line names element_action (18 lines, 2992 of 3000 characters; 2996 with M3.8's wait line after integration). The menu_path action and respond_to_dialog came with M3.5 part 2, the file dialog helper with M3.14. Not done (BACKLOG.md): refs in openedPopup (it names the window: handle, title, class and process), an atomic keyboard path (below) and a real-desktop run of the gated scenario.

**Review fixes (M3 review: `m3-fix-action`, `m3-fix-refs`, `m3-fix-policy`, `m3-fix-surface`).** *Click point.* The mouse paths (double_click, right_click and fallback mouse) clicked UI Automation's clickable point, or the bounds' centre, without checking what is there, and the input guard checks only the top-level window at the point, so a sticky header, a cookie banner or an open dropdown of the same window took the click. The point is now chosen on the worker inside the gate and hit-tested with IUiaTree.FromPoint, walking up at most 32 levels: UI Automation's clickable point, then the centre of the bounds (as far as they lie inside the window), then the centres of the bounds' quarters; the first point whose element is the element, or one inside it, is clicked. Where only an element that holds this one is found (some providers do not hit-test their inner elements), or the hit test cannot tell, that point is clicked with a warning; with another element at every point, the call is refused with TargetOccluded and nothing is clicked. The hit test never runs inside the input service's rechecks, where a cross-process call could hang the gate, so something that moves over the point between the check and the click is not caught. *Toggle.* At most two toggles, and the loop stops when the element is back in its state before: a two-state box asked for indeterminate used to be toggled three times and left flipped; now it is left as it was, with ActionFailed. *realize* of an offscreen item it could not act on (no VirtualizedItem or ScrollItem pattern) is outcomeVerified false, where it reported true. *Nothing read before a refusal.* The own window of an element of another process is checked before the element's state is read, so TargetDeniedByPolicy and the other refusals carry no name or value of it, and the MSAA path reads only the default action's name (MSAA's other properties include the value, which can be a password) and is skipped for a password field. *Moved refs.* A ref the registry moved to a re-created element that alone fits its description is refused (ElementStale, refMoved true) until a read returns that element again (M3.4; the repo owner's decision). *Codes.* Activation failures map as the input guard maps its own: InvalidHandle, NotResponding, TargetDeniedByPolicy and PausedByUser keep their codes, a window that did not come to the front (hidden, cloaked, refused by the foreground lock) is TargetNotForeground, and a menu that activation could not close is TargetInMenuMode, worded for a click on the mouse paths; a ModifiersHeld refusal names release_all_input, since element_action has no releaseHeldModifiers. *openedPopup.* The probe leaves out tooltips (another thread of the application can show one while the action's thread is busy), windows without a size and disabled or cloaked windows; it reports InvokedPendingModal only for a new window that can be the dialog the action waits for (a #32770 dialog box, a window with a title bar that is no tool window, or one whose owner is disabled), else ActionTimedOut, and puts such windows first. *observe* is refused before anything is done (InvalidArguments) when get_desktop_state is not served, as observe screenshot follows capture_window. *Arguments.* element_action refuses an argument it does not declare with InvalidArguments naming the closest declared one (get_ui_elements' controlTypes for controlType; find_ui_elements' withinRef, with a hint to find the element first), as do respond_to_dialog, file_dialog, the input tools and the close and kill tools (UndeclaredArgumentToolFilter, inside the audit and error filters, outside the input gate); the read tools still ignore such an argument. The toggle tests measure their wait on an injected clock instead of the system timer. Left open (BACKLOG.md): the keyboard path (mode type or paste, append_text) is not atomic. The gate is released between the focus check and the keys, which the input service sends as separate calls, so a focus change by a parallel call or the user in that gap can send the Delete or the text to another control of the same window; closing it needs a focus recheck inside the input gate before each batch (a hook like PointRecheck) or a gate that element_action can hold across its key calls.

### M3.7 wait_for_window

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m3-wait-for-window` (Done)

Launch-then-act and close-then-verify are the most common races. One server-side wait with a clear timeout replaces guessed sleeps and repeated find_windows calls.

**Design.** Async wait_for_window(selector, condition=exists|gone|foreground|visible|title_changed|input_idle, newOnly/excludeHandles, timeoutMs=10000 (max 60000), stableMs=300, ct, progress). It runs on the M2.6 IWindowWaitService and the M2.4 resolver, so it finds untitled dialogs. It returns {satisfied, timedOut, elapsedMs, window, candidates when ambiguous}. input_idle is best effort and documented as meaningless for console and UWP launchers. It polls for now and switches to event-driven wake-ups once M7.2 exists.

*Sources: r1:R50, r2:R30, r1:WC-G1, r1:PAL-G1, r1:MCP-G3, r2:WC-G1, r2:PAL-G1, r2:MSP-G2*

**As shipped (`m3-wait-for-window`).** New read-only tool wait_for_window (windows toolset, ReadOnly/not destructive/idempotent/closed-world, on the input gate's read-only list so a wait of up to a minute never holds the gate, not audited since it acts on nothing, served in read-only mode and while input is paused) on the new IWindowWaitService.WaitForConditionAsync(WindowConditionRequest, IProgress<OperationProgress>, ct) (WindowWaitService.Conditions.cs; no new native wrapper members). Parameters: window (a required selector), condition (strict enum WindowWaitCondition: exists, gone, foreground, visible, title_changed, input_idle), newOnly, excludeHandles (at most 256), previousTitle (title_changed only), timeoutMs (0-60000, default 10000; 0 checks once) and stableMs (0-10000, default 300, cut to timeoutMs); anything else is InvalidArguments before anything waits, as is a bare {"foreground": true}. Every poll resolves the selector with the M2.4 rules (handle and foreground through ResolveWindow, filters through FindWindows, so className or includeUntitled finds an untitled dialog and includeCloaked is honoured), minus excludeHandles and, with newOnly, the windows open at the start; index then picks among what is left. Polls every 100 ms for 2 s, then every 250 ms, with Task.Delay on an injectable TimeProvider, and reports progress about once a second (elapsed of timeoutMs). Conditions: visible is on screen (visible, not minimized, not cloaked), as exists already follows the listing's visibility; gone by handle is the handle being no window (a hidden window still exists); title_changed follows the one window the selector names at the start and compares normalized titles, so a title passing through "Saving..." counts only once the new title has held stableMs. Design changes against the proposal: (1) a wait that is not satisfied is success false, so isError like every failure (ToolOutcome), with the new WindowErrorCode WaitTimedOut (37) and a body that still carries satisfied, timedOut, elapsedMs and the window as last seen (still open, not in front, not on screen, old title, not idle), because a client that ignores the body must not go on as if the window were there; (2) previousTitle was added, because the action and the wait are sequential calls and a save can finish before the wait starts; for the same reason excludeHandles is the recommended way to catch a new dialog and newOnly is documented as missing a window the last action already opened; (3) several windows satisfying exists, visible or foreground is satisfied with no window but matchCount and up to 5 candidates (a selector never silently picks the first), while title_changed and input_idle, which need one window, end with AmbiguousWindow; (4) input_idle also requires the window not to be hung (IsHungAppWindow, checked first, so a hung window is never waited on) and runs WaitForInputIdle on the window's own process (a UWP app's, not ApplicationFrameHost; never a launcher), at most 250 ms per poll; idleCheck says inputIdle or respondingOnly (no message loop: a console program); documented as best effort since Windows answers it once per process; (5) the wait ends early, unsatisfied, when it can no longer be met: a window.handle that is no window (InvalidHandle, any condition but gone), title_changed's window closing (InvalidHandle) or not resolving to one window at the start (its resolution's errorCode with candidates); (6) a gone wait whose selector matched nothing at the start is satisfied with a warning, so a misspelled selector does not read as "it closed". Server instructions: the waiting rule names wait_for_window. SKILL.md's Wait step now teaches it (excludeHandles before acting, gone, foreground and visible, title_changed with previousTitle, input_idle's limits, candidates, WaitTimedOut). Gated no-input E2E scenarios (FixtureWindowWaitTests) cover exists with excludeHandles against the fixture's document window, visible and gone. Not done: event-driven wake-ups (M7.2) and rebuilding ProcessService's launch wait on it (the launch wait has its own baseline and ownership rules, M2.6). Waits for elements came with M3.8.

**Review fixes (M3 review: `m3-fix-waits`).** A satisfied wait returns only the windows that held the condition for stableMs: a window first seen on the last poll used to count, which left `window` out as ambiguous, and input_idle now chooses among the windows that held (AmbiguousWindow only when several did). newOnly's baseline is the windows shown when the wait starts (visible, and not cloaked unless the selector takes cloaked windows), so a window that was hidden then (a tray app's main window, a dialog an app keeps hidden for reuse) counts as new once it is shown; the baseline used to be every top-level window, hidden ones too. newOnly, and an excludeHandles that holds the handle, are refused with window.handle (InvalidArguments: such a wait could only time out). title_changed by handle resolves the handle without the selector's title filters (the process and class filters still guard against a reused handle), so it follows a window whose title already changed instead of ending with HandleMismatch, and a selector without a handle that no longer matches says that the title may have changed before the call. The timeout messages say that a window came on screen or to the front less than stableMs before the timeout, instead of calling it hidden or saying that none came to the front, and gone on a handle that had closed before the wait no longer advises checking the selector.

### M3.8 wait_for_element
