**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m3-wait-for-element` (Done)

Most flakiness in desktop automation is timing: controls not rendered yet, a dialog still opening, a button that enables only after validation. One blocking call that returns the final state replaces polling full tree walks.

**Design.** Async wait_for_element(window selector, element selector | ref, scope, condition=exists|gone|enabled|visible|focused|name_equals|value_equals|toggle_state, expected?, timeoutMs=10000 (max 60000), ct, progress). It waits for the window first, which covers 'after launch'. It runs the M3.4 selector under the CacheRequest with backoff from 100 to 500 ms, optionally wakes early on StructureChanged and PropertyChanged handlers (removed in a finally block), and applies the M3.3 warm-up. It returns {satisfied, timedOut (distinct from not found), element with ref, elapsedMs, polls, lastObservedState}.

*Sources: r1:R45, r2:R23, r1:UIA-G4, r1:MCP-G3, r2:UIA-G4, r2:MSP-G2*

**As shipped (`m3-wait-for-element`).** New read-only tool wait_for_element (Tools/ElementWaitTools.cs, a tool class of its own so UIElementTools' constructor is untouched; `elements` toolset, ReadOnly/not destructive/idempotent/closed-world, on the input gate's read-only list so a wait of up to a minute never holds the gate, not audited since it acts on nothing, served in read-only mode and while input is paused) on the new IElementWaitService/ElementWaitService (singleton; no new native wrapper members, no new error codes). The element is named by `ref`, or by find_ui_elements' criteria (name, nameMatch, automationId, controlType, className, withinRef) in a `window` selector or below withinRef: the tool builds find_ui_elements' own request model (ElementQuery), and every check calls IElementSearchService.FindAsync with it (window handle, visibleOnly=false, enabledOnly=false, maxResults, detail and timeout set by the wait), so the M3.5 `scope` reaches the search unchanged (done at integration: the tool takes `scope`, refused with `ref` and other than `window` with `withinRef`, and the criteria carry it as ElementQuery.Scope). Conditions (strict enum ElementWaitCondition): exists (default; an offscreen or disabled match counts, since the condition decides), gone (a complete search finds none: a search cut by Timeout, SearchLimit or PartsSkipped never proves it; for a ref, ElementStale or AmbiguousElement), enabled, visible (not offscreen and a non-empty size), focused, name_equals and value_equals (ordinal, case-sensitive; value_equals compares the whole value: a value the search cut to 200 characters is read in full through the match's ref when the expected text starts with what was read), toggle_state (expected on, off or indeterminate). expected is required for the three comparisons and refused otherwise; timeoutMs 0-60000 (default 10000, the wait for the window included; 0 checks once). With criteria, the first 10 matches (ElementWaitService.CandidateLimit; 1 for exists and gone, whose matchCount still counts all) are tested and any satisfies the condition; that element is returned with its ref, so the next call acts on exactly it. Detail standard is asked only for focused, toggle_state and value_equals. A ref is resolved on each check with IElementRegistry.Resolve inside one UiaWorker read (after the window's hung check) that also reads only what the condition needs (focus, toggle state, value) and the clickable point; a ref the registry moved to a re-created element is followed under its new ref. *The window*: a selector is waited for first through IWindowWaitService.WaitForConditionAsync (exists, stableMs 0, the whole timeout), so a wait after a launch covers a window that is still opening; several windows end the wait with AmbiguousWindow and candidates, other early ends of the window wait pass their code on, and a window that never appears is WaitTimedOut with no windowHandle and polls 0. Refinement: gone does not wait for the window (timeout 0): no matching window means none of its elements is there, satisfied with a warning, as wait_for_window's gone does for a selector that matched nothing. A ref and withinRef name their window through IElementRegistry.Lookup, with no window wait; window together with ref or withinRef is refused (InvalidArguments) rather than compared. *Timing*: the first check runs at once, then the checks back off from 100 ms by half to 500 ms (100, 150, 225, 337, 500, ...) with an injectable TimeProvider and delay; each check gets the time left, at least 1 s (so the last check is not cut short) and at most 30 s; a wait can end up to about 3 s after timeoutMs when a provider does not return from one call (the search's 2 s grace to stop; the docs said a second until the M3 review); progress about once a second. *Warm-up* (wave B note): once per wait, not per check: ElementQuery gains `WarmUp` (init property, default true) that ElementSearchService honours, and the wait turns it off once a check has reached the window (since the M3 review; it was tied to the first check, whose failure lost it); that check's accessibilityWarmup is reported. *Result* (WaitForElementResult): success = satisfied, satisfied, timedOut (only when timeoutMs passed), elapsedMs, polls, windowHandle, element (find_ui_elements' FoundElement: the satisfying element, or the element as last seen), matchCount, lastObservedState (a sentence such as "Button 'OK' (w4120035e9) is disabled", only when not satisfied), accessibilityWarmup, candidates, warning, error, errorCode. Consistent with wait_for_window: not satisfied is success false and so isError, with WaitTimedOut (WindowErrorCode 37, reused rather than a new UIElementErrorCode, so no number can collide with a sibling branch); early ends are WindowNotFound (the window closed, any condition but gone, which a closed window satisfies with a warning), ElementStale or AmbiguousElement for a ref or withinRef (stop signals, as the wave B note asks; for gone, a stale ref satisfies it), InvalidControlType, AutomationUnavailable, and InvalidArguments for a name regex that runs too long. A check that cannot tell (NotResponding, OperationTimedOut, ElementAccessFailed, OperationException, a search that did not cover the window) becomes lastObservedState and the wait goes on. Design changes against the proposal: (1) value_equals on a password field does not compare: the task's design said "compare, never echo", but IUiaTree.ReadValue and IUiaValuePattern.Value forbid reading a password field's value and M1.9 and M3.4 kept that rule ("not even to mask it"), and providers mostly refuse or mask it (WPF throws, Chromium returns bullets), so a comparison would be unreliable as well; password matches are skipped, and when only password fields match the wait ends at once with InvalidArguments. No password value and no expected text appear in a result or message (test: the serialized result never contains the password). Reversing this is a user decision: it needs the never-read rule lifted for comparisons. (2) UIA event wake-ups (StructureChanged, PropertyChanged) are not added: the seam has no event members, so they would need new members on both engines (COM handler objects under UIA3, Automation.Add*EventHandler under UIA2), handler registration and removal are cross-process calls that can block on a hung provider (removal cannot run on the UiaWorker thread that a stuck read holds, and must not run on a UIA event thread), and nothing here can run them against a real desktop; polling at 100-500 ms gives the same results, so events are a latency optimisation left to M7.2 with wait_for_window's. (3) No stableMs: an element that flickers (a list re-created) can satisfy gone for one check; the repo owner left it for later (BACKLOG.md). Server instructions: the waiting line names both wait tools (18 lines, 2,981 characters). SKILL.md's Wait step teaches it. Shared files touched: ElementSearchModels.cs (ElementQuery.WarmUp), ElementSearchService.cs (WarmUp honoured; Minimal internal), ServiceRegistration (service and tool class), InputGateToolFilter.ReadOnlyTools, Toolsets (elements), ServerIdentity, ErrorCodes.cs (WaitTimedOut doc comment), FakeUiaTree (Name, IsEnabled, IsOffscreen, HasKeyboardFocus and Value settable), ContractServices, the contract tables, NativeCallGuardTests (ElementWaitService reads UI Automation only through IUiaTree). Tests: ElementWaitServiceTests (the real search and registry over FakeUiaTree, a manual clock), WaitForElementToolTests, WaitForElementContractTests, and a gated no-input E2E scenario (FixtureElementWaitTests, not run in this change: the document window the fixture opens by itself waited for and its Save button found, a ref that matches find_ui_elements', gone at the first check, a value_equals timeout). Not done: event wake-ups (M7.2) and real-desktop runs (BACKLOG.md).

**Review fixes (M3 review: `m3-fix-waits`, `m3-fix-policy`).** gone is satisfied, with a warning, when window.handle had already closed (the window wait's InvalidHandle, confirmed with IsWindow) and when withinRef's element is gone, at the start or during the wait; it used to end with InvalidHandle or ElementStale. A ref or withinRef this server session never issued (one from before a restart, or a made-up one) says nothing about any element, so it ends the wait with ElementStale for gone too (ElementLookup.NotIssued), where it satisfied gone. A window counts as closed only when Win32 says so (IsWindow fails, or its handle now names a window of another process or class); UI Automation failing to open a window that is still there is a check that cannot tell, where it satisfied gone or ended the other conditions with WindowNotFound. Without a scope, gone searches the window and its popups on every check whatever is in front (find_ui_elements' default narrows to the window alone while the app is not in front, so a check while another app was in front could miss a popup's element and satisfy gone), and a check that left one of the window's popups out unread (an app the target policy denies, the agent's host) cannot satisfy it. value_equals' full reads of long values share the check's time with the search. A window of an app the target policy denies ends the wait before the first check (TargetDeniedByPolicy), and a ref or withinRef moved to a re-created element is reported (refMoved and a warning, M3.4).

### M3.9 Read element text and tables (TextPattern, ValuePattern, Grid/Table)

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m3-read-text-tables` (Done)

Reading the Outlook message list or reading pane, Explorer's details view, Task Manager or a Notepad or Word document currently means dumping trees (Name only) or taking screenshots.

**Design.** read_element_text(ref|selector, offset=0, maxLength=20000) reads ValuePattern.Value in full, then TextPattern.DocumentRange (Clone, MoveEndpointByUnit to the offset, GetText(maxLength)) for paging, then Name. It returns {text, totalLength, truncated, nextOffset, source} and redacts IsPassword fields. read_table(ref, startRow=0, maxRows=50, maxColumns=20) reads headers through TablePattern.GetColumnHeaders and cells through GridPattern.GetItem, or a DataItem/Row walk under one CacheRequest. It returns {headers, rows, totalRows, truncated}.

*Sources: r2:crit-missing:text-table-reading*

**As shipped (`m3-read-text-tables`).** Two read-only tools in the `elements` toolset (ReadOnly, not destructive, idempotent, closed world like the other UI Automation reads, which also return application text; on the input gate's read-only list; not audited, they act on nothing; read-only mode serves them), in a new tool class Tools/ElementReadTools.cs over the new IElementReadService (Services/ElementReadService.cs and ElementReadService.Table.cs, a singleton in ServiceRegistration). Every read is one call on the UiaWorker under the call's timeout: the ref's window from the registry (Lookup, no UI Automation), the hung-window precheck (UiaTargets.NotRespondingReason: NotResponding), the M3.3 warm-up within a third of the timeout, the ref resolved through IElementRegistry.Resolve (ElementStale, AmbiguousElement, never another element), then the read. *read_element_text*: `ref`, or `window` with find_ui_elements' criteria (`name`, `nameMatch`, `automationId`, `controlType`, `className`), turned into find_ui_elements' own ElementQuery (MaxResults 5, visibleOnly; at integration with M3.5 also `scope`, the same default as find_ui_elements, refused with `ref`) and run through IElementSearchService, not a fork of it; the criteria must find exactly one element (none: ElementNotFound; several: AmbiguousElement with `candidates`, each a FoundElement with its ref; a search cut short by Timeout, SearchLimit, PartsSkipped or, at integration, WindowLimit reads nothing, since another match may exist); `offset` (0+), `maxLength` 20000 (1-50000), `timeoutMs` 5000 (1000-30000) for the search and the read together. Returns `ref`, `controlType`, `text`, `source` (Text, Value, Name, Redacted), `offset`, `totalLength` (when known), `truncated`, `nextOffset`, `isPassword`, `accessibilityWarmup`. Design changes: (1) the Text pattern comes before the Value pattern (the design had Value first), because a browser document reports its URL as its value and because only the Text pattern pages without reading the whole document for every page; where an edit has both, they hold the same text. (2) Paging is done by the provider: the new seam member IUiaTextPattern.GetTextPage(offset, maxLength) moves the start of a fresh DocumentRange (no Clone needed: DocumentRange is a new range per call) by `offset` characters, collapses the end onto it, moves the end by `maxLength` characters and reads that range, then probes one more character only for a full page; it returns the provider's own character counts, so `nextOffset` is exact even for providers that count `\r\n` as one character, and `totalLength` is reported only once the end was reached, instead of reading the whole document to measure it. Implemented in both engines (Uia3Tree over IUIAutomationTextRange, UiaTree over TextPatternRange; both are UI Automation action owners in NativeCallGuardTests, and the E2E WrapperCatalog classifies GetTextPage as a read: it moves the endpoints of the client's own range, never the selection). (3) An empty document falls through to the value and a failing pattern to the next source, but an element with a Text or Value pattern is never read by its name: an empty edit box reads as `""`, not as its label. (4) The password flag (ReadIdentity) is read first; a password field is never read through any source (success, `source: Redacted`, `isPassword: true`, no `text`), and one whose flag cannot be read is not read either (ElementAccessFailed). The Value and Name sources are cut in UTF-16 characters and never between a surrogate pair. *read_table*: `ref` (required), `startRow` 0, `maxRows` 50 (1-200), `maxColumns` 20 (1-50), `timeoutMs` 10000 (1000-30000). Through the Grid pattern when present (GetItem per cell of the page only, since the M3 review only for Win32, WinForms and Chromium grids, below; headers through TablePattern.GetColumnHeaders, else a `Header` among the grid's first three children; RowCount and ColumnCount as `totalRows` and `totalColumns`), else a walk of the element's DataItem, ListItem and TreeItem children, read in one cached call (GetChildElements) when there are at most 1000 children and one at a time beyond the page otherwise (then `totalRows` is left out); a `Header` child or a first row of HeaderItems gives the headers; a row's children are its cells (read in one cached call when at most 64), a row without children is one cell, its own text. Rows inside Group children are not walked. A cell's text is its name, else its value (never a password field's: null), else the names of up to 8 children (since the M3 review the value comes first, below); a cell with no element is null. Bounds: headers and cells cut at 500 characters (`cellsTruncated`), at most 40,000 characters of headers and cells per page (bytes of JSON since the M3 review; `truncationReason: Size`), whole rows only. `truncationReason` is the first of Timeout, Size, MaxRows and MaxColumns that applies, and `nextStartRow` is set whenever rows were left out. A read that times out returns the rows read until then (cooperatively between cells, or from the shared progress when the worker abandons a stuck call, with a 2 s grace). An element with neither a Grid pattern nor rows, and that is no DataGrid, List, Table or Tree, fails with the new UIElementErrorCode PatternNotSupported (13, appended after AmbiguousElement; named generically so element_action can share it). Tests: ElementReadServiceTextTests and ElementReadServiceTableTests over FakeUiaTree with the new FakeTextPattern (optionally counting `\r\n` as one character), FakeGridPattern and FakeTablePattern; ElementReadToolsTests (argument rules); Uia3Tree tests of GetTextPage over strict COM mocks; ElementReadContractTests (JSON shape, the redacted field end to end, and the largest results: a million-character document and a 1000 x 100 grid of long or empty cells through the real service stay under 16,000 tokens); payload budgets 1,200 (read_element_text, 802 measured: a 3,000-character e-mail) and 1,000 (read_table, 643: 50 rows of 5 columns); the contract tables (annotations, toolsets, every-tool arguments, docs catalog, DI, NativeCallGuardTests). A gated no-input E2E scenario (FixtureElementReadTests, not run in this change) reads the fixture's edit box by criteria and its virtualized ListBox as a one-column table. Not done: row headers (TablePattern.GetRowHeaders), rows inside groups, the text attributes and selection of a range, a richer fixture grid (a WPF DataGrid with headers), and real-desktop runs (BACKLOG.md). Observed while measuring: the SDK serialized results with System.Text.Json's default encoder, which writes quotes, `<`, `>`, `&`, `'` and every non-ASCII character as six-byte `\uXXXX` escapes, so non-Latin text cost up to six times its length in the JSON for every reading tool; the M3 review changed the encoder for every tool (below).

**Review fixes (M3 review: `m3-fix-reading`, `m3-fix-policy`).** *read_table never scrolls.* It asked for every cell of a page through GridPattern.GetItem, which makes WPF's DataGrid scroll to the cell and realize its row (WPF's GridView and the Windows Community Toolkit's DataGrid scroll too), although read_table is read-only and runs outside the input gate, also while input is paused. GetItem is now used only for Win32, WinForms and Chromium grids (by the grid's framework id), whose GetItem only looks the cell up. Every other grid is read through its realized row children, each placed by its first cell's GridItem row (the new read-only IUiaGridItemPattern), and a page ends at the first row that is not realized (truncationReason NotRealized, with a warning to scroll the grid with element_action and read on); the E2E guard counts GetItem as a UI Automation action. *Cells and headers.* A cell with the Value pattern reads its value before its name (never a password field's value), since many providers name a cell by what it is: WinForms "Amount Row 1, Not sorted.", WPF "Item: ..., Column Display Index: 0", Explorer the column's name. A cell that disappears is null, and a grid that disappears after rows were read returns them (truncationReason Disappeared, with a warning), where either failed the whole table. Headers that are grid items (Chromium's th cells) are paired with the columns they cover, top row first ("Q1 / Revenue"), and the rows they fill are neither data rows nor counted in totalRows. An expanded tree item's child items are no longer its cells (nested TreeItems are left out; DataItem and ListItem children stay cells, as WinForms, Chromium and treegrid cells are DataItems). *Bounds in bytes.* A text page was read with GetText(-1), as long as the provider made it, and both reads counted UTF-16 characters, so Cyrillic, CJK or emoji text passed the 16,000-token bound of one result. Both engines now read a page through UiaTextPageReader: the range's text with a limit, and a range made to span fewer of the provider's characters when their text is longer than maxLength, so nextOffset stays just past the text and nothing is skipped (textSkipped says when one of the provider's characters alone was longer than the page). read_element_text keeps a page within 60,000 bytes of JSON (ElementReadService.MaxTextBytes) by reading fewer characters, and read_table counts its 40,000 limit in bytes of JSON (MaxTableTextBytes), cutting a page's first row to fit when it alone is over it. *Relaxed JSON.* Every tool result, the typed results and the capture metadata included, is now written with the relaxed encoder (ToolJson.ResultEncoder) that the error results, get_desktop_state and the audit log already used: non-ASCII text, apostrophes, `<`, `>`, `&`, `+` and the backtick are no longer six-byte escapes (quotes, backslashes, control characters, U+2028 and U+2029 and characters outside the Basic Multilingual Plane still are). *Criteria.* read_element_text and element_action answer a criteria search the same way: several matches are AmbiguousElement, even from a search that did not finish (element_action said OperationTimedOut); a search that did not finish with one match or none is OperationTimedOut (read_element_text said ElementNotFound with none, and ElementAccessFailed for skipped parts); ElementNotFound needs a search that covered everything. Both reads refuse a window of a denied app before the warm-up or any read (TargetDeniedByPolicy, M1.12) and report a moved ref (refMoved, M3.4). ElementReadContractTests bound non-ASCII text and short cells too.

### M3.10 Realize virtualized list and tree items

**Priority:** P2 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m3-virtualized` (Done)

Outlook, Explorer, Settings and long WPF/WinUI lists virtualize, so off-screen items do not exist in the UIA tree and find reports a miss for an item that is really there.

**Design.** find_ui_elements gains searchVirtualized=true. When the scope container supports ItemContainerPattern, it loops FindItemByProperty(startAfter, Name|AutomationId, value), then calls VirtualizedItemPattern.Realize() and ScrollItemPattern.ScrollIntoView, and returns a ref. element_action gains a 'realize' action. The fallback scrolls page by page with ScrollPattern and re-queries, bounded by maxScrolls and the deadline. The fixture app's 10k-item list is the test.

*Sources: r1:crit-missing:virtualized-lists, r2:crit-missing:virtualized-items, r1:UIA-G8*

**As shipped (`m3-virtualized`).** No new tool, error code, native wrapper member or toolset change. *Design change: find_ui_elements only reads.* The design had find_ui_elements realize and scroll the item. But find_ui_elements is annotated readOnlyHint (clients may run it without asking), served in read-only mode (WAM_READ_ONLY) and in the `elements` toolset when the user turned `input` off, and is not audited; realizing and scrolling change what the user sees, so doing them there would bypass the user's choices. So the work is split: find_ui_elements `searchVirtualized` (default false) only finds, and every realize and scroll is element_action's, the one tool with the input gate, pause, target policy and deny list, host guard and audit line. *find_ui_elements* (ElementQuery.SearchVirtualized, an init property like WarmUp; ElementSearchService.Virtualized.cs): after a tree search of the named window that finished with fewer than maxResults matches, the window's List, Tree, DataGrid and Table elements (or withinRef's element itself when it is one; at most 8, found with one bounded ElementSearch, those with ItemContainer first; not the scope's popups) that support ItemContainer are asked through IUiaItemContainerPattern.FindItemByProperty(startAfter, AutomationId or else Name, value), looping startAfter, one provider call per item (at most 200 per list), under the search's deadline. Each item is read (ReadElement), tested against the other criteria client-side (UiaConditionEvaluator, without visibleOnly: such items are offscreen by nature), skipped when the tree search found it (runtime id), and registered with Unique null (a list may hold look-alikes; the tree matches lose their uniqueness proof too when such items exist). FoundElement gains `virtualized: true`; FindElementsResult gains `virtualized` (itemsFound, containers with ref, controlType, name, itemContainer, scrollable, and a note: no list, no ItemContainer, the timeout, maxResults already full). Items beyond the room left count one more in matchCount with truncationReason MaxResults. It needs automationId or an exact name (FindItemByProperty compares one whole property, case-sensitively in WPF): InvalidArguments otherwise and without a window (ElementSearchService.VirtualizedArgumentError, also thrown as ArgumentException by the service). Placeholders are mostly unreadable in some providers: an item whose properties cannot be read is skipped (PartsSkipped, below). *element_action realize* (ElementActionService.ChooseRealize, the single implementation for both paths): the VirtualizedItem pattern's Realize, then on the same runner thread the element read again and, when still offscreen, ScrollItem.ScrollIntoView; an offscreen element without VirtualizedItem is scrolled into view (method ScrollItem, warning); otherwise nothing. realize is now verified (`outcomeVerified`: not offscreen), where it was null. By criteria, element_action sets SearchVirtualized for realize when the criteria name an automationId or an exact name, so one call finds and realizes a row. *Scroll fallback* (ElementActionTools.Realize.cs): new parameter `maxScrolls` (realize with criteria only, default 0 = off, max 100; InvalidArguments otherwise). When the criteria search finds nothing, the window's visible lists are found (find_ui_elements' search, scope window); exactly one is required (several: AmbiguousElement with their refs, nothing scrolled; none: ElementNotFound saying so); it is scrolled with IElementActionService.ActAsync(scroll, page) down to its end, then back up, searching below the list ref after each page (the shared ElementQuery with withinRef), until one match is realized through ActAsync(realize), maxScrolls pages, or timeoutMs (which bounds the whole search: each page, search and the final realize get what is left, at least a second). Each page passes element_action's checks, so a pause or refusal ends the search with its code; the call is one audit line (element_action). The list is left where the item was found or the search ended (there is no restore: the Scroll pattern's SetScrollPercent is not exposed by element_action). Tests: ElementSearchVirtualizedTests and the realize region of ElementActionServiceTests over the new FakeVirtualList (10,000 items, ItemContainer, placeholders that Realize puts in the tree, ScrollIntoView brings on screen), including search then realize then a plain search finding the row under the same ref; ElementActionRealizeToolTests (criteria, maxScrolls, every page and the realize through the action service, turning at the end, several lists, a paused scroll); FindUIElementsToolTests (argument rules). A gated E2E scenario (FixtureVirtualizedListTests, not run: no-input searchVirtualized for item 7500 of the fixture's 10,000-item ListBox; input-tier realize by ref, by criteria, and by maxScrolls with nameMatch contains) is the real-desktop check. Review fixes: a lookup that stops early is truncation, as a tree search that did not finish is (VirtualizedLookup.Stop, the more serious reason kept): Timeout for the deadline or a provider TimeoutException, PartsSkipped for a provider that failed (also after the first item, as one that rejects startAfter), an item it matched that could not be read, a list still matching after 200 probes, more than 8 lists with ItemContainer (those on screen are now searched first) or a list search that did not finish; the tree matches then lose their uniqueness proof. So element_action realize by criteria refuses a partial lookup with OperationTimedOut instead of acting on a match not known to be the only one, or reporting ElementNotFound and starting the scroll fallback. virtualized:true is documented as found through the list (usually not realized, else realized but scrolled out of view, which visibleOnly drops from the tree search). Not done (BACKLOG.md): real-desktop runs (Explorer's and Outlook's placeholders, which may answer FindItemByProperty with elements whose properties fail, are unverified; such a list is now PartsSkipped, so element_action refuses it by criteria and the ref route is the way), a scroll restore, scrolling a list in a popup, a case-insensitive FindItemByProperty retry, and choosing one of several lists for the fallback (element_action has no withinRef or listRef; navigation tree plus content list windows such as Explorer and Outlook, and web pages with a navigation list beside their results, are AmbiguousElement).

**Review fixes (M3 review: `m3-fix-action`, `m3-fix-refs`).** realize of an offscreen item it could not act on (no VirtualizedItem or ScrollItem pattern) is outcomeVerified false, where it reported true (M3.6). A list item named by its text that a tree search proved alone is checked with its list through FindItemByProperty before its selector is kept, so a ref is never moved to an unrealized twin that a later scroll realizes, and the list search leaves out the windows the window owns, as every window search does (M3.4). The review deferred the choice of the list for maxScrolls: a listRef (or withinRef) parameter on element_action, or, as a cheaper partial fix, keeping only the lists with a vertical Scroll pattern of their own (BACKLOG.md).

### M3.11 Capture registry, image-space clicks and Set-of-Marks tied to element refs

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m3-capture-registry`, `m3-set-of-marks` (Done)

The server, not the model, should convert image coordinates to screen coordinates, and it should reject stale captures. Numbered marks are the same refs element_action accepts, so the agent can pick a target visually and then act on it semantically.

**Design.** ICaptureRegistry (LRU of 32 entries, 10-minute TTL) stores CaptureRecord{id, ts, hwnd?, origin, scale, windowRectAtCapture, elements?}. Mouse tools accept space=screen|window|client|image: image needs captureId, and window and client resolve through GetWindowRect or the client rectangle mapped with MapWindowPoints (as M1.2 does, so mirrored windows work). If the window has moved more than 2 px since the capture, the call is refused with StaleCapture. Results echo both the requested point and the resolved screen point. capture_with_elements draws overlays after resizing, so marks survive downscaling. Each mark is a filled, contrast-colored tag showing the element's registry ref. Defaults: labels=true, maxElements=80, and actionableOnly when controlTypes is empty. drawOverlays=false returns a clean image with the marks only in the JSON, and the legend has one compact line per mark (`[w1e17] Button 'Save' #btnSave center=(x,y)`). There is no click_mark tool; element_action(ref) acts on a mark directly. capture_window accepts ref plus padding to crop to one element, and take_screenshot with monitorIndex=-1 captures the whole virtual screen.

*Sources: r1:R17, r1:R18, r2:R13, r2:R14, r2:R15, r1:SCR-G1, r1:SCR-G2, r1:SCR-G8, r1:INP-G2, r2:SCR-G2, r2:SCR-G3, r2:SCR-G7, r2:UIA-G10, r2:INP-G8, r2:SCR-3, r2:SCR-5, r2:SCR-missed-capture-timing-skew, r2:SCR-missed-faint-overlays*

**As shipped, part 1 (`m3-capture-registry`): the capture registry and coordinate spaces.** Already done before this item: the true capture origin and imageToScreen (M1.2, the transform an image point is converted through), the visible-frame origin and the 64-megapixel budget (M2.9). New: ICaptureRegistry/CaptureRegistry (singleton; 32 captures, least recently used dropped; 10-minute time to live on the monotonic clock, counted from the capture, not from the last use) stores CaptureRecord {captureId, capturedAt, geometry: image width and height, imageToScreen, capturedRegion, window? {handle, processId, windowRect, includeFrame}, virtualScreen?}. Ids are `c<n>-<tag>`: n counts the captures of the server run, so an id is never reused, and the tag is random per run, so an id kept from an earlier run finds nothing (a refinement: plain counters would let a stale id from a restarted server name a new capture). ScreenshotService registers every successful capture (take_screenshot, capture_region, capture_window, capture_with_elements) and reports `captureId` (additive, last field of ScreenshotResult and AnnotatedScreenshotResult); a failure registers nothing, and a window capture whose rectangle cannot be read gets no captureId. The window's rectangle and process are read right before the pixels, as the wrapper reads the origin. The mouse tools (click, right_click, double_click, mouse_move, scroll; there is no drag tool yet, M4) take `space` (strict ToolEnum CoordinateSpace screen|window|client|image, nullable: omitted means image with a captureId, else screen) and `captureId`. CoordinateSpaceResolver (no new native wrapper members) converts: image through the record's imageToScreen (ImageToScreenTransform.ToScreen, rounded as documented), only inside the image (CoordinatesOutOfBounds otherwise); window from GetWindowRect's corner; client from the client rectangle mapped with MapWindowPoints (INativeHitTestWrapper.GetClientAreaOnScreen, left < right, so mirrored windows work as in M1.2); a minimized window has no window or client point (CoordinatesOutOfBounds). New InputErrorCode values StaleCapture (46) and CaptureNotFound (47). Staleness, refined beyond "moved more than 2 px": any edge of the window rectangle more than 2 px from the capture's (moved or resized), the window closed, its handle now belongs to another process (a reused handle at the same place), it is minimized (restoreIfMinimized captured it and minimized it again), and for a client capture its client area moved (a toolbar appeared without the window moving); a capture without a window is stale when the virtual screen's bounds changed (monitors moved, added or rescaled). Refinement: the check runs when the point is converted and again inside the input gate, through the new InputTarget.PointRecheck that InputTargetGuard.CheckMouse runs after the target's own checks (InvalidHandle, selector recheck, policy, host, modal) and before the hit test, so a window that moves while the call waits up to 30 s for the gate is caught; window and client points get the same recheck (StaleCapture when the window moved meanwhile). Target: a window capture's window becomes the input's windowHandle when the call names none, so the converted point goes through the guarded path exactly as a screen point with that target (TargetOccluded when another window covers it); a call that names another window is InvalidArguments (an image of window A never becomes a click on window B); a target the capture supplied never lifts the host guard (allowHostTarget still needs the window named by handle). Results echo the point: x and y are the converted screen point, and `requested` {x, y, space, captureId} is set for any space but screen. InvalidArguments for image without captureId, a captureId with another space, window or client without a window, and scroll's space or captureId without x and y. take_screenshot monitorIndex -1 captures the whole virtual screen (capturedRegion its bounds, no monitorIndex or scaleFactor, RegionPartlyOffMonitors when monitors of different sizes leave black areas), refused with CaptureFailed over the 64-megapixel or 16384-pixel budget before anything is allocated. The audit log keeps a mouse call's `space` (a fixed value); captureId is recorded as its length, and the converted x and y are the result's numbers. The server instructions' coordinate line now names captureId (same length budget). Tests: the registry (ids, limit, expiry, threads), the resolver (each space, every staleness case, the recheck), the guard's point recheck, the tools (spaces, target rules, echo, refusals), the service's registration and the virtual screen, and an in-process contract test from capture_window through click; gated no-input E2E scenarios (FixtureCaptureIdTests, not run here) convert the fixture's swatch centres back from a scaled capture and a client point. Left for wave D, which built them in part 2 (below): Set-of-Marks marks and legend tied to element refs, overlays drawn after resizing, labels/maxElements/actionableOnly/drawOverlays and capture_window's ref plus padding; elements in the capture record were declined there.

**As shipped, part 2 (`m3-set-of-marks`): Set-of-Marks tied to element refs, and the ref crop.** Already done before this part: every element capture_with_elements draws already had its M3.4 ref (the walk registers each element it returns, and AnnotatedElement carried `ref`), and the walk already had a maxElements bound (M3.1). *Marks.* ScreenshotService.CaptureWindowWithElements now collects the marks first (each element's part inside the capture at full resolution, then scaled to the returned image as before) and draws them through the new Services/ElementMarkRenderer on the returned image after ResizeImage (Render annotates the scaled bitmap), so the 2-pixel boxes and the tags keep their size at any maxDimension; the old pen widened by the scale (OverlayPenWidth) is gone. Each mark is its type's box and a tag filled with the box's color, opaque, showing the element's ref in Segoe UI bold 11 px, black or white by WCAG relative luminance (black above 0.179); all boxes are drawn before all tags, so no box crosses a tag. Refinement: tags avoid each other: above the box's top-left corner, else inside its top, below it, inside its bottom, the first that lies in the image and covers no earlier tag (else the first in the image), moved left at the right edge, so neighbouring toolbar buttons do not hide each other's refs. An element without a ref (no runtime id) gets a box without a tag. The service's `highlightInteractable` flag became ElementOverlayOptions {Draw, Labels, HighlightInteractable}; `drawOverlays: false` (Draw) renders no overlay at all, the image as captured. *Defaults* (tool parameters): `labels` true, `maxElements` 80 (clamped to 1-5000 like get_ui_elements; the walk used to have no limit here), and `actionableOnly`, nullable, true when controlTypes is empty: the new UIElementFilter.ActionableOnly keeps an element with an action pattern (UiaPatterns.Actions: Invoke, Toggle, Value, SelectionItem, ExpandCollapse and RangeValue; the design's list plus RangeValue, the existing set, so sliders are marked), enabled or not (a disabled element is a mark with a dimmed box and `[disabled]` in the legend), read from the patterns fetched with the level, or by one IUiaTree.ReadElement for an element read on its own (a failed read leaves it out and counts it skipped, as interactableOnly does). It differs from interactableOnly, which also keeps focusable-only elements (panes, documents) and drops disabled ones. Without controlTypes, labels, panes and groups are therefore no longer drawn; `actionableOnly: false` gives the earlier set. *Legend.* The tool adds `legend` (one string, a line per mark in the marks' order, built by Tools/ElementMarkLegend): `[w4120035e17] Button 'Save' #btnSave center=(392,196)`, `[-]` for an element without a ref, `[disabled]` for a disabled one, the name cut at 60 characters and quoted and escaped as in get_ui_elements' text format (whose Quote and NeedsQuotes became internal for it). Refinement: `center` is the middle of the element's part inside the captured region, taken in screen pixels and converted to the returned image through the inverse of imageToScreen (rounded), so a mouse tool given it with the captureId converts it back to the element's middle (an in-process contract test clicks a legend centre of a 1600-to-1568 scaled capture and lands on (400, 300)); the middle of the rounded imageBounds would land a pixel or two off. No click_mark tool: element_action(ref) acts on a mark, and the legend centre with the captureId is the point path. *Payload.* `elements` (imageBounds, screenBounds, overlayColor) is left out unless `detail: "full"` (new strict ToolEnum CaptureElementDetail legend|full, InvalidArguments otherwise); `elementCount` still counts the marks. The typical result fell from 6,544 to 2,312 tokens (budget 9,500 lowered to 3,500); the legend's quotes cost six bytes each through the SDK's default JSON encoder (the wave C note). This changes capture_with_elements' default output and element set, which the Changes section of docs/TOOLS.md states. *Not done, on purpose:* the design's "elements in the capture record": nothing would read them. element_action resolves a mark's ref through the element registry, which keeps the element and finds it again (a copy in the capture record would only go stale), and an image-space click needs only the transform the record already keeps. *The ref crop.* capture_window takes `ref` and `padding` (0-500, default 8, only with ref; InvalidArguments otherwise, and for a ref that is not well formed). The ref is looked up without UI Automation first (an unknown or expired ref is ElementStale at once); its window is the capture's when handle and window are left out, and a handle or window that names another window is InvalidArguments, since the element is not in that window's image (a popup's element belongs to the popup's window). The target policy (TargetDeniedByPolicy), restoreIfMinimized and the window checks (InvalidHandle, WindowMinimized, WindowHidden, Cloaked) run before the element is read; then IElementRegistry.ResolveAsync (5 s, with the window's hung check) gives the live element (ElementStale, AmbiguousElement, NotResponding, OperationTimedOut otherwise), whose bounds read now, plus the padding, are the area; an offscreen element or one without bounds is the new ScreenshotErrorCode ElementOffscreen (17, the name element_action's UIElementErrorCode already uses). The new IScreenshotService.CaptureWindowArea captures the window exactly as capture_window (PrintWindow, so covering windows do not show; the visible-frame crop), cuts it to the area clipped to the captured window or client area (ElementOffscreen when nothing is left), and registers the crop with the window's record: capturedRegion and imageToScreen start at the crop's corner, so image points of the crop convert to the screen, and the staleness checks are a window capture's. The result gains `ref`, the ref the element resolved to (the one passed, or the element's own when it was found again under another). Captures are still not audited; no new tool, toolset or native wrapper member; the server instructions are unchanged (their act line already names element_action and refs). Tests: the walk's actionable filter (cached and one at a time), the renderer (tag size after scaling, line width, labels off, no ref, drawOverlays off, placement, text contrast), the crop (origin, registration, clipping, outside, minimized), the tool (defaults, explicit actionableOnly, maxElements range, overlay switches, legend and detail, every ref refusal and its order), the legend (format, quoting, centre through a scaled transform, a clipped element), in-process contract tests (a legend centre clicked with the captureId; a ref crop clicked at its middle), the payload budget, and gated no-input E2E scenarios (FixtureSetOfMarksTests, not run here: the fixture's button is marked with find_ui_elements' ref and its centre converts into it; a ref crop of the edit box is its bounds plus padding). Not checked on a real desktop: how legible the tags are on real windows and fonts, and how crowded 80 marks are in a dense window (BACKLOG.md).

**Review fixes (M3 review: `m3-fix-surface`, `m3-fix-action`).** *Client-area ref crop.* capture_window with `ref` and includeFrame false registered the crop as the captured region, and a client capture's staleness check compares the client area with that region, so every point of a real crop was StaleCapture; the record now keeps the client area as captured, before the crop (CapturedWindow.ClientArea), and checks that. *Marks of a window that moved.* capture_with_elements reads its elements before the pixels, so a window that moved or resized in between showed the marks and the legend centres beside their controls, while the captureId converted points as usual. The window's rectangle is now read before the walk and compared with the one read with the pixels: a window that moved or resized by more than 2 px, or whose handle now belongs to another process, gives a capture without a captureId and a WindowMoved warning (D-SCR-17). *Screen captures.* The record keeps each monitor's device name and bounds, so a screen captureId is StaleCapture once a monitor moved, or two swapped places, inside the same virtual screen (a capture whose monitors could not be read keeps its captureId and compares only the virtual screen); and a restoreIfMinimized capture's warning says that its captureId converts points only while the window is restored to the same place. *Before every press.* A converted point is checked against its capture again before every press (InputTargetGuard.RecheckMouse runs InputTarget.PointRecheck), the second click of a double click too, not only once inside the gate. The server instructions' act line allows an image point with its captureId (it said "not image points", against the coordinates line), and the mouse tools' and get_ui_elements' descriptions name refs, captureIds and the legend.

### M3.12 get_desktop_state: one-call observation

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m3-desktop-state` (Done)

Getting oriented takes three or four calls today, two of them heavy. One compact observation with actionable refs becomes the recommended first call.

**Design.** Read-only get_desktop_state(window? (default: foreground), include=[windows, focus, elements, popups] plus optional screenshot, maxElements=80, interactableOnly=true, screenshotMaxLongEdge=1280). It returns: the foreground window {handle, title, process}; the focused element; the cursor position; held modifiers and Caps Lock; the pause state; the top N user-facing windows in z-order as compact rows [handle, title, process, state, monitor]; the target's open popups; the target's interactive elements in text format with refs; and, optionally, a Set-of-Marks image block. Action tools gain observe=none|state|screenshot, which returns this state in the same response. SKILL.md makes it the first call.

*Sources: r1:R19, r1:R37, r2:R53, r1:INP-G6, r1:MCP-G4, r2:MSP-G9*

**As shipped (`m3-desktop-state`).** New read-only tool `get_desktop_state` (Tools/DesktopStateTools.cs; `elements` toolset; read-only, idempotent, closed world; on the input gate's read-only list, not audited) over IDesktopStateService/DesktopStateService (a singleton in ServiceRegistration). *Parameters:* handle or window (default: the window in front), include (strict ToolEnum DesktopStateSection windows|focus|elements|popups|screenshot; default the first four), maxElements 80 (1-500), interactableOnly true, scope (find_ui_elements' ElementSearchScope; desktop refused), maxWindows 20 (1-100, added: the design had no bound for the rows), screenshotMaxLongEdge 1280 (256-1568), timeoutMs 5000 (1000-30000, the one deadline). *Always read* (cheap, Win32 only): the window in front {handle, title, processName, processId, isAgentHost}, the cursor (INativeInputWrapper.GetCursorPos), the pause (IInputSession.PauseState), the modifiers down (IInputSession.ReadModifiersDown, get_control_status' read) and Caps Lock (new INativeInputWrapper.IsKeyToggled over GetKeyState's toggle bit, classified Read in the E2E WrapperCatalog; NativeInputWrapper is an action wrapper, so NativeCallGuardTests needed no entry). *Sections, side by side under one deadline:* windows (IWindowService.GetAllWindows, front first, compact rows {handle, title, process, state, monitor, isAgentHost}, plus windowCount; typed objects rather than the design's positional arrays, which have no schema and are easy to misread); focus (IElementInspectionService.GetFocusedElementAsync: ref, controlType, name, automationId, bounds, window, process, isPassword; the value is never included here); popups (IPopupService.GetActivePopupsAsync, with refs); elements (IUIElementService.GetUIElementsAsync, get_ui_elements' walk with its hung-window precheck, warm-up and ref registration, interactableOnly, visibleOnly, depth 10, rendered by UIElementTextFormat); screenshot (IScreenshotService.CaptureWindow, capture_window's path: a registered captureId, output image only, never a file). *Scope:* the elements section uses find_ui_elements' scope with the same default (IPopupService.IsInForeground: windowAndPopups when the window or a popup is in front): the open popups (GetPopupList, tooltips skipped) are walked first, front first, within half of maxElements, then the window with what is left, each block under a '# popup <handle> <kind>' or '# window <handle> <title>' header line (headers only when a popup was walked); popup refs are registered under the popup's own window, as M3.5 does. *Deadline:* the UI Automation parts get the deadline less a fifth (at most 1 s) so a walk asked to stop can still return what it read (truncationReason Timeout); whatever has not answered at the deadline is reported as OperationTimedOut in `errors` [{section, errorCode, error}] and abandoned (its refs still register); a part that fails is listed with its own code (NotResponding, InvalidHandle, WindowMinimized, ...). success is false only when a named handle is no window (InvalidHandle). *Refinements against the design:* (a) the window in front is not read unasked when it is the agent's host (TargetIsAgentHost: while an agent works its terminal is usually in front, and its tree is the conversation, not the app) or a window the target policy denies (TargetDeniedByPolicy: a sweep the agent did not aim reads neither, as M3.5's desktop scope); a named window is read as get_ui_elements reads it; the error has section `target` and the rest is returned, with the window rows to pick a handle from; the focused element follows the same rule for the window that holds it (the window in front when the read does not say): unasked it is not read at all, and when another window was named a focus found in a host or denied window, or in one that cannot be read to be checked, is dropped with a `focus` error (review fix: the focus section first read a denied window's focused element); window rows list denied apps' windows like find_windows does; (b) the screenshot follows capture_window: never of a policy-denied window (named or not) nor of one whose process cannot be read to check it (CaptureFailed), and refused before anything is read (InvalidArguments) when capture_window is not served (capture toolset off, excluded, or read-only mode: capture_window is not read-only because it can write files, and the user who turned captures off did not want pixels), the precedent of element_action's paste following set_clipboard; Set-of-Marks are not drawn (M3.11 part 2, sibling branch); (c) the result's JSON (and element_action's `observed`) was written with relaxed escaping (DesktopStateTools.StateJsonOptions), so the quoted names of the element lines are not six-byte \u0027 escapes (wave C note); since the M3 review every tool result is written that way (ToolJson.SerializerOptions, M3.9), and StateJsonOptions is gone. No new error code. *observe on element_action* (the only action tool that has it; others may gain it later): ObserveMode none (default)|state|screenshot (strict ToolEnum). The tool now returns a CallToolResult (ToolResultPayload ElementActionResult; JSON unchanged without observe) and, after the action has returned and released the gate, adds `observed` (ElementActionResult.Observed, serialized last): get_desktop_state's default observation of the element's window, or of the window in front once the action closed that window (IWindowService.IsValidWindow), plus with screenshot the capture as image content first. It observes failed actions too (helpful after ElementNotFound), but an action refused for its window (TargetDeniedByPolicy, TargetIsAgentHost) observes without naming that window, so the default rules for the window in front apply. observe screenshot is refused before anything is done when captures are off. The audit log keeps `observe` (AuditRecord.NamedStringArguments), never the observation. *Instructions and skill:* the locate line names get_desktop_state, the verify line checks the new state with it, and the last line makes it the first call and still says to call get_server_info once (build, DPI, pause, host windows, toolsets, warnings); made room by tightening wording (2,997 of 3,000 characters, every rule kept). `include` is the complete list of sections (the docs say so; `["screenshot"]` alone reads only the capture). The '# popup'/'# window' headers quote titles with UIElementTextFormat.Quote, which now also turns line and paragraph separators (U+2028, U+2029) into spaces in every element line. SKILL.md's start section, act, verify and "See What Is in a Window" workflow use it. *Payload:* the typical observation (15 window rows, the focus, 2 popups, 80 element lines) is 2,129 tokens (budget 3,200), against about 7,600 for get_foreground_window, get_all_windows, get_focused_element, get_active_popups and get_ui_elements together. *Tests:* DesktopStateServiceTests (defaults, shared-walk filters and warm-up, focus value never returned, rows and truncation, sections, pause, host and policy targets, no foreground, invalid handle, a part that never answers, failing parts, the cooperative stop, popups first within the budget, scope window, capture options, policy, failed capture, captures off, cancellation), DesktopStateToolsTests, element_action observe tests, DesktopStateContractTests (through the production registrations: typical, screenshot with a registered captureId, unknown include, observe screenshot, no observe unchanged, captures off), the audit contract, and the contract tables. Gated no-input E2E scenario FixtureDesktopStateTests (not run in this change). Not done (BACKLOG.md): observe on other action tools (they would follow get_desktop_state being served and element_action's rule for a refused window), Set-of-Marks marks on get_desktop_state's screenshot (a plain capture_window capture), and a real-desktop run.

**Review fixes (M3 review: `m3-fix-policy`, `m3-fix-surface`, `m3-fix-action`, `m3-fix-failclosed`).** The window in front is checked before the focus is read: when another window was named and the one in front is the agent's host or a denied app, the focus is not read at all (a `focus` error), where it used to be read, warmed up and registered, then dropped. A named window of a denied app is not read (a `target` error), and a window whose process cannot be named is neither chosen nor has its focus read while the policy denies anything (M1.12). Popups of a denied app or of the agent's host are left out of the popups section and the walk, and counted (popupsExcluded). The UI Automation parts hand back what they read before the deadline: a walk or popup read still stuck in a call when it is asked to stop is abandoned after half of what the part budget leaves of the deadline (UIElementFilter.StopGrace, and the popup service's stopGrace), where the 2 s and 1 s graces could outlast the deadline and lose the whole section as OperationTimedOut; the parts count from the call's start, and a stop during the popups starts no window walk. element_action's observe is refused before anything is done when get_desktop_state is not served (the elements toolset off, or an exclusion). `scope` lists only window and windowAndPopups (desktop is still refused).

### M3.13 Launch-and-arrange in one call

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m3-launch-arrange` (Done)

Starting an app and placing it takes four calls today, and the agent races the app's own restore of its saved bounds. One call should launch, place, focus and confirm.

**Design.** launch_application and batch items gain placement {snap?, monitor?, bounds?, state?, focus?} and reuseExisting. Once M2.6 has resolved the window, apply M2.3 geometry and M1.3 activation. Re-check after about 500 ms and re-apply once if the app restored its saved bounds (Chrome, VS Code). Return {finalBounds, finalState, focused, launchStatus}.

**As shipped (`m3-launch-arrange`).** launch_application gains `placement` (LaunchPlacement {snap, monitorIndex, bounds, state, focus}; snap and state are strings with [ToolValues], so a batch item with a bad value fails alone, and the schema lists the values; state is the new ToolEnum LaunchWindowState normal|maximized|minimized, with the set_window_state names as unlisted aliases) and `reuseExisting`; ApplicationLaunchRequest gains both. Strict check before anything is launched (LaunchPlacementPlan.TryCreate): InvalidSnapPosition, InvalidState, InvalidMonitorIndex (negative, or past the monitors IMonitorService lists), and InvalidArguments for snap with bounds, bounds with monitorIndex (bounds are virtual-screen coordinates), maximized with snap or bounds, focus with minimized, a size below 1 px, and a placement that asks for nothing; the single tool throws a tool error and launches nothing, a batch item fails alone and is not launched, and two batch items with focus fail the call (only one window can end in front). A placement implies the window wait. The new ILaunchPlacementService/LaunchPlacementService (no native calls of its own) applies it through IWindowService (snap: SnapWindow; bounds: SetWindowFrame, the visible frame; monitorIndex alone: MoveWindowToMonitor center, or maximize with state maximized; maximized alone: SetWindowState; normal alone: Restore until normal, at most twice; minimized: after the geometry; all with bringToFront raise-only) and IForegroundService.EnsureForeground, as focus_window does (policy, hidden, cloaked, pause and the foreground lock apply). The launch tools already run inside the input gate (the gate filter), so the placement does too. Before a window is touched: InvalidHandle (it closed), PausedByUser, ProtectedWindow (refinement: a window of the agent's own host, which `wt` or an openedInTab launch can return, is never moved or focused automatically; the window tools still can when named), TargetDeniedByPolicy. Re-check: the service waits DefaultRecheckDelay (500 ms) once for all placed windows, compares each window's state, visible frame and monitor with what it read right after the placement (so an app's minimum size does not count as "moved back"), and places a moved window once more (reapplied); a pause during the wait skips the second placement (PausedByUser). Focus comes last, in order. The first failing step ends that window's placement (a failed snap is not followed by focus). An unexpected exception while placing is MoveFailed and while focusing FocusFailed (per window), and AppLauncherTools turns any exception of the placement service other than cancellation into MoveFailed for its windows, so it never hides the launch. Result: LaunchResultDto and LaunchResultItem gain `placement` {finalBounds (visible frame, none when minimized), finalState, monitorIndex, focused (IsInFront, also without focus), reapplied, errorCode?, error?}. Partial success: a placement failure leaves success true and isError unset (ToolOutcome reads only the top-level success), with placement.errorCode (window-tool codes, WindowNotFound when the launch found no window, ProtectedWindow, InvalidHandle) and an error that says never to launch again. The batch places after all windows were found (so a placement cannot race another item's wait), then rechecks them together once. reuseExisting (ProcessService.FindOpenWindow, after validation, before the start): a window counts when it is in IWindowService.GetApplicationWindows (Alt-Tab set: visible, uncloaked so on this desktop, unowned, not tool or shell, not the server's; minimized included), is not the agent's host or denied by the target policy (EvaluateTargetPolicy), belongs to the same app (the program's full path via GetProcessImagePath, its name, or a same-app handoff name from LaunchWindowOwner.SameAppProcessNames: calc, code, wt, ms-settings; never the host handoffs cmd/powershell/pwsh/wsl to WindowsTerminal or http/https to browsers), matches expectWindow.processName when set (required when the app is unknown: documents, Store apps, web addresses), and shows what the launch opens (expectWindow.titleContains as a substring; otherwise every name of ProcessService.ReuseTitleNames, the DocumentHints names of non-option arguments and of a document or web-address target (since the M3 review a web address only with titleContains, below), each as a whole name (ProcessService.TitleShowsName, since the M3 review LaunchTitles.Shows: each side is the title's end, a character that cannot continue a name, or whitespace not followed by a letter or digit, so todo.txt is not shown by old-todo.txt, todo.txt.bak or my todo.txt, nor MyApp by MyApp2 or MyApp.Tests; review fix, the first version matched substrings). A Store app (shell:AppsFolder\<AUMID>) or a bare scheme (ms-settings:) opens nothing, so it needs no title name; a non-web URI with content (ms-settings:display, mailto:) or arguments that name nothing are never reused without titleContains. When windows of the app were passed over for their titles, the warning says how many. The front-most match wins (warning with the count); nothing is started; the new LaunchStatus AlreadyOpen (`alreadyOpen`, no processId). In a batch a reused window goes to one item, and only windows open before the batch's first start count (a snapshot of the handles), so an item never reuses a window an earlier item started. Audit: AuditTarget and AuditItem gain AuditPlacement (args: snap and state only as valid canonical names, monitorIndex, bounds, focus; result: finalBounds, finalState, focused, reapplied, errorCode); the result's launchStatus is kept as an identifier, and a launch batch item's launchStatus is its status. No new error codes or native wrapper members. Not done (BACKLOG.md): an app that moves its window later than ~500 ms or more than once is not followed; placing by monitor identity (M6.7/M6.8); placement for apply_layout's launchMissing (it has its own placements). A gated E2E scenario (FixturePlacementTests) places and focuses the fixture through the service.

**Review fixes (M3 review: `m3-fix-launch`, `m3-fix-failclosed`).** reuseExisting took a path separator as a name boundary, so "C:\other\todo.txt - Notepad++" was reused for C:\notes\todo.txt, and a browser window whose page title showed a web address's host (which the page sets) was reused for that address. The title rules now live in LaunchTitles, shared with the launch wait (M2.6): a name shown after a path counts only when that path is the launched one (a relative argument taken in the working directory); a window that shows only the name is still reused, after any window that shows the full path, with a warning that names its title and says to check it (the repo owner's decision); and a web address reuses a window only with expectWindow.titleContains, with a warning that the page controls that title. A window whose process cannot be named is neither placed nor reused while the policy denies anything (M1.12). Left for later by the repo owner (BACKLOG.md): a guard on the programs launch_application starts, which runs cmd, PowerShell and other interpreters with any arguments.

*Sources: r1:R51, r2:R37, r1:PAL-G2, r2:PAL-G2*

### M3.14 Common file dialog helper (localization-safe)

**Priority:** P2 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** extend · **Branch:** `m3-dialogs` (Done)

Open and Save As dialogs appear in almost every end-to-end task, and composing them from element actions costs 4-6 error-prone calls.

**Design.** file_dialog(window? (default: the foreground dialog, found through the popup scope), path, action=open|save, overwrite=false, timeoutMs). Locate the #32770/IFileDialog window and set the filename edit (AutomationId 1148, or ComboBoxEx32 > Edit) through ValuePattern with the full path. Invoke the default button by AutomationId ('1'/IDOK), not by caption. Handle the 'already exists, replace?' prompt according to overwrite, using respond_to_dialog, and wait for the dialog to be gone. Return {completed, finalPath, promptHandled}. Validate the path the same way as screenshot outputPath. No English captions anywhere.

*Sources: r1:crit-missing:file-dialog, r2:non-goal:english-only-dialogs*

**As shipped (`m3-dialogs`).** *file_dialog(path, action open|save (required), overwrite=false, handle or window (required since the M3 review), allowHostTarget, timeoutMs 10000, 1000-60000)* (Tools/DialogTools.cs over IFileDialogService/FileDialogService; `input` toolset, destructive, not idempotent, closed world, SelfGatedTools, audited). *Path* (FileDialogPaths.Check, before anything is touched; the tool throws InvalidArguments for its form): as the captures' outputPath, fully qualified and on a drive letter (network and device paths refused: checking them, or the dialog opening them, makes Windows connect to another computer and can leak the NTLM hash; a mapped drive letter is accepted as for outputPath), no wildcard, quote, `<>|` or control character (a dialog takes `*.txt` as a filter, quoted names as several files), no alternate data stream, no environment variable that expands (review fix: the dialog expands %NAME% itself, so the existence and overwrite checks would be of another path), at most 1,024 characters, normalized with GetFullPath. Decision on "may open read any path": open needs the file or folder to exist and is otherwise unrestricted: the server only types the path into the user's own dialog, which the application then opens with the user's rights, exactly as type_text into that box would, so a narrower rule here would protect nothing type_text does not already allow; the network-path rule is kept because the dialog itself resolves the path. Save needs an existing folder and a file name (not a folder); an existing file without overwrite is FileExists before anything is typed (improvement on the design, which only answered the prompt: the prompt is not always shown, and a refusal before typing is cleaner). *Steps*, each act through IElementActionService (so pause, gate, policy, host, BlockedByModal, NotResponding and the audit apply to each): the pause; the dialog located as respond_to_dialog locates one (IDialogService.Locate: the modal dialog of the window named or in front); IInputTargetGuard.CheckTarget on it before anything is read; the file name box by the control ids only file dialogs have (review fix: a bare Edit 1001 is common in ordinary resource dialogs, and 1152 is also the Find dialog's Find What box, so the path could have been typed into any dialog in front and its OK pressed): the Edit inside the ComboBox 1148 (Open) or FileNameControlHost (Save As; its Edit is 1001) or that ComboBox, else an Edit 1148 (cmb13), else an old-style edt1 1152 only next to that dialog's file type or drive ComboBox (1136, 1137); DialogNotFound with nothing typed otherwise (an ordinary dialog, a folder picker without a name box, a UWP picker); element_action set_value (mode value: the Value pattern, WM_SETTEXT through the Win32 proxy) with the full path, read back (outcomeVerified false is DialogNotCompleted, nothing pressed); the button with AutomationId 1 (IDOK, Button or SplitButton, exactly one; ElementNotFound otherwise) pressed by element_action invoke with a 2 s timeout (FileDialogService.PressTimeout: a Win32 button's Invoke blocks while the replace prompt is open; ActionTimedOut there is watched, not a failure); then the dialog is polled (100 ms) until it is gone or hidden (completed) or timeoutMs passes (DialogNotCompleted: a folder path shows the folder, or the app is busy). A prompt (the pending press's openedPopup, or a new Dialog popup of the file dialog) is checked with CheckTarget first (review fix: a prompt of a denied process, such as a credential prompt for a network drive, is neither read nor answered; its refusal code ends the call), then read with IDialogService.ReadAsync; for save, the first one with the standard yes and no buttons (by id: IDYES/IDNO or CommandButton_6/7, so no English caption is read anywhere) while File.Exists(finalPath) (review fix: another Yes/No question, such as "save in Pictures instead?", is handed back instead of being answered Yes or reported as FileExists) is the replace prompt and is answered through IDialogService.RespondAsync: "yes" with overwrite (and the wait goes on), "no" otherwise (FileExists, promptHandled true, the file dialog left open); any other prompt (an invalid name, a missing folder, open's create question) is not answered: DialogNotCompleted with `prompt` (handle, title, text, buttons) for respond_to_dialog. Each read or action is bounded by what is left of timeoutMs, between 1 s and 5 s. Result {success, action, dialogHandle, completed, finalPath (the normalized path typed; the application may still add an extension and writes the file after the dialog closes, said in the docs; a completed save with no file at finalPath yet adds a warning, from a read-only File.Exists), promptHandled, prompt, warnings}. The dialog is noted for the audit line right after it is located, before the guard. *Audit (privacy decision):* `path` is recorded as `pathLength` only (the default rule for strings; a file name can say what the user works on), with `action`, `overwrite`, `completed` and `promptHandled`; the dialog window is noted. Coverage: FileDialogPathsTests (real temporary files), FileDialogServiceTests (fakes: ids, order of actions, the replace prompt both ways, other prompts handed back, refusals before typing), DialogToolsTests, AuditLogContractTests (no file name in the line). No gated E2E scenario: the fixture app opens no Open or Save As dialog, and adding one to it was out of this item's scope (follow-up: a fixture button that shows a SaveFileDialog would let FixtureDialogTests cover file_dialog end to end). Not done: UWP/WinUI FileOpenPicker (PickerHost) and other non-#32770 pickers; telling an Open dialog from a Save As dialog in general (without captions only FileNameControlHost marks a Save As dialog: since the M3 review `open` is refused there, and a save is confirmed by the file it writes).

**Review fixes (M3 review: `m3-fix-dialogs`, `m3-fix-policy`).** file_dialog needs `handle` or `window` (InvalidArguments otherwise), since the file dialog in front may be another application's. The replace prompt is answered only when, besides the standard yes and no buttons and the file existing, its text names the file: its name or, as Explorer hides known extensions, its name without the extension, as a whole name, in any language (file names are not localized); another Yes/No question (save in Documents instead?, an application's own question) is handed back in `prompt` instead of being answered Yes with overwrite or reported as FileExists. A save is completed only once a file is seen written at finalPath (created, or its write time or length changed since the press); a dialog that closed without that is success true with completed false and a warning, so a save typed into an Open dialog, or one to which the application added another extension, is never reported done. `open` is refused before anything is typed where the file name box is FileNameControlHost, which only a Save As dialog has (DialogNotFound); the id 1148 is not taken for an Open dialog, since the old-style Save As dialog has it too. The audit line names the file dialog as its target (M3.5). Not done (BACKLOG.md): the pickers above, a fixture button that shows a Win32 Save As dialog so a gated scenario covers file_dialog end to end, and a real-desktop check of the ids it relies on (FileNameControlHost with Edit 1001, ComboBox 1148 with Edit 1148).

## M4 Input vocabulary

Keyboard and mouse produce exactly what was asked, on any keyboard layout and button configuration. The tools cover the chords, drags, multi-clicks, modifier-clicks and long text that real tasks need, and nothing can stay held down after a call returns.

### M4.1 Keyboard engine: layout-aware mapping and a full key grammar

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** fix

Ctrl or Alt plus punctuation never fires, 'Hello' types 'hello', AZERTY digits come out wrong, '100%{ENTER}' sends Alt+Enter, and scan codes are always 0. There are also no escapes, groups, Win modifier or numpad keys.

**Design.** A pure KeySpecParser produces InputEvents and is unit-tested through the input seam with a mocked layout. Grammar: escapes {+} {^} {%} {~} {(} {)} {{} {}}; groups such as +(abc); repeats such as {LEFT 5} (capped at 100); ~ as Enter; # as the Win modifier; {KEY down}/{KEY up} within one call, with key-ups appended at the end of the batch; NUMPAD0-9, ADD, SUBTRACT, MULTIPLY, DIVIDE, F13-F24, VK_OEM_* and media/browser keys. Syntax errors return InvalidKeySpecification. send_keys maps every character, modified or not, with VkKeyScanExW on the foreground thread's layout to get the VK plus any Shift/AltGr it needs. wScan comes from MapVirtualKeyExW(MAPVK_VK_TO_VSC_EX), with EXTENDEDKEY set for 0xE0 codes. KEYEVENTF_UNICODE is used only for characters not on the layout, so 'j', '/', Office KeyTips and RDP/VM clients work. Caps Lock is taken into account. type_text stays Unicode by default and normalizes \r\n. If the text contains \n or \t, an explicit controlChars=unicode|keys|shiftEnter is required; there is no default newline-to-Enter, which would submit chat messages or move Excel cells. send_keys gains delayMs pacing between top-level tokens. A partial SendInput insert triggers the M1.10 compensating key-ups and returns an error with progress.

*Sources: r1:R27, r2:R32, r1:crit:R27, r2:crit:R32, r1:INP-2, r1:INP-3, r1:INP-4, r1:INP-9, r1:INP-10, r1:INP-8, r1:INP-missed-1, r2:INP-4, r2:INP-5, r2:INP-6, r2:INP-7, r2:INP-8, r2:INP-9, r2:INP-G4*

### M4.2 Mouse correctness: atomic absolute clicks, swapped buttons, real double-click

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** fix

Today a click is SetCursorPos, a 10 ms sleep, then relative button events, so it can land wherever the cursor drifted. Swapped buttons invert click and right_click, and a double-click can exceed the double-click time.

**Design.** A shared InputBatchBuilder sends one SendInput array per operation: MOVE|ABSOLUTE|VIRTUALDESK to the normalized point, then the button events. Primary and secondary map to physical buttons through GetSystemMetrics(SM_SWAPBUTTON). Double and triple clicks go in one batch with gaps under GetDoubleClickTime()-50. After sending, read GetCursorPos into ActualX/Y and return CursorClamped on a mismatch.

*Sources: r1:R29, r2:R33, r1:INP-6, r1:INP-11, r2:INP-10, r2:INP-11*

### M4.3 mouse_click, drag and press_keys, with no cross-call holds

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** extend

Adds drag-and-drop, middle and X buttons, triple clicks, modifier-clicks and structured chords such as 'ctrl+shift+t', which are more reliable for LLMs than SendKeys punctuation. Press-and-hold exists only within a single call.

**Design.** mouse_click(x,y | captureId | ref, button=primary|secondary|middle|x1|x2, clickCount 1-3, modifiers [ctrl, shift, alt, win], window?) sends modifiers down, the click and modifiers up atomically; click, right_click and double_click become thin aliases (consolidated in M6.1). drag(from, to, button, steps=10, stepDelayMs, holdBeforeMoveMs=100, modifiers?, window?) interpolates absolute moves beyond SM_CXDRAG and releases the button in a finally block. press_keys(keys: 'ctrl+shift+t' | ['ctrl','shift','t'] | sequences such as 'tab*3', repeat, holdMs, delayBetweenMs, window?) shares the M4.1 key table. There are no standalone key_down/mouse_down tools. If they are ever added, they need a 10 s TTL watchdog plus release on ApplicationStopping and stdin EOF. All of these go through the M1 guards. Document that Win+L and Ctrl+Alt+Del cannot be injected.

*Sources: r1:R28, r1:R29, r2:R34, r1:crit:R28, r1:crit:R29, r1:INP-G3, r1:INP-G4, r2:INP-G3, r2:INP-G4, r2:MSP-G10*

### M4.4 type_text paste mode and bounded long text

**Priority:** P2 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** extend

Long or multi-line text typed key by key is slow, and autocomplete and auto-indent corrupt it. A runaway type_text cannot be bounded until a paste path exists.

**Design.** type_text gains method=unicode|keys|paste|auto, where auto pastes above about 200 characters. paste saves the text clipboard, sets the new text with ExcludeClipboardContentFromMonitorProcessing, sends Ctrl+V with scan codes through the guard, waits about 300 ms and restores the old text; the restore is best effort and documented as lossy. keys uses VkKeyScanEx, for RDP and VM targets. With delay=0, text goes out in 64-character batches. Results return Length and CharactersTyped (counting runes) instead of echoing Text. The 5000-character keystroke cap ships together with paste mode, never before it.

*Sources: r1:R31, r2:R35, r2:R11, r2:crit:R11, r1:INP-G5, r1:INP-G8, r2:INP-G5*

## M5 Browser bridge

Reliable CDP for debug-enabled Chromium, bound to the right window and tab, with a few high-value tools: readiness, tabs, navigation, waits and paged content. The UIA fallback is locale-independent, and anything richer is handed to Playwright MCP explicitly. After M2 this milestone can run in parallel with M3/M4.

### M5.1 Debug-browser launch readiness and first-run suppression

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** S · **Kind:** fix

launch_browser_with_debug reports success without checking that the endpoint answers. Fresh profiles hit welcome, default-browser and search-engine-choice screens. Per-user installs are missed.

**Design.** Always pass --no-first-run, --no-default-browser-check and --disable-search-engine-choice-screen. Default to port=0 and read <userDataDir>/DevToolsActivePort, which removes the IsPortInUse race and the well-known 9222; an explicit port is validated to 1024-65535. Poll /json/version for up to about 10 s and return cdpReady, browserVersion, browserWsEndpoint and cdpEndpoint (http://127.0.0.1:<port>); otherwise fail with DebugLaunchFailed and a reason. Resolve the executable through App Paths (HKCU, then HKLM), then ProgramFiles, ProgramFiles(x86) and LocalApplicationData, with an executablePath override, and list the paths tried in errors. Refuse a browser's default 'User Data' directory. The tool description warns that the profile starts empty and the local endpoint is unauthenticated.

*Sources: r1:R62, r2:R45, r1:BRW-4, r1:BRW-10, r1:BRW-11, r1:PAL-missed-1, r2:BRW-8, r2:BRW-13, r2:PAL-missed-debug-launch-unchecked*

### M5.2 CDP transport seam, persistent sessions and explicit errors

**Priority:** P1 · **Effort:** M · **Kind:** improve

CdpService builds HttpClient and ClientWebSocket inline and uses one-shot evaluates with a hard-coded id of 1, so the hang and matching bugs cannot be tested. Make the transport testable and every failure explicit.

**Design.** Inject an HttpMessageHandler and an ICdpConnectionFactory/ICdpTransport that produces ICdpConnection{SendAsync(method, params, ct), Events}.
